# Ranked opening routes and claim strategies

Current website copy, checked 15 October 2026. **64 roles/states, 149 opening routes, 161 opposing-role claim pairings, and 29 truthful Town-role explanations.**

The first route is the low-information beginner recommendation. Later routes are conditional alternatives, not weaker actions in every situation. The first two public claims stay visible; lower-ranked role icons reveal one comparison at a time. Role definitions live in inline dropdowns. All 29 Town claims remain selectable, while the researched shortlist ranks only choices with an explainable use case.

Mechanic facts come from the linked role pages and official patch notes; the ordering, target preferences, and false-claim strategy are editorial deductions. No measured win-rate claim or live-client experiment is implied. This guide assumes vanilla text chat and normal factions. Source audits: [Town](../research/ROUTES_TOWN_SOURCES.md), [Coven](../research/CLAIM_ROUTES_COVEN_SOURCES.md), [Neutral/Apocalypse](../research/CLAIM_ROUTES_NEUTRAL_SOURCES.md).

Official ability names remain in the copyable example wills. Route selections change the plan, checkpoints, and follow-up priorities; they do not perform an action or silently change the chosen public claim.


## Jailor

TPow — Town Power.

### Route 1: Question an unresolved prisoner (recommended)

**Best when:** Use the questioning route when nobody needs a specific defensive jail and an unresolved claim would give Town a useful D2 lead.

**Action:** Choose an unresolved prisoner during D1, then ask their exact role, intended action, and target reasoning on N1. You cannot execute tonight.

**Target rule:** Prefer a player whose D1 statements can be checked. With no information, choose an eligible unknown; avoid a credible key Town actor whose action you would suppress.

**Why:** A documented claim gives you a testable D2 lead; N1 is a questioning night, not a killing opportunity.

**Change plan when:** Do not jail a valuable Town claim merely to obtain a private chat. If you miss the day selection, record the missed jail instead of inventing a prisoner.

**Tradeoff:** Jailing an ally suppresses their night action, and a dangerous prisoner can retaliate; no execution is available on N1.

**Checkpoints:**

- Get one usable prisoner claim
- Compare it with independent D2 evidence
- Use execution on evidence, not to punish a nervous answer

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. From N2, execute a supported evil when execution is enabled; first compare their answer with independent evidence and protection possibilities.
2. Without a strong execution case, jail a different unresolved player and obtain a fresh, specific account; consecutive jails are restricted.
3. Before the next day ends, select the next prisoner and preserve the claim log; a wrong Town execution removes your remaining ability.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jailor_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Shelter a threatened low-action Townie

**Best when:** A credible Town player is publicly threatened, has no important N1 action, and ordinary direct attacks are the concern.

**Action:** Select that player during D1 and explain privately that this is a defensive jail. Still record their exact claim; you cannot execute on N1.

**Target rule:** Prefer a well-supported, threatened day-role claim over a protector or investigator who needs to act tonight. A loud TPow claim alone is insufficient trust.

**Why:** The prisoner loses their action, but ordinary direct visitors are obstructed. Sheltering a valuable day actor can outweigh obtaining a fresh unknown claim.

**Change plan when:** Return to questioning unresolved players when the immediate threat passes. Jail has astral and passive bypasses, and consecutive jails are restricted.

**Tradeoff:** The prisoner is not invulnerable, and you may protect an evil if the original Town read was weak.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify a concrete threat
- Check the lost night-action cost
- Log the prisoner’s exact claim

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. From N2, compare evidence before deciding on an execution.
2. Rotate to an unresolved eligible prisoner once shelter is no longer the priority.
3. Keep the protective explanation in the log so an uneventful night is not mistaken for proof of guilt.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jailor_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-bug-fixes-patch-notes-r1-5-01/).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Jailor role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Jailor (TPow) — Truthful role · recommended

A documented claim gives you a testable D2 lead; N1 is a questioning night, not a killing opportunity.

- Start with the legal opening: Choose an unresolved prisoner during D1, then ask their exact role, intended action, and target reasoning on N1. You cannot execute tonight.
- Choose the prisoner during the preceding day and record their exact claimed role and useful statements.
- N1 permits questioning but not execution. Do not jail the same person consecutively.
- Record released or executed explicitly and distinguish an attempted execution from its actual outcome.

**Best when:** You are Jailor in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not execute N1. Do not repeat the same prisoner on consecutive nights. Do not rely on jail to stop every astral effect.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jailor_(ToS2)).

## Marshal

TPow — Town Power.

### Route 1: Prepare cases and preserve Tribunal (recommended)

**Best when:** Keep the ordinary preparation route when you lack two supported cases or Town can resolve the immediate case through a normal trial.

**Action:** No N1 ability. Prepare a short claim-and-evidence list; preserve both Tribunal charges for a voting day.

**Target rule:** Prepare the best supported accusation and a separate second candidate. With no evidence, collect claims and hold; an empty shortlist is a reason to wait.

**Why:** Tribunal removes the defense and judgment stages, so activating before you have cases gives coordinated evils a powerful tool.

**Change plan when:** Use earlier if a supported evil must die before Town loses vote control, but do not activate simply to confirm your role or because D2 began.

**Tradeoff:** Holding preserves the charges but can miss a decisive voting window; preparation must continue during the day.

**Checkpoints:**

- Build one evidence-backed case
- Check whether extra trials help Town
- Spend the ability with an organized voting plan

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Before activation, name the intended candidates, state the evidence, and check whether Town can supply the needed votes.
2. Start Tribunal when faster executions or extra voting time materially help; count the available executions and keep the order explicit.
3. During Tribunal, direct votes to the agreed cases and stop improvising replacements from silence alone; dead roles and wills wait until the Tribunal ends.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Marshal_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Prepare an urgent D2 Tribunal

**Best when:** Vote control is about to disappear or ordinary trial time cannot resolve multiple independently supported evils.

**Action:** N1 still has no action. Use the night to draft the candidate order and evidence; on D2, activate only after checking support and the available execution count.

**Target rule:** Name the first case and a separate justified next case. Do not fill the second slot with the quietest player merely because another execution is available.

**Why:** A planned Tribunal can remove threats before they act again. Preparing an earlier activation trades secrecy for time and extra execution capacity.

**Change plan when:** If D2 evidence supports only one case and normal voting can complete it, return to the default hold. Do not demand a second execution just to use the mode.

**Tradeoff:** Evils can exploit rushed nominations; the first victim’s role and will are unavailable before the next decision.

**Checkpoints:**

- Write the candidate order
- Count likely supporting votes
- Check each case independently

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. State the evidence and order before activating.
2. Keep nominations focused while defense and judgment are skipped.
3. Review the delayed flips afterward before planning another Tribunal.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Marshal_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Marshal role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Marshal (TPow) — Truthful role · recommended

Tribunal removes the defense and judgment stages, so activating before you have cases gives coordinated evils a powerful tool.

- Start with the legal opening: No N1 ability. Prepare a short claim-and-evidence list; preserve both Tribunal charges for a voting day.
- Write No night ability instead of manufacturing nightly checks.
- Use a D entry for a real Tribunal and list the people actually executed.
- Do not label every Tribunal victim evil: execution is an event, not an alignment result.

**Best when:** You are Marshal in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not activate just to confirm yourself. Do not expect immediate feedback after the first victim. Do not use an outdated three-execution default.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Marshal_(ToS2)).

## Mayor

TPow — Town Power.

### Route 1: Stay hidden until votes matter (recommended)

**Best when:** Stay unrevealed while ordinary Town votes can resolve cases and revealing would mainly advertise an attractive kill.

**Action:** No N1 action. Record claims and identify the strongest D2 question; you cannot Reveal on D1.

**Target rule:** Watch contested claims and who supplies evidence rather than who is loudest. With no information, prepare ordinary claim questions and preserve Reveal.

**Why:** An unrevealed Mayor preserves an important vote swing without immediately advertising the exact role.

**Change plan when:** Reveal on a later voting day when it secures a supported execution, prevents your own mistaken execution, or establishes needed leadership; avoid waiting past a decisive vote.

**Tradeoff:** Waiting too long forfeits possible vote growth and can leave you unable to reveal once you are on trial.

**Checkpoints:**

- Keep a clear evidence list
- Reveal for a specific vote
- Reassess vote control after each death

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use ordinary discussion to identify a supported evil and count the votes before revealing.
2. Reveal when the extra vote power changes that outcome; state the intended vote and evidence immediately.
3. After revealing, coordinate the remaining votes and follow-up cases, and plan around the protection and communication restrictions shown by your role card.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Mayor_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Plan early D2 vote growth

**Best when:** Town needs a visible coordinator, extra votes can secure a supported case, and likely survival makes future vote growth useful.

**Action:** There is no N1 action and no D1 Reveal. Prepare a supported D2 voting plan, then Reveal before a trial begins if the benefit justifies exposure.

**Target rule:** Choose an evidence-based case and count the actual vote shortfall. A desire to look confirmed is not by itself a voting plan.

**Why:** Earlier Reveal starts the increasing vote count sooner. It can be worth the exposure when additional voting days are plausible or leadership is otherwise failing.

**Change plan when:** If a coordinated ordinary vote already solves the case and you expect to be killed after revealing, preserve the option until its votes change an outcome.

**Tradeoff:** The public role can attract day kills or role guessers; extra vote weight does not make your reads correct.

**Checkpoints:**

- Compare immediate votes with exposure
- Prepare one supported case
- Reveal before anyone reaches trial

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Announce the intended vote and its evidence after Reveal.
2. Recount your growing vote weight at each new day.
3. Reassess exposed-role survival and protection restrictions instead of assuming public identity grants safety.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Mayor_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Mayor role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Mayor (TPow) — Truthful role · recommended

An unrevealed Mayor preserves an important vote swing without immediately advertising the exact role.

- Start with the legal opening: No N1 action. Record claims and identify the strongest D2 question; you cannot Reveal on D1.
- Write No night ability for quiet nights; Mayor does not investigate or visit a target.
- Record Reveal on the actual day it happens, never D1 in the normal setup.
- Keep observational vote notes distinct from ability results.

**Best when:** You are Mayor in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not assume the ToS1 fixed-vote model. Do not wait until on trial to reveal. Do not lead votes without checking evidence.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Mayor_(ToS2)).

## Monarch

TPow — Town Power.

### Route 1: Hold Knight without a credible candidate (recommended)

**Best when:** Hold when D1 gives you no credible Town candidate or the candidate is unlikely to live to receive knighthood.

**Action:** Default to holding Knight with no credible Town read. Knight tonight only if a well-supported Town candidate is likely to survive.

**Target rule:** Prefer a strongly evidenced Town player with useful future votes, not merely a TPow claim. With no information, preserve the charge rather than pick a random knight.

**Why:** Knight grants permanent voting power to any faction, and its defense does not protect you immediately during the knighting night.

**Change plan when:** If a credible Town candidate exists and survival or impending vote pressure makes delay costly, knight now; do not preserve both charges until they can no longer affect voting.

**Tradeoff:** Holding avoids strengthening an enemy but leaves you without the Basic Defense a living Knight would later provide.

**Checkpoints:**

- Preserve charges without trust
- Empower an independently supported ally
- Track how Knights change the vote

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Knight the best-supported living Town candidate once evidence exists; check that they are likely to remain alive.
2. Recount vote control before using the second charge; strengthen a reliable voter rather than rewarding agreeable chat.
3. Track living knights and your resulting defense, and reassess any knight whose actions contradict Town interests; knighthood does not confirm faction.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Monarch_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Knight a credible Town candidate

**Best when:** A candidate has concrete Town evidence, should survive tonight, and the additional future vote has useful value.

**Action:** Knight that eligible player on N1 if the ability is enabled. Record the intended recipient and check for the public Knight announcement at dawn.

**Target rule:** Prefer evidence and likely survival over confident speech. You need a reliable future voter, not merely a player who agrees with you.

**Why:** Earlier knighthood can put an extra Town vote into play sooner and provide later defense while the Knight lives. This is the conditional exception to blind N1 holding.

**Change plan when:** If the candidate’s claim becomes doubtful or their death is likely, hold. The first knighting does not give defense early enough to save you that same night.

**Tradeoff:** A wrong Knight permanently strengthens an enemy, and you can still die during the initial knighting night.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify why the candidate is Town
- Check likely survival
- Confirm the actual knighting

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Recount Town and opposing vote weight before the next charge.
2. Choose another reliable voter or reinforce one only for an explicit vote-control reason.
3. Reassess the Knight’s behavior; a Knight icon is not faction confirmation.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Monarch_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Monarch role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Monarch (TPow) — Truthful role · recommended

Knight grants permanent voting power to any faction, and its defense does not protect you immediately during the knighting night.

- Start with the legal opening: Default to holding Knight with no credible Town read. Knight tonight only if a well-supported Town candidate is likely to survive.
- Record each Knight attempt, its target, and whether the effect actually completed.
- Do not mark a selection as completed before the night resolves.
- Track the two charges from real outcomes; failed or dead-target attempts need the actual feedback.

**Best when:** You are Monarch in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not knight an impressive speaker solely on confidence. Do not assume Knights are unvoteable. Do not assume a future Knight saves you tonight.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Monarch_(ToS2)).

## Prosecutor

TPow — Town Power.

### Route 1: Preserve prosecution for a supported case (recommended)

**Best when:** Preserve charges while discussion and ordinary voting can remove the supported suspect.

**Action:** No N1 action. Build one supported accusation and prepare the question that could disprove it; Prosecute is a later voting-phase ability.

**Target rule:** Prefer independently supported contradictions or a mechanically sound finding. With no information, gather a claim and wait; refusing an early role reveal is not sufficient evidence.

**Why:** One mistaken Town prosecution both removes an ally and costs the remaining charge, so impatience has unusually high cost.

**Change plan when:** Act earlier if a supported evil will otherwise survive a vote Town cannot win. Prefer ordinary voting when it achieves the same result without spending your charge.

**Tradeoff:** An unused prosecution may become worthless after Town loses control, but a wrong Town prosecution also costs the remaining charge.

**Checkpoints:**

- Obtain the target’s answer
- Establish a supported case
- Prosecute for a concrete Town benefit

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Check the case and the target’s defense in chat before forcing an execution; distinguish real evidence from pressure.
2. Prosecute during a legal voting phase when bypassing ordinary voting materially helps; it is unavailable during Tribunal.
3. After the reveal, preserve the remaining charge for another supported case and remember that prosecuting a Jester makes you their revenge candidate.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Prosecutor_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Prepare a necessary D2 prosecution

**Best when:** Independent evidence supports an evil who is likely to escape ordinary voting or kill again before Town can act.

**Action:** No N1 ability exists. Assemble the evidence and alternative explanations tonight; during a later legal voting phase, Prosecute only if ordinary voting cannot solve the problem safely.

**Target rule:** Use a concrete contradictory action or supported finding, not refusal to full-claim or a nervous response. Check whether the suspected goal is a Jester hanging.

**Why:** The ability bypasses a stalled vote and normal judgment. Its best early use solves a specific failure in the ordinary process rather than merely speeding up a weak read.

**Change plan when:** If Town gains enough votes for a normal trial, use that process and retain the charge. Prosecution is unavailable during Tribunal.

**Tradeoff:** You reveal yourself, bypass the target’s defense, and become the sole revenge candidate if you prosecute a winning Jester.

**Checkpoints:**

- Write the decisive evidence
- Check Jester and framing explanations
- Explain why ordinary voting fails

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use at most the permitted prosecution that day and review the actual flip.
2. Preserve the second charge after a justified evil removal.
3. After a Town mistake, stop planning further prosecutions and contribute through truthful voting.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Prosecutor_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Prosecutor role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Prosecutor (TPow) — Truthful role · recommended

One mistaken Town prosecution both removes an ally and costs the remaining charge, so impatience has unusually high cost.

- Start with the legal opening: No N1 action. Build one supported accusation and prepare the question that could disprove it; Prosecute is a later voting-phase ability.
- Write No night ability instead of an investigative or protective target.
- Use D entries for prosecutions, with the actual target and outcome.
- Track two total charges and at most one use per day; obey the live phase restrictions.

**Best when:** You are Prosecutor in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not prosecute merely to prove your role. Do not expect to use it during Tribunal. Do not use outdated Hangman-suicide advice.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Prosecutor_(ToS2)).

## Coroner

TI — Town Investigative.

### Route 1: Prepare the first useful Autopsy (recommended)

**Best when:** Prioritize an active enemy killer’s victim after the first useful death; N1 has no normal usable autopsy.

**Action:** In a normal fresh match, N1 has no eligible death record to work from. Prepare to Autopsy an overnight victim on D2, then Examine a suspect on N2.

**Target rule:** For the first Autopsy, prefer a victim of a killer still threatening Town. With no useful clue, choose a normal Coven or active killing-role victim rather than a departed Jester/Doomsayer victim.

**Why:** Coroner needs a recorded killer before its night check is useful; inventing an N1 result creates an impossible history.

**Change plan when:** If no suitable body exists, hold and state why. Unusual setup, revived bodies, and disputed counterattack interactions need their own source/feedback check.

**Tradeoff:** A record of an already departed killer adds little, and a negative Examine does not clear someone of all evil roles.

**Checkpoints:**

- Create an eligible autopsy record
- Examine a relevant suspect
- Explain exactly which killer records a match concerns

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Autopsy a useful new victim during each day to expand the retained collection of recorded killers.
2. Examine a living suspect who could be one of those killers, prioritizing a player with a conflicting action or kill opportunity.
3. Report the exact killer match and role when received; on a negative result, say only that the player did not match your recorded killers.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Coroner_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Identify a disputed Town attacker

**Best when:** A real night kill is attributed to a Town killing ability and identifying its actor would resolve an important competing claim; no more urgent enemy-killer record is available.

**Action:** N1 still has no meaningful autopsy-based check. On D2, Autopsy the relevant victim and use a later Examine on a plausible surviving claimant.

**Target rule:** Choose the body tied to the disputed night attack. Avoid visiting an alleged Veteran on a likely Alert and do not equate an attacking role with Town allegiance.

**Why:** A killer record can answer who performed an important action, not only hunt Coven. Resolving a genuine identity dispute may give Town better evidence than an irrelevant departed-neutral record.

**Change plan when:** Return to active enemy killers when their records become available. Treat control, Shroud, role changes, and marked untested interactions as context rather than universal guilt rules.

**Tradeoff:** The source marks some counterattack interactions untested, and revealing a real Town killer may help an enemy Witch or role guesser.

**Checkpoints:**

- Record the correct victim
- Name the identity question
- Separate role identity from allegiance

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Accumulate later useful autopsies instead of erasing earlier records.
2. Examine the best-supported candidate for the remaining identity question.
3. Share the exact match scope and keep dangerous Town-role exposure proportionate.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Coroner_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Coroner role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Coroner (TI) — Truthful role · recommended

Coroner needs a recorded killer before its night check is useful; inventing an N1 result creates an impossible history.

- Start with the legal opening: In a normal fresh match, N1 has no eligible death record to work from. Prepare to Autopsy an overnight victim on D2, then Examine a suspect on N2.
- Write D2 autopsy separately from N2 examination; killer-role feedback arrives at night’s start.
- Retain all previous autopsies because later examinations use the accumulated collection.
- Use Match or No match within that collection; No match is not confirmed Town.

**Best when:** You are Coroner in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not claim a meaningful N1 examination without records. Do not treat Shrouded Town killers as necessarily evil. Do not assume changing bookholders erases old evidence.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Coroner_(ToS2)).

## Investigator

TI — Town Investigative.

### Route 1: Investigate an unresolved visitor (recommended)

**Best when:** Investigate an unresolved claimed visitor when its actual history can be compared with the crime result.

**Action:** Investigate one unresolved player on N1, preferably someone whose claimed action you can question on D2.

**Target rule:** Prefer an unconfirmed claimed visitor or a player with a checkable D1 statement. With no information, choose an eligible unknown away from obvious Veteran bait.

**Why:** Crimes create lines of questioning: Trespassing concerns faction-crossing visits, while Murder concerns attacking that night. Neither is automatically evil.

**Change plan when:** If the only candidate is a credible key Town player with little uncertainty, investigate another unknown. Do not convert framing-sensitive results into an automatic execution.

**Tradeoff:** Murder or Trespassing can have Town explanations, while No Crime is not a Town clearance.

**Checkpoints:**

- Preserve literal crime feedback
- Ask for the corresponding action history
- Compare with independent observations

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Post the literal result with night and target, then ask a Trespassing target for their visit history or a Murder target for their action.
2. Investigate a fresh unresolved player whose current activity could explain a kill or contradiction; historical Murder is not checked on later nights.
3. Compare crimes with movement evidence, deaths, and known framing roles; preserve No Crime as a narrow result rather than a Town clear.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Investigator_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Test a disputed no-visit story

**Best when:** Someone has made a specific no-night-action or astral-only claim that conflicts with other evidence, rather than merely being quiet.

**Action:** Investigate that eligible claimant on N1 and preserve the exact crime feedback. Ask for the claimed action history before treating any result as an accusation.

**Target rule:** Prefer the disputed claimant only when the contradiction matters more than checking an ordinary unknown. Do not approach obvious Veteran bait for this experiment.

**Why:** A crime result can create a useful question about a claimed history. The point is to compare the story with evidence, not to prove faction from a single label.

**Change plan when:** If the account and independent evidence explain the result, investigate a fresh unknown next. Framing and forced attacks can invalidate simple crime-to-guilt shortcuts.

**Tradeoff:** No Crime can fit many enemies, and a frame or Shroud may produce a crime on an honest Town role.

**Checkpoints:**

- Record the disputed statement
- Copy the exact crime result
- Ask which action explains it

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Compare tonight’s Murder separately from accumulated Trespassing.
2. Use movement and attack reports to test the answer.
3. Post observations and remaining explanations instead of an automatic guilty verdict.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Investigator_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem2.com/news/nn2khtionvzvslwh2lcs8nmh64t8hr).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Investigator role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Investigator (TI) — Truthful role · recommended

Crimes create lines of questioning: Trespassing concerns faction-crossing visits, while Murder concerns attacking that night. Neither is automatically evil.

- Start with the legal opening: Investigate one unresolved player on N1, preferably someone whose claimed action you can question on D2.
- Use Murder, Trespassing, or No Crime as actually reported; preserve combined or unusual feedback verbatim.
- Murder concerns an attack that night, not an old kill or a generic evil read.
- Trespassing concerns opposing-faction visiting history, so Town can show it too; do not invent it for an action that never directly visits.

**Best when:** You are Investigator in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not use old blood-only templates. Do not invent crime for a nonvisiting action. Do not treat No Crime as a faction clearance.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Investigator_(ToS2)).

## Lookout

TI — Town Investigative.

### Route 1: Watch a credible useful Town house (recommended)

**Best when:** Watch a credible useful exposed Town target when the destination is likely to generate interpretable and reasonably safe visitor traffic.

**Action:** Watch a credible exposed Town claim or sensible protection-call target on N1; your goal is a visitor list that can be checked tomorrow.

**Target rule:** Prefer a credible vulnerable Town leader or information role. With no useful call or role evidence, watch an active eligible unknown; avoid obvious Veteran bait and warn yourself about crowded-target risk.

**Why:** Watching likely traffic makes your observation more actionable than choosing an isolated random house.

**Change plan when:** Skip a reckless call if Veteran, Crusader, or a known rampaging threat makes the location unusually dangerous; choose a less crowded useful target.

**Tradeoff:** A crowded destination may kill you through visitor danger or rampage, and a visitor list omits astral visits.

**Checkpoints:**

- Get one correctly directed visitor list
- Ask what those visitors did
- Resolve a specific effect or contradiction

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Compare the visitor list with claimed destinations and effects at that house; a contradiction is more valuable than merely finding a visitor.
2. Watch the credible Town target most likely to receive a kill or disruption next, while adapting to known rampage danger.
3. Publish useful contradictions promptly and preserve the complete list; no visible visitor does not exclude astral actions or prove that nothing happened.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Lookout_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Watch a secondary valuable house

**Best when:** The main protection call is crowded, threatened by a rampage, or already covered by a credible Lookout.

**Action:** Watch a different credible useful player on N1 and keep the exact visible visitor list for D2 comparisons.

**Target rule:** Prefer a secondary investigator, protector, or day actor who may attract an enemy but fewer known friendly visitors. Avoid a Crusader or Veteran hazard.

**Why:** Spreading observation can catch an enemy avoiding the obvious call. It also reduces dependence on one dangerous gathering point.

**Change plan when:** Return to the primary asset if its risk changes and your observation is now needed there. With no useful alternative, watch a safe eligible unknown without pretending the choice proves anything.

**Tradeoff:** The quieter house may receive no visitors, so this sacrifices likely traffic for safety and wider coverage.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify why the main house is risky
- Pick one useful secondary target
- Preserve all visible visitors

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Compare visitors with independently stated destinations.
2. Coordinate with genuine protection or Spy information when it answers a concrete conflict.
3. Move away from houses where rampage risk outweighs likely information.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Lookout_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Hear destinations before revealing the list

**Best when:** You already have a valid Watch result and a short delay would test a specific visit claim without endangering an immediate Town decision.

**Action:** Use a legal N1 Watch normally. At dawn, claim your information role when needed, ask the relevant players for destinations, then reveal the unchanged result promptly.

**Target rule:** Aim the question at someone whose claimed route can be compared with your observed house. Keep the timestamped list in your will before asking.

**Why:** A player who supplies an independent incompatible destination gives a clearer conflict than someone who simply echoes your published list.

**Change plan when:** Post immediately if an urgent accusation or vote needs the result. Do not stay silent all day, hide decisive information, or fabricate a different alignment.

**Tradeoff:** A long delay can look evasive and withhold useful evidence; this is a short evidence check, not a reason to suppress your will.

**Checkpoints:**

- Save the result before questioning
- Ask a specific route question
- Reveal in time to inform voting

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. State exactly who was visible at the watched house.
2. Consider astral visits and disruption before calling a missing visitor impossible.
3. Use later Watch targets to resolve the remaining conflict.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Lookout_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Lookout role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Lookout (TI) — Truthful role · recommended

Watching likely traffic makes your observation more actionable than choosing an isolated random house.

- Start with the legal opening: Watch a credible exposed Town claim or sensible protection-call target on N1; your goal is a visitor list that can be checked tomorrow.
- Write the watched house first, then who visited that house; this is the opposite direction from Tracker.
- Use all visible visitors the game reports. No visible visitors is a real result; roleblocked is no result.
- Do not normally watch yourself or claim that absence proves no astral action occurred.

**Best when:** You are Lookout in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not swap visitors with destinations. Do not claim you watched yourself normally. Do not expose protective identities unnecessarily.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Lookout_(ToS2)).

## Psychic

TI — Town Investigative.

### Route 1: Use Good vision on an evil read (recommended)

**Best when:** Use the N1 Good vision on a plausible evil candidate when you have one; otherwise use an eligible unknown and retain the uncertainty.

**Action:** Choose a suspicious but eligible unresolved player for the N1 Good vision; with no read, choose an unknown you can follow up on D2.

**Target rule:** For odd-night Good visions, prefer a credible evil candidate; for even-night Evil visions, prefer a strongly supported Town anchor. With no information, choose an eligible unknown and avoid forced certainty.

**Why:** If that chosen player is later shown evil, the other name becomes a stronger Town lead, subject to result-altering effects.

**Change plan when:** Do not choose an obvious visiting hazard merely to optimize the pool. When no trusted anchor exists on even nights, use an unresolved player and describe the wider uncertainty.

**Tradeoff:** A Good pair does not identify a single guaranteed Town player unless additional assumptions and evidence support the narrowing.

**Checkpoints:**

- Publish a complete labeled vision
- Gather independent evidence about its members
- Use the next vision without inventing a single culprit

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Post the exact Good pair or Evil trio early each day, including night and chosen anchor, so Town can compare it with other evidence.
2. On even nights, include an independently supported Town anchor to focus scrutiny on the other two names; seek claims, not an automatic execution.
3. On odd nights, include a supported evil candidate where safe; revisit earlier pools when deaths or reliable findings establish identities.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Use a safe unresolved vision target

**Best when:** The apparent suspicious candidate is an obvious visiting hazard, already resolved, or based only on weak bait.

**Action:** Select a safer eligible unknown for the N1 Good vision. Record the chosen target and both names exactly, and post the genuine vision near D2 start.

**Target rule:** Choose someone whose later claim you can check, away from credible Veteran or dangerous-house warnings. Do not use a known Town anchor expecting an informative Good pair.

**Why:** Surviving with a valid vision and a checkable target is more useful than risking the role for a tiny theoretical improvement from an unreliable evil read.

**Change plan when:** On even-night Evil visions, prefer a credible legal Town anchor to narrow the remaining group. Never rewrite old names after a flip.

**Tradeoff:** A neutral unknown is a weaker anchor, and framing or illusion can undermine simple faction inferences.

**Checkpoints:**

- Include the actual chosen target
- Record N1 Good as two names
- Keep the pair conditional

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use later alignments and deaths to interpret prior visions.
2. Switch target logic for even-night Evil groups of three.
3. Explain disruption or a missing vision without inventing replacement names.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Psychic role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Psychic (TI) — Truthful role · recommended

If that chosen player is later shown evil, the other name becomes a stronger Town lead, subject to result-altering effects.

- Start with the legal opening: Choose a suspicious but eligible unresolved player for the N1 Good vision; with no read, choose an unknown you can follow up on D2.
- Odd nights: Good vision with two names. Even nights: Evil vision with three names. Follow special client feedback in unusual late games.
- Mark the player you selected and include them in that night’s vision.
- A Good pair has at least one apparent good player; an Evil trio has at least one apparent evil player. Neither means exactly one.

**Best when:** You are Psychic in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not reverse night parity. Do not omit the selected target. Do not silently turn a group lead into a confirmed accusation.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

## Seer

TI — Town Investigative.

### Route 1: Compare a Town read with an unknown (recommended)

**Best when:** Compare a credible legal Town read with an unknown when that anchor is supported and has not already been compared.

**Action:** Compare two eligible players on N1: one you read more strongly as Town and one unresolved player. If nobody has earned trust, compare two unknowns.

**Target rule:** Prefer players with distinct, checkable claims and avoid obvious visiting hazards. With no information, select two eligible unknowns, record both, and treat the pair as unresolved.

**Why:** A reliable anchor makes a pair easier to interpret; two unknowns still supply a relationship Town can resolve later.

**Change plan when:** Do not choose a revealed or previously compared player when the client disallows it. A merely confident claim is not a confirmed anchor.

**Tradeoff:** A false anchor can invert the conclusion, and revealed or previously compared players may be illegal targets.

**Checkpoints:**

- Keep the complete pair history
- Collect both claims
- Resolve a pair with independent evidence

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Post both targets and the exact Friends/Enemies result early, and ask for claims from a useful unresolved pair.
2. Use fresh eligible pairs on later nights, choosing your strongest available independent Town read as the anchor where possible.
3. Revisit earlier pairs after reliable role/faction evidence arrives; Friends does not prove Town and Enemies does not identify which player is evil.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Seer_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Compare two competing unresolved claims

**Best when:** Neither claimant deserves to be treated as Town, and the relationship between their claims would help the next discussion.

**Action:** Compare the two eligible players on N1, record both names and the exact Friends or Enemies result, and obtain their separate histories on D2.

**Target rule:** Use two distinct unresolved players with checkable claims. Both must be legal unused targets; avoid known visiting hazards.

**Why:** This creates a relationship test without pretending that a confident speaker is a confirmed anchor. It is useful when neither participant has earned that trust.

**Change plan when:** If one later gains independent Town evidence, revisit what the old comparison implies. You normally cannot compare either player again, so build the next pair from legal unused players.

**Tradeoff:** Friends can mean two enemies share a faction, and Enemies does not tell you which individual is evil; framing and illusion remain possible.

**Checkpoints:**

- Check both players are eligible
- Treat both as unresolved
- Keep the exact pair result

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Ask both players to explain their roles independently.
2. Use deaths and genuine evidence to interpret the relation.
3. Build later comparisons without recycling used or publicly revealed targets.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Seer_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Seer role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Seer (TI) — Truthful role · recommended

A reliable anchor makes a pair easier to interpret; two unknowns still supply a relationship Town can resolve later.

- Start with the legal opening: Compare two eligible players on N1: one you read more strongly as Town and one unresolved player. If nobody has earned trust, compare two unknowns.
- Use two distinct eligible players and retain a used-target history; follow the client’s reuse and revealed-target restrictions.
- Show Friends or Enemies exactly. A failed pair is not a usable comparison.
- Do not turn Enemies into both evil or Friends into confirmed Town.

**Best when:** You are Seer in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not reuse targets as though checks were unlimited. Do not claim a pair when blocked. Do not call all two-visit players Seer.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Seer_(ToS2)).

## Sheriff

TI — Town Investigative.

### Route 1: Search an unresolved claim (recommended)

**Best when:** Search an unresolved player whose claim can be questioned next day rather than repeatedly checking a low-uncertainty Town read.

**Action:** Search one unresolved player on N1, preferably someone whose claim or behavior you can question tomorrow.

**Target rule:** Prefer an unconfirmed player outside obvious Veteran bait. With no information, choose an eligible unknown; quiet chat or a particular player number is not evidence.

**Why:** An early check produces useful pressure, but Innocent misses several evil categories and Suspicious can be framed.

**Change plan when:** Switch away from a credible low-uncertainty Town claim unless a specific contradiction needs checking. Repeating an Innocent player without new cause usually adds little.

**Tradeoff:** Innocent includes several enemy types; Suspicious can also result from framing.

**Checkpoints:**

- Publish one honest check
- Ask a specific follow-up question
- Combine results with claims instead of treating detection as certainty

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Post exact night, target, and Innocent/Suspicious result early; if interrupted, post the interruption instead.
2. Use a Suspicious result to obtain a claim and compare independent evidence, accounting for Enchant and Reap.
3. Search fresh unresolved players as the roster narrows; combine Innocent results with movement, claims, and vote behavior instead of treating them as confirmations.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Search beyond the obvious killer read

**Best when:** The loudest suspect is specifically a likely defended Neutral Killing role, or another credible investigator is already resolving that exact case.

**Action:** Search a different eligible unconfirmed player on N1, preferably a weakly supported Town claim that could conceal non-book Coven. Record the exact result for D2.

**Target rule:** Pick a checkable unknown without known visiting danger. Do not choose by player number or assume a quiet protector claim is automatically Coven.

**Why:** Sheriff is poorly suited to clearing or detecting many Neutral Killing suspects. Using the check on another live possibility can add information while discussion handles the first case.

**Change plan when:** If your new target is Suspicious, obtain their account and check framing explanations before pushing. An Innocent result should widen the evidence base, not close the case.

**Tradeoff:** This diversifies information; it does not guarantee a detectable Coven target or excuse ignoring a well-supported original suspect.

**Checkpoints:**

- Know what Sheriff can detect
- Choose a different live uncertainty
- Post the exact result

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Spread searches across unresolved candidates unless a concrete new reason supports a repeat.
2. Compare Suspicious results with claims and possible frames.
3. Keep behavior and independent evidence active even after Innocent checks.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Sheriff role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Sheriff (TI) — Truthful role · recommended

An early check produces useful pressure, but Innocent misses several evil categories and Suspicious can be framed.

- Start with the legal opening: Search one unresolved player on N1, preferably someone whose claim or behavior you can question tomorrow.
- Use the literal Innocent or Suspicious feedback, keeping failed checks separate.
- Choose an unresolved target and commit to that number before hearing everyone’s morning reports.
- Record roleblock or another interruption instead of converting no result into Innocent.

**Best when:** You are Sheriff in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not call an innocent result a confirmed ally. Do not label every suspicious result Coven. Do not hide results until pressured without a reason.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

## Spy

TI — Town Investigative.

### Route 1: Bug a useful likely enemy target (recommended)

**Best when:** Bug a credible useful player likely to receive enemy activity and survive long enough for the persistent Bug to matter.

**Action:** Bug a credible exposed Town or likely attack/disruption target on N1.

**Target rule:** Prefer a player likely to draw evil activity and survive it. With no role information, choose an active eligible unknown away from obvious visiting hazards.

**Why:** Bugs persist while the recipient lives, so early placement on a useful likely survivor can pay across several nights.

**Change plan when:** A likely immediate death may erase future bug value; choose a secondary valuable target if the obvious one appears doomed or dangerous to visit.

**Tradeoff:** A quiet Bug gives no Town clearance, and a quickly killed target loses the continuing coverage.

**Checkpoints:**

- Track placements across nights
- Preserve trigger scope
- Connect one actual effect to an independently checkable claim

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Plant on a fresh useful living player when possible rather than wasting placement on an already bugged survivor.
2. Report actual triggered role feedback with its house and night; combine it with Lookout or claims to investigate visitor identity.
3. Review all surviving bug locations each dawn and distinguish an evil role trigger from proof of a specific player number.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Spy_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Bug a durable secondary target

**Best when:** The most exposed Town actor seems likely to die immediately, already has useful Bug coverage, or attracts dangerous visitor traffic.

**Action:** Plant the N1 Bug on a different credible useful player with a plausible chance of surviving multiple nights.

**Target rule:** Choose a secondary investigator or day actor who enemies may later disrupt. Avoid visiting an obvious Veteran or other dangerous house.

**Why:** Because the Bug persists until that player dies, spreading early coverage onto a surviving useful target can create more future opportunities than duplicating an obvious doomed destination.

**Change plan when:** Add another worthwhile target on later nights rather than repeatedly replanting the same still-active Bug. Return to the main asset if a new Bug actually fills a gap.

**Tradeoff:** A less exposed target may never be visited by an enemy; the route trades immediate activity for longer coverage.

**Checkpoints:**

- Distinguish survival from immediate traffic
- Record where the Bug was placed
- Track which Bug remains active

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Report the evil-role feedback actually triggered.
2. Compare it with Watch and destination claims without inventing visitor names.
3. Replace dead-target coverage with a living useful destination.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Spy_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem2.com/news/nn2khtionvzvslwh2lcs8nmh64t8hr).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Spy role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Spy (TI) — Truthful role · recommended

Bugs persist while the recipient lives, so early placement on a useful likely survivor can pay across several nights.

- Start with the legal opening: Bug a credible exposed Town or likely attack/disruption target on N1.
- Record each new Bug target and keep earlier Bugs in the active history.
- Write the role or effect the feedback reports, not a visitor player number you were never given.
- Label the trigger night explicitly, even when the Bug was placed several nights earlier.

**Best when:** You are Spy in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not use ToS1 global visit logs. Do not use the obsolete catch-all event-bug template. Do not forget that prior bugs remain active.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Spy_(ToS2)).

## Tracker

TI — Town Investigative.

### Route 1: Track a checkable unresolved visitor (recommended)

**Best when:** Track an unresolved claimed visitor whose destination could be compared with their independent D2 account.

**Action:** Track an unresolved player on N1; prefer a claimed visitor whose route could be compared with their D2 account.

**Target rule:** Choose an unconfirmed visitor or checkable D1 claim. With no information, track an eligible unknown while avoiding obvious Veteran bait.

**Why:** Movement contradictions are your strongest practical contribution; merely seeing a visit does not establish faction.

**Change plan when:** A known astral/nonvisiting role usually offers less movement information unless you are testing a specific contradiction. Do not expose yourself to an explicit hazard for a weak lead.

**Tradeoff:** A matching destination proves movement, not allegiance or ability; astral actions can appear as no direct visit.

**Checkpoints:**

- Get the direction right
- Compare movement with the exact claimed action
- Keep consistent separate from confirmed

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Record exact visible destinations, then compare them with the player’s action claim and any effects at those destinations.
2. Track an unresolved player whose route can explain a current death, disruption, or incompatible claim.
3. Use repeated consistent routes as corroboration while checking setup exceptions; report no visible visit without calling it confirmed inactivity.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tracker_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Test a disputed stay-home claim

**Best when:** A player specifically claims a nonvisiting or astral-only role, and another observation gives you a concrete reason to doubt that account.

**Action:** Track that eligible player on N1 and record the actual direct destination or no-visit result. Compare it with their separately stated action on D2.

**Target rule:** Use a checkable contradiction, not mere suspicion of a quiet player. Avoid targets whose credible Veteran claim makes your visit dangerous.

**Why:** Observed movement can challenge a stay-home account more directly than a faction check. The value is a specific movement conflict, not a promise to identify the player’s role.

**Change plan when:** If the no-visit story remains compatible, track a fresh unresolved visitor next. Control, role changes, or an explained different action can change the interpretation.

**Tradeoff:** No visit is compatible with many Town and enemy roles, so an empty result cannot validate the claim.

**Checkpoints:**

- Preserve the original stay-home claim
- Copy actual movement only
- Ask before supplying the answer

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. State the direction clearly: your tracked player visited the destination.
2. Use later claims to distinguish a real action from a compatible route.
3. Move to another unresolved visitor once this timeline is resolved.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tracker_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Tracker role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Tracker (TI) — Truthful role · recommended

Movement contradictions are your strongest practical contribution; merely seeing a visit does not establish faction.

- Start with the legal opening: Track an unresolved player on N1; prefer a claimed visitor whose route could be compared with their D2 account.
- Write tracked player -> their destinations, not the list of people who visited them.
- Include multiple visible destinations when actually shown; corpse destinations remain valid references.
- Use No visible visit only after a successful observation. Use failed or roleblocked for missing information.

**Best when:** You are Tracker in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not equate no visit with no action. Do not omit extra destinations. Do not assume a two-visit result proves Town.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tracker_(ToS2)).

## Bodyguard

TP — Town Protective.

### Route 1: Guard a credible vulnerable Town actor (recommended)

**Best when:** Guard a credible useful likely attack target when preserving them is more valuable than spending the once-only self option.

**Action:** Guard a credible exposed Town Power or useful information claim on N1; save Self Protect unless you expect a direct attack on yourself.

**Target rule:** Prefer a supported Town player likely to be attacked. With no trusted claim, guard a sensible eligible unknown away from obvious Veteran bait rather than treating a loud protection call as confirmed Town.

**Why:** Your value is interrupting a harmful visitor and preserving a useful ally; a self-vest spends a night protecting no one else.

**Change plan when:** Self Protect when you are the likely Basic-attack target and no better guard assignment exists. Avoid a known dangerous visiting location; Guard does not stop every astral or passive threat.

**Tradeoff:** Guard can sacrifice you, stops one harmful direct visitor, and does not provide general defense against every kind of attack.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose a reasoned protection target
- Distinguish an attempt from an interception
- Reassess the actual attack type

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Guard the best-supported vulnerable Town player who is likely to draw a direct harmful visit.
2. Coordinate only with a justified trusted protector if mutual coverage materially improves survival; do not assume a whispered claim is confirmed.
3. Reassess as attack types become known and preserve an accurate guard/vest history; a quiet night does not mean you saved or cleared the target.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Self Protect under a direct threat

**Best when:** You have a credible reason to expect a Basic Attack on yourself and no more valuable Guard assignment; the client enables the ability.

**Action:** Use Self Protect on N1 instead of Guard, if legal. Record the spent self charge and actual attack feedback.

**Target rule:** Your target is yourself. A random fear of dying is weaker justification than an exposed role, credible threat, or deliberate enemy attention.

**Why:** Keeping a useful protector alive can be worthwhile when you are the likely victim. Self Protect is a survival choice and does not counterattack the attacker.

**Change plan when:** Return to Guard once the personal threat passes. Do not spend the option during an attack-disabled Grace Period merely to generate an entry.

**Tradeoff:** You leave another player unguarded, consume a limited resource, and receive no guaranteed attacker identity.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify the personal threat
- Check the self charge is available
- Record the true feedback

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Guard a credible vulnerable Town asset on following nights.
2. Plan around one harmful direct visit rather than all attack types.
3. Preserve the distinction between a self defense and a successful Guard interception.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-bug-fixes-patch-notes-r1-5-01/).

### Route 3: Guard the less-covered useful player

**Best when:** The main Town asset already has credible compatible protection, while another supported Town actor is exposed.

**Action:** Guard the secondary useful target on N1 when protection is enabled, after checking that the assignment does not create a known visitor hazard.

**Target rule:** Prefer the uncovered actor with a plausible direct-attack threat, not a random swap solely to avoid the popular call.

**Why:** Spreading protection can prevent an enemy from simply choosing the uncovered valuable house. Bodyguard works differently from a Barrier, so coordination should account for the actual protection already present.

**Change plan when:** If the primary target has no real coverage or a more urgent threat, return there. Keep assignments private unless sharing resolves a concrete collision.

**Tradeoff:** The main target may still be attacked, and claimed protection may be false; avoid relying on an unverified coverage promise.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify the coverage gap
- Avoid a hazardous destination
- Write the actual Guard target

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Reassess exposure after new roles reveal.
2. Treat counterattack and survival feedback separately.
3. Do not infer a full attacker list from one interception.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-bug-fixes-patch-notes-r1-5-01/).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Bodyguard role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Bodyguard (TP) — Truthful role · recommended

Your value is interrupting a harmful visitor and preserving a useful ally; a self-vest spends a night protecting no one else.

- Start with the legal opening: Guard a credible exposed Town Power or useful information claim on N1; save Self Protect unless you expect a direct attack on yourself.
- Write Guard and the selected player even when nothing was reported; choosing a target is not a save.
- Use Self Protect at most twice and remember it supplies Basic Defense rather than a normal guard on another player.
- Keep the destination aligned with any actual observed movement.

**Best when:** You are Bodyguard in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not say you grant a normal barrier. Do not expect to stop all attacks. Do not assume dying to protect someone proves their faction.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

## Cleric

TP — Town Protective.

### Route 1: Barrier a credible vulnerable Town actor (recommended)

**Best when:** Barrier a credible vulnerable Town actor when ordinary protection is enabled; otherwise hold and preserve the self option.

**Action:** If Barrier is enabled, protect a credible vulnerable Town claim on N1. If Grace Period disables other-player Barrier, hold and preserve Self Barrier.

**Target rule:** Prefer a supported exposed Town Power, then a useful Town Investigative. With no credible claim, choose an eligible unknown away from Veteran bait; a random target is not a confirmed ally.

**Why:** Repeatable protection on a valuable ally usually adds more team value than using your one self-protection without an attack reason.

**Change plan when:** Use Self Barrier when you are a plausible attack target and preserving yourself outweighs another assignment. Change course for known rampage or Unstoppable threats that Barrier cannot safely solve.

**Tradeoff:** A quiet target list offers no confirmation, and Barrier cannot stop Unstoppable attacks.

**Checkpoints:**

- Make one legal Barrier choice
- Record actual outcomes honestly
- Reassess who needs protection after D2

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Protect the most valuable credible Town player likely to be attacked; update that choice after new claims and deaths.
2. Protect a threatened fellow protector when keeping the protection network alive is more valuable than stacking the obvious target.
3. Spend Self Barrier on a credible survival need and record only actual attacked/saved feedback, not an assumed successful heal.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Self Barrier under a credible threat

**Best when:** You are the plausible attack target, the once-only option is available, and keeping you alive outweighs the other protection assignment.

**Action:** Use Self Barrier instead of a player-targeted Barrier if the client enables it. Record that the limited self use is spent; only report a save if feedback supports one.

**Target rule:** Choose yourself for a concrete survival reason. Avoid spending the charge on a quiet Grace Period night with no attack risk.

**Why:** Preserving a useful healer can outweigh one night of external coverage when enemies are likely to aim at you. It is a targeted response to exposure rather than an automatic first-night ritual.

**Change plan when:** Resume protecting other credible Town players when the immediate threat passes. Known Unstoppable threats require a different survival plan.

**Tradeoff:** You consume your only self use and leave another house without your Barrier.

**Checkpoints:**

- Explain why you may be attacked
- Check the limited self option
- Keep save claims tied to feedback

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Protect the most credible vulnerable Town actor next.
2. Keep intended target and actual protection feedback separate.
3. Update assignments when threats and credible claims change.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-bug-fixes-patch-notes-r1-5-01/).

### Route 3: Barrier the uncovered useful claim

**Best when:** The obvious leader already has credible coverage and another useful supported role is exposed or more likely to be attacked.

**Action:** Barrier the less-covered useful player on N1 if the action is enabled. Keep the destination fixed in your notes before seeing the morning deaths.

**Target rule:** Prefer a genuine coverage gap over simply following the loudest TP/LO call. Avoid direct visits into credible Veteran or other dangerous-house warnings.

**Why:** Distributed coverage makes the secondary useful role harder to pick off. This matters when stacking onto one house adds less value than preserving another actor.

**Change plan when:** If the first asset’s protection was only a weak claim or their threat becomes urgent, reassess instead of blindly honoring the split.

**Tradeoff:** The original protection claim may be false, and spreading does not guarantee either house survives.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify the uncovered asset
- Check the visit is reasonably safe
- Record the planned destination

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Change targets with an explicit threat-based reason.
2. Use actual attack feedback to report genuine protection.
3. Check a death’s attack type and interruption before deciding what failed.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-bug-fixes-patch-notes-r1-5-01/).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Cleric role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Truthful role · recommended

Repeatable protection on a valuable ally usually adds more team value than using your one self-protection without an attack reason.

- Start with the legal opening: If Barrier is enabled, protect a credible vulnerable Town claim on N1. If Grace Period disables other-player Barrier, hold and preserve Self Barrier.
- Choose a plausible valuable recipient before night resolves; keep the number consistent with your actual observed visit.
- Write Barrier 4, not saved 4, unless the actual feedback supports a save.
- Use Self Barrier at most once. Do not promise a private protection plan to everyone.

**Best when:** You are Cleric in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not declare someone confirmed merely because you protected them. Do not walk onto an alerting Veteran casually. Do not promise protection through all threats.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

## Crusader

TP — Town Protective.

### Route 1: Fortify a useful low-traffic house (recommended)

**Best when:** Fortify a credible useful lower-traffic house when the target needs protection and friendly visitor danger is limited.

**Action:** Fortify a credible but less crowded Town claim on N1 rather than following a mass TP/LO call.

**Target rule:** Prefer a useful information role or protector likely to attract an evil visitor but few Town visitors. With no information, choose a low-traffic eligible unknown away from obvious Veteran bait.

**Why:** You attack one random direct visitor regardless of faction; a crowded protection pile creates avoidable Town casualties.

**Change plan when:** If every known target is expected to receive Town visits and you cannot coordinate safely, holding is better than a clearly reckless assignment. Fortify the main asset only when the visitor risk is understood.

**Tradeoff:** You can attack one random friendly visitor, so a crowded call can harm Town even while the resident is protected.

**Checkpoints:**

- Protect without inviting a Town pileup
- Separate a visitor kill from guilt
- Coordinate the next night’s traffic

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Protect the useful Town target most likely to draw an evil visit without drawing several friendly visits.
2. Coordinate a single protection assignment with justified trusted Town when possible; explicitly avoid stacking visiting protectors on your target.
3. After attack feedback, compare deaths and visitor claims without assuming every attacked visitor was evil; adapt when rampage threatens the location.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Crusader_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Fortify the main asset with coordination

**Best when:** A high-value Town target needs your protection and the likely friendly direct visitors can avoid that house or knowingly account for the risk.

**Action:** Fortify that target on N1 only when the ability is enabled. Coordinate enough to reduce friendly visits; keep target-protection feedback separate from the one visitor attack.

**Target rule:** Use a supported threatened asset, not an unverified loud claim. Do not promise to choose which visitor gets attacked.

**Why:** Fortify combines protection of the resident with danger to one visitor. It can be worth using on the main asset when friendly traffic has been managed, instead of automatically abandoning every busy player.

**Change plan when:** Move to a lower-traffic useful house if coordination fails. Do not assume that merely announcing Crusader prevents all Town visits.

**Tradeoff:** An unexpected friendly visitor can still be attacked, and your Basic visitor attack may not kill a defended enemy.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify who really needs protection
- Reduce friendly direct traffic
- Separate resident and visitor outcomes

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Rotate when visitor risk or protection need changes.
2. Report only the visitor attack feedback actually received.
3. Never infer the attacked visitor’s identity without independent evidence.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Crusader_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-bug-fixes-patch-notes-r1-5-01/).

### Route 3: Hold when friendly traffic cannot be managed

**Best when:** Every plausible valuable target is expecting essential Town visits and no safe alternative house is apparent.

**Action:** Hold Fortify on N1 rather than deliberately placing a random visitor attack into necessary friendly traffic. Record why you held.

**Target rule:** No target is selected for this route. Use the following day to find a useful lower-traffic assignment or coordinate a safer protection plan.

**Why:** An ability with friendly-fire risk can have negative value in a known crowd. A reasoned hold preserves Town visitors instead of pretending activity is always beneficial.

**Change plan when:** Act once a worthwhile lower-risk target appears; do not turn one unsafe night into permanent inactivity.

**Tradeoff:** Holding leaves the potential resident unprotected by you and may miss an enemy visitor; it is for a known bad assignment, not general fear.

**Checkpoints:**

- Name the friendly-traffic risk
- Record a truthful hold
- Plan the next safe assignment

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Find a supported asset whose expected visitors are limited.
2. Coordinate before entering a crowded protection call.
3. Keep Fortify protection distinct from your random visitor attack.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Crusader_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-bug-fixes-patch-notes-r1-5-01/).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Crusader role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Crusader (TP) — Truthful role · recommended

You attack one random direct visitor regardless of faction; a crowded protection pile creates avoidable Town casualties.

- Start with the legal opening: Fortify a credible but less crowded Town claim on N1 rather than following a mass TP/LO call.
- Write the fortified player, with protection feedback and visitor-attack feedback kept separate.
- Do not claim to know which visitor was struck unless the game or independent evidence supplies it.
- Record at most the actual visitor attack rather than pretending you hit every visitor.

**Best when:** You are Crusader in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not pile onto a crowded TP/LO call unannounced. Do not treat a surviving visitor as a unique role. Do not assume you attack every visitor.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Crusader_(ToS2)).

## Oracle

TP — Town Protective.

### Route 1: Aegis Oracle without a credible assignment (recommended)

**Best when:** Use the self-role fallback when Aegis is enabled, no credible threatened role is known, and spending the once-only Oracle choice is worthwhile.

**Action:** If Aegis is enabled and you know no useful role claim, select Oracle on N1: you know at least your own role exists. If Grace Period disables Aegis, hold.

**Target rule:** Prefer a credible threatened role over the self-default when one is known. With no information, Oracle is the conservative fallback; record that its one use is spent.

**Why:** Blindly protecting an unconfirmed role can protect nobody; Oracle is certain to cover you, but spends your one Oracle selection.

**Change plan when:** Skip Oracle if you have a credible vulnerable Town role worth preserving, or expect little threat to yourself and want to reserve self-coverage. Never repeat the same role on consecutive nights.

**Tradeoff:** The Oracle choice can be used only once; guessing a role that is absent grants no useful coverage.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose a role, not a player
- Respect consecutive and self-role restrictions
- Update protection from actual role evidence

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Aegis the credible threatened Town role that is both present and likely to be attacked, considering other known protection.
2. On the required rotation night, protect the next most useful known role rather than guessing a famous role with no evidence it exists.
3. Use attack feedback to improve coordination and keep the exact role-selection history; Aegis chooses roles, not numbered players.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Aegis a credible threatened role

**Best when:** A useful vulnerable Town role is credibly known and preserving its holders is more urgent than covering your own role.

**Action:** Select that Town role for N1 Aegis if enabled, such as Seer when a credible Seer is exposed. Write the role name, not a player number.

**Target rule:** Choose the exact role category with evidence of presence and danger. A claimed alignment such as TI alone does not identify which Aegis role to select.

**Why:** This directs coverage toward an established Town asset while saving the one Oracle self-role choice. It is stronger than the blind self fallback when the target role is credible and exposed.

**Change plan when:** Choose a different role next night. If the role claim collapses or the threat is Unstoppable, reassess rather than treating Aegis as universal safety.

**Tradeoff:** The claim may be false or already protected, and Aegis does not tell you exactly who holds or was saved as that role.

**Checkpoints:**

- Verify which role is actually known
- Check the role differs from last night
- Log Aegis by role name

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Prioritize newly exposed useful Town role categories.
2. Reserve Oracle for a later meaningful self threat if it remains available.
3. Report actual protection feedback without inventing the saved player’s identity.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem2.com/news/nn2khtionvzvslwh2lcs8nmh64t8hr), [source 3](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-bug-fixes-patch-notes-r1-5-01/).

### Route 3: Reserve the one Oracle choice

**Best when:** No credible role is exposed, you expect little personal danger, and retaining guaranteed self-role coverage for later is worth uncertain N1 protection.

**Action:** If Aegis is enabled, choose a plausible allowed Town role from the actual setup instead of Oracle. If no role choice has a reasonable basis, record a deliberate hold.

**Target rule:** Use roledeck evidence where available. In an open list, acknowledge that a guessed role may not exist; never describe a guess as a known protected player.

**Why:** This keeps the once-only Oracle selection for a night with more personal threat. The tradeoff is explicitly accepting weaker immediate targeting information.

**Change plan when:** If a credible threatened role appears before lock-in, prefer that concrete assignment. Spend Oracle later when it answers a real threat rather than saving it past the match.

**Tradeoff:** This is lower priority because the selected role may be absent, and an unexpected N1 attack may kill you before the saved option matters.

**Checkpoints:**

- Preserve the limited Oracle option
- Use actual roledeck evidence
- Admit uncertain role presence

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use new claims to replace role guesses with informed coverage.
2. Never repeat the same role on consecutive nights.
3. Spend the saved self-role option when a credible threat justifies it.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem2.com/news/nn2khtionvzvslwh2lcs8nmh64t8hr), [source 3](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-bug-fixes-patch-notes-r1-5-01/).

### Claim strategy
