Use your actual Oracle role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Oracle (TP) — Truthful role · recommended

Blindly protecting an unconfirmed role can protect nobody; Oracle is certain to cover you, but spends your one Oracle selection.

- Start with the legal opening: If Aegis is enabled and you know no useful role claim, select Oracle on N1: you know at least your own role exists. If Grace Period disables Aegis, hold.
- Write Aegis Sheriff, for example, rather than Barrier 4.
- Choose a different role on consecutive nights and use the Oracle self-role option only once.
- If Grace Period disables N1 Aegis, write unavailable rather than a fictitious protection.

**Best when:** You are Oracle in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not write normal visiting protection wills. Do not repeat a role on consecutive nights. Do not claim precise player feedback the game did not supply.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

## Trapper

TP — Town Protective.

### Route 1: Build before planning your first Trap (recommended)

**Best when:** Build on N1 and prepare to place the first trap where it can preserve a valuable player without avoidable harmless traffic.

**Action:** Build on N1. You cannot place a fresh trap before completing the building night; record whether construction actually succeeded.

**Target rule:** No N1 player target is needed. Use D2 claims to choose a credible valuable target for the first placement; with no credible claim, use an eligible unknown whose survival matters more than expected harmless traffic.

**Why:** Your first useful protection requires a ready trap. Accurate state tracking prevents an impossible N1 placement and a wasted N2 plan.

**Change plan when:** If jailed, roleblocked, or otherwise prevented from building, rebuild instead of pretending a trap is ready. Avoid a placement expected to be immediately consumed by avoidable harmless visitors.

**Tradeoff:** A disrupted Build leaves no ready trap, and friendly visitors can consume the first placement.

**Checkpoints:**

- Complete the build
- Make one legal placement
- Interpret the actual trigger before rebuilding

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Once ready, place on a credible vulnerable Town asset likely to draw a harmful visitor.
2. Leave an untriggered useful trap in place; dismantle only when the old assignment has lost value enough to justify relocation delay.
3. After a trigger, report the exact protection or visitor-role feedback, then rebuild before trying to place again.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trapper_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Plan an information-focused first trap

**Best when:** No clear attack target emerges, but checking the role mix visiting an important house would resolve a concrete dispute.

**Action:** N1 is still Build; there is no alternate N1 placement. After successful construction, use D2 to plan a legal first Trap on the informative house for N2.

**Target rule:** Prefer a house where known visit claims are worth checking. Coordinate unnecessary friendly visits only when safe; do not invent a trap on yourself.

**Why:** When no harmful visit triggers its attack, a triggered trap can report visitor roles. Accepting that it may be spent for information can be reasonable if the information answers an important current question.

**Change plan when:** If a credible Town asset faces an urgent direct attack, prioritize protection instead. After a trigger, build again before promising another trap.

**Tradeoff:** A harmless visitor can spend the trap, role feedback does not itself identify each visitor, and hostile traffic may trigger a different outcome.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm Build actually completed
- Name the visitor-role question
- Distinguish a role list from identities

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Record the real trigger and role feedback.
2. Compare roles with visit claims without assigning unnamed roles to players automatically.
3. Rebuild and reassess the next target when the trap is consumed.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trapper_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Trapper role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Trapper (TP) — Truthful role · recommended

Your first useful protection requires a ready trap. Accurate state tracking prevents an impossible N1 placement and a wasted N2 plan.

- Start with the legal opening: Build on N1. You cannot place a fresh trap before completing the building night; record whether construction actually succeeded.
- Start with N1 Build unless an actual setup effect changes availability.
- Record placement on a later night and preserve that original house while the trap remains active.
- If it triggers, copy reported roles and the actual protection outcome; do not invent visitor numbers.

**Best when:** You are Trapper in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not place N1 as though already built. Do not keep two active traps. Do not claim a trigger while still rebuilding.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trapper_(ToS2)).

## Deputy

TK — Town Killing.

### Route 1: Preserve the shot and build evidence (recommended)

**Best when:** Keep the shot while evidence is incomplete or an ordinary Town vote can resolve the same suspect.

**Action:** No N1 action. Prepare one D2 contradiction question and preserve your single day shot; you cannot shoot on D1.

**Target rule:** Build a case from incompatible claims or independent evidence. With no information, ask for actions and wait; silence, confidence, or refusal to name an exact role immediately is not enough.

**Why:** A speculative shot can spend your only charge without resolving the game. Investigation through discussion is free.

**Change plan when:** Fire on a later legal day phase when a supported evil is worth removing now. If the evidence remains weak or expected Defense makes the shot ineffective, keep the charge.

**Tradeoff:** Holding too long can forfeit an urgent removal, but firing weak evidence may waste your only shot.

**Checkpoints:**

- Build one supported case before shooting
- Keep a miss unresolved
- Continue gathering evidence after the shot is spent

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Challenge the best specific contradiction and hear the explanation before shooting.
2. Use the day shot when evidence and timing justify it; check known defense and whether the target is a sensible shooting candidate.
3. After shooting, mark the charge spent and continue through votes and questions; a miss does not clear the target as Town.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Prepare a justified D2 shot

**Best when:** A strongly supported enemy needs to die before another day action or cannot be removed through the available vote, and Basic Attack is likely to work.

**Action:** There is no N1 action or D1 shot. Use N1 to assemble the case; on D2, fire only in a permitted day phase after checking new evidence and expected defense.

**Target rule:** Prefer a concrete evil contradiction over silence. Account for early Neutral Evil defense and defended killing roles that can make a shot fail.

**Why:** A day shot can act before another night and solve an urgent timing problem. Its value comes from the particular threat removed, not publicly demonstrating that you have a button.

**Change plan when:** If the suspect has likely defense or Town can vote them normally, preserve the charge. A miss is not proof that the target is Town.

**Tradeoff:** The shot is limited, a failed attempt need not reveal the role publicly, and framing can make simple hit-based deductions unsafe.

**Checkpoints:**

- Write the supported evil case
- Check likely Basic-Attack failure
- Identify why removal is urgent

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use the actual outcome rather than a fabricated hit or miss story.
2. Reassess a miss using Town status, defense, framing, and genuine evidence.
3. Once the shot is spent, contribute through discussion and voting.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem2.com/news/nn2khtionvzvslwh2lcs8nmh64t8hr).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Deputy role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Deputy (TK) — Truthful role · recommended

A speculative shot can spend your only charge without resolving the game. Investigation through discussion is free.

- Start with the legal opening: No N1 action. Prepare one D2 contradiction question and preserve your single day shot; you cannot shoot on D1.
- Write N1 No night ability rather than inventing a nightly check.
- A shot is D2 or later and consumes your only use; record the day target and actual hit or miss.
- Do not describe a private miss as a public demonstration of your identity.

**Best when:** You are Deputy in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not use outdated shoot-Town-and-die rules. Do not expect multiple bullets. Do not treat a private miss as hard confirmation.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)).

## Trickster

TK — Town Killing.

### Route 1: Mirror a credible vulnerable Town actor (recommended)

**Best when:** Mirror a credible useful likely attack target while preserving the once-only Self Mirror for a more personal threat.

**Action:** Place Magic Mirror on a credible vulnerable Town target on N1; reserve your one Self Mirror unless you are the likely attack target.

**Target rule:** Prefer a supported exposed Town Power or information role likely to be attacked. With no information, choose an eligible unknown away from obvious Veteran bait rather than random Self Mirror.

**Why:** Absorbing an attack can save an ally, identify the attacking role, and create your later offensive action.

**Change plan when:** Use Self Mirror when a credible attack threat is on you. Do not expect Mirror to stop every passive, indirect, or Unstoppable attack.

**Tradeoff:** Mirror absorbs one compatible attack and cannot stop every indirect or Unstoppable threat.

**Checkpoints:**

- Attempt a reasoned Mirror
- Track the real stored attack
- Unleash only on a supported target

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. While you have no stored attack, Mirror the most credible likely direct-attack target and record any absorbed attacking role.
2. After absorption, Unleash on a supported evil when the stored attack and collateral risk make sense; do not fire merely to clear the slot.
3. Once safely discharged, return to protecting likely targets; if the stored attack rampages, account for friendly visitors before selecting its destination.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trickster_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Self Mirror when enemies may target you

**Best when:** You are a credible likely attack target, the one self option is available, and another external assignment is less valuable.

**Action:** Use Self Mirror on N1 if enabled. Record whether an attack was actually absorbed and the exact attacking role feedback if supplied.

**Target rule:** Choose yourself because of exposure or a concrete threat, not simply to manufacture a private result. Keep Town’s other uncovered assets in mind.

**Why:** Surviving with a stored attack can preserve your future protection and give useful attacker-role evidence. This is a limited defensive resource, not an automatic free N1 defense.

**Change plan when:** If no attack is absorbed, resume external Mirrors as appropriate. If an attack is stored, resolve the Unleash decision before promising new Mirrors.

**Tradeoff:** Using the once-only self option leaves someone else uncovered; some indirect or Unstoppable attacks bypass or break Mirror.

**Checkpoints:**

- Check the self use is available
- Separate selection from absorption
- Preserve the exact attack role

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Unleash only on a supported enemy with the stored attack’s risks understood.
2. Account for harmful-visit protection and any stored rampage.
3. Return to protective Mirrors after the stored attack is safely discharged.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trickster_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-bug-fixes-patch-notes-r1-5-01/).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Trickster role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Trickster (TK) — Truthful role · recommended

Absorbing an attack can save an ally, identify the attacking role, and create your later offensive action.

- Start with the legal opening: Place Magic Mirror on a credible vulnerable Town target on N1; reserve your one Self Mirror unless you are the likely attack target.
- Write the mirror target first; ordinary mirroring is not a kill and does not guarantee a save.
- If an attack is absorbed, record the reported attacker role and hold that attack until Unleash.
- Do not add another Mirror night while the stored attack remains unused.

**Best when:** You are Trickster in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not use obsolete taunt mechanics. Do not mirror again while holding an unused attack. Do not unleash a rampage onto crowded allied traffic.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trickster_(ToS2)).

## Veteran

TK — Town Killing.

### Route 1: Conserve Alert on a quiet night (recommended)

**Best when:** Conserve Alert on a quiet N1 when no concrete signal makes hostile visitors more likely than useful friendly visitors.

**Action:** Default to no Alert on a quiet, low-information N1. Alert if your D1 behavior or a concrete threat makes an evil visit more plausible than friendly visits.

**Target rule:** You choose your own house, not a player target. With no information, preserve the charge; do not invite TP/LO and then shoot the responders.

**Why:** You have three Alerts and shoot all direct visitors, including Town; spending one blindly can remove allies instead of enemies.

**Change plan when:** Alert tonight if a credible attack or hostile visit is expected and you have discouraged friendly visitors. Check Grace Period and roleblock risk rather than assuming Alert guarantees safety.

**Tradeoff:** You remain exposed to attacks while holding, but a blind Alert can shoot Town visitors and waste one of three charges.

**Checkpoints:**

- Avoid Town visitor bait
- Spend an Alert for a reason
- Explain deaths without assuming visitor guilt

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use Alert on nights when an enemy visit is plausible and friendly traffic can be kept low.
2. After any shots, compare the count with deaths and claims without assuming every visitor was evil.
3. Preserve at least some defensive flexibility while the match continues, but spend remaining Alerts when a supported imminent threat makes saving them pointless.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Veteran_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Alert against a credible hostile visit

**Best when:** Your actual D1 exposure or a concrete threat makes an enemy visit plausible, and you have not invited friendly Town visitors.

**Action:** Alert on N1 if the client enables it. Do not add a fake TP/LO call; record the actual shot count and feedback at dawn.

**Target rule:** The selected house is your own. First consider which Town roles might reasonably visit you and discourage avoidable friendly traffic without fabricating information.

**Why:** Spending an Alert is justified when likely hostile traffic gives it survival or removal value. This is a threat-based exception to conserving on a quiet night.

**Change plan when:** If the threat disappears or necessary friendly visits are likely, hold. An Alert selection may still be roleblocked and does not grant universal protection.

**Tradeoff:** Alert attacks all direct visitors regardless of faction, while roleblocks, stronger attacks, and astral actions can defeat simple safety assumptions.

**Checkpoints:**

- Check hostility is plausible
- Avoid soliciting Town visitors
- Record actual shots only

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Compare shot count, deaths, and real claims.
2. Treat surviving visitors and missing deaths as incomplete evidence.
3. Spend later Alerts when imminent threats justify them instead of saving every charge indefinitely.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Veteran_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Veteran role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Veteran (TK) — Truthful role · recommended

You have three Alerts and shoot all direct visitors, including Town; spending one blindly can remove allies instead of enemies.

- Start with the legal opening: Default to no Alert on a quiet, low-information N1. Alert if your D1 behavior or a concrete threat makes an evil visit more plausible than friendly visits.
- Write Alert or No Alert for each night, tracking the three total uses.
- Record only the visitor-shot count actually reported; a count does not reveal player identities.
- If roleblocked or otherwise interrupted, preserve that fact rather than treating the selected Alert as successful.

**Best when:** You are Veteran in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not use TP/LO bait as a beginner default. Do not assume roleblock immunity. Do not claim infinite alerts.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Veteran_(ToS2)).

## Vigilante

TK — Town Killing.

### Route 1: Load first and set an evidence threshold (recommended)

**Best when:** Load on N1, then preserve the shot unless a supported evil is a sensible Basic-Attack target.

**Action:** Load on N1 by staying home. You cannot shoot tonight; at dawn check that loading actually succeeded.

**Target rule:** No N1 target is needed. Prepare a D2 shortlist from conflicting claims; with no evidence, load or hold instead of treating a random shot as investigation.

**Why:** A loaded bullet creates the option to remove a supported evil later. An interrupted load does not create ammunition.

**Change plan when:** From N2, shoot when a supported evil case and expected Defense justify the Basic Attack. Hold when the target may be Town, protected, or a lethal visiting hazard.

**Tradeoff:** Loading does not promise a safe future shot, and shooting Town removes further gun use.

**Checkpoints:**

- Complete the first Load
- Establish a case before firing
- Track ammunition and whether the gun is still usable

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Shoot a well-supported evil when removing them tonight matters and the attack is likely to work.
2. Without a justified shot, stay home to Load if a load remains available; check the client’s ammunition state.
3. After a shot, record success/failure and whether the gun is lost; shooting Town removes further gun use, so do not plan a second shot from outdated state.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vigilante_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Prepare the first supported N2 shot

**Best when:** D2 evidence is likely to identify an evil whose removal tonight matters and who is not expected to resist or turn your visit against Town.

**Action:** N1 remains Load by staying home; no N1 shot exists. At dawn confirm ammunition, then use D2 to test a specific case for a possible N2 shot.

**Target rule:** Prefer a well-supported vulnerable enemy over a defended Neutral Killing or a player who is merely unwilling to full-claim. Check visitor hazards and friendly protection.

**Why:** An evidence-backed early shot may remove a dangerous actor before another day. Planning the threshold in advance is useful; deciding the victim before D2 evidence arrives is not.

**Change plan when:** If the case stays uncertain, stay home to Load when another load is available or hold if already prepared. Do not treat the gun as a random investigation.

**Tradeoff:** A wrongly chosen Town target costs more than one bullet, and a night spent attempting to shoot does not also load.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm the N1 Load completed
- Require a concrete supported case
- Check attack and visiting risk

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Shoot only when removal and expected success justify the risk.
2. Record actual outcome, ammunition, and whether you still have the gun.
3. Return to loading or holding when another shot lacks support.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vigilante_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem2.com/news/nn2khtionvzvslwh2lcs8nmh64t8hr).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Vigilante role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Vigilante (TK) — Truthful role · recommended

A loaded bullet creates the option to remove a supported evil later. An interrupted load does not create ammunition.

- Start with the legal opening: Load on N1 by staying home. You cannot shoot tonight; at dawn check that loading actually succeeded.
- Start N1 Load if it succeeds; record an interruption instead if loading failed.
- Keep Load and Shoot on separate nights and track at most three total loads.
- A shot without a death is not a confirmed evil: defense or disruption can matter.

**Best when:** You are Vigilante in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not use the old automatic-suicide rule. Do not shoot N1. Do not claim a fresh bullet without a valid load.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vigilante_(ToS2)).

## Admirer

TS — Town Support.

### Route 1: Search using the Obsession alignment clue (recommended)

**Best when:** Use the starting subalignment clue to search compatible candidates, keeping Bestow for a justified useful beneficiary.

**Action:** Admire an eligible player whose claim matches your Obsession’s starting subalignment; with no such claim, choose an eligible unknown.

**Target rule:** Prefer a compatible credible Town claim. With no information, choose an unknown rather than a known Town Power, Town Outlier, or Amnesiac, which cannot be the normal starting Obsession.

**Why:** Finding your Obsession unlocks Care, while the alignment clue helps avoid spending nights on incompatible candidates.

**Change plan when:** If the suspected Obsession dies before discovery, follow the new clue rather than continuing the old search. Do not treat a matching alignment claim as proof.

**Tradeoff:** A compatible claim can be false, and a blind Bestow can permanently improve an enemy.

**Checkpoints:**

- Use the Obsession clue
- Confirm whether Care unlocked
- Spend support charges on a reasoned ally

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Before discovery, Admire the best remaining eligible candidate matching the current Obsession clue.
2. After discovery, spend Care when the Obsession or you face a credible attack; preserve both charges when neither is threatened.
3. Use a daytime Bestow on a justified trusted useful Town actor who benefits from astral visits and disruption immunity; avoid permanently upgrading an untested evil claim.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Admirer_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Bestow an evidenced Town actor early

**Best when:** A useful Town actor has concrete independent support and benefits from astral visits or immunity to disruption; an eligible day Bestow is available.

**Action:** Use the daytime Bestow on that supported actor, then continue the normal N1 Admire search among candidates matching your Obsession clue. Bestow is not a different night action.

**Target rule:** Choose the beneficiary for a specific useful action they need to carry out. Your Obsession search and your Bestow target need not be the same person.

**Why:** The permanent upgrade can protect repeated useful actions from direct-visit danger, roleblocking, or control. Acting early is worthwhile when the trust is real rather than only an alignment claim.

**Change plan when:** If the candidate is only persuasive or their ability gains little, preserve Bestow. Keep searching until real feedback replaces Admire with Care.

**Tradeoff:** Bestow is permanent, and upgrading the wrong player may make an enemy harder to disrupt or observe.

**Checkpoints:**

- Separate Bestow from Obsession search
- Name the beneficiary’s useful action
- Preserve actual discovery feedback

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. After discovery, use Care for a credible threat rather than automatically spending both charges.
2. Keep the second Bestow for another evidenced useful beneficiary.
3. If the clue rerolls before discovery, stop searching from the obsolete clue.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Admirer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-season-5-patch-notes-r1-5-00/).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Admirer role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Admirer (TS) — Truthful role · recommended

Finding your Obsession unlocks Care, while the alignment clue helps avoid spending nights on incompatible candidates.

- Start with the legal opening: Admire an eligible player whose claim matches your Obsession’s starting subalignment; with no such claim, choose an eligible unknown.
- Record the starting Obsession subalignment and each Admire target with its actual result.
- Use Care only after discovery, tracking its two charges separately.
- Record daytime Bestows with a D label; they are not nighttime Admire actions, and only two are available.

**Best when:** You are Admirer in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not use marriage/proposal or toxic-relationship templates. Do not Care before finding the Obsession. Do not invent an impossible Obsession category.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Admirer_(ToS2)).

## Amnesiac

TS — Town Support.

### Route 1: Wait honestly for automatic Remember (recommended)

**Best when:** Wait truthfully for automatic Remember feedback and prepare to adopt the new role’s actual current state.

**Action:** No selectable N1 ability in a normal fresh match. Record that you are waiting to Remember an eligible dead Town role.

**Target rule:** No N1 player target exists. With no information, collect claims and watch the graveyard; let actual morning feedback determine your new role.

**Why:** Remembering is automatic; inventing a corpse choice or an investigation obscures what the role actually does.

**Change plan when:** If you Remember, switch immediately to that role’s current legal state and opening priorities. Do not assume every death is eligible or that you choose the most attractive corpse.

**Tradeoff:** You have no selectable N1 ability and cannot choose a favorite corpse; waiting is not evidence you performed a check.

**Checkpoints:**

- Observe the actual Remember
- Preserve the transition history
- Follow the acquired role without inventing past results

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Check morning role feedback and the graveyard so you recognize a successful Remember promptly.
2. After Remembering, use the new role’s best currently available action; account for the current night rather than replaying its N1 instruction.
3. Preserve your earlier Amnesiac history and explain the transition truthfully when needed; do not erase missing-action nights to look stronger.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Amnesiac_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Disclose the transition timeline early

**Best when:** Claim collection starts before you have Remembered, or a new role will otherwise make your missing early actions look inconsistent.

**Action:** N1 still has no selectable action. State your true Amnesiac waiting state when requested, preserve the morning feedback, and explain the later role transition when it actually occurs.

**Target rule:** No player or corpse target is selected. Focus on a dated account of which nights you could not act and what role you really received.

**Why:** A contemporaneous honest account makes the later change easier to follow without inventing an impossible early ability. This route prioritizes explaining the timeline over minimizing exact-role disclosure.

**Change plan when:** After Remembering, use the new role’s legal current action immediately. A remembered setup role may still need Load, Build, or another prerequisite.

**Tradeoff:** Early disclosure may advertise a weak or changing claim; it does not publicly prove that you were Amnesiac.

**Checkpoints:**

- State the real waiting nights
- Check actual Remember feedback
- Switch to the new legal action

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Keep your original Amnesiac line in the will.
2. Use the received role’s present charges and prerequisites.
3. Give Town useful votes and questions even while no eligible role arrives.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Amnesiac_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Amnesiac role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Amnesiac (TS) — Truthful role · recommended

Remembering is automatic; inventing a corpse choice or an investigation obscures what the role actually does.

- Start with the legal opening: No selectable N1 ability in a normal fresh match. Record that you are waiting to Remember an eligible dead Town role.
- Write Waiting to remember, not a night visit to select a corpse.
- Remember is automatic at the eligible morning; Town Power priority and Unique-role restrictions can matter.
- Mark the transition day and new role before logging its later actions.

**Best when:** You are Amnesiac in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not claim a chosen remember target like ToS1. Do not remain indefinitely unremembered after suitable deaths without explanation. Do not erase the role timeline.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Amnesiac_(ToS2)).

## Retributionist

TS — Town Support.

### Route 1: Wait for a useful legal Town corpse (recommended)

**Best when:** Wait on a normal N1 with no usable corpse, then choose a legal Town body whose ability solves the current most important problem.

**Action:** In a normal fresh match there is no eligible corpse to Raise on N1. Prepare a truthful “no usable body” opening.

**Target rule:** No N1 target is required without a body. On later nights prefer a corpse whose ability solves a current need, then choose its legal living target; without a useful body, hold.

**Why:** Your action depends on actual dead Town roles; a generic N1 raise would be impossible.

**Change plan when:** Never invent access to an evil, stoned, unavailable, or otherwise ineligible corpse. A powerful corpse is still a poor choice if its current target is speculative or dangerous.

**Tradeoff:** Corpse access can fail, and a powerful borrowed ability is still harmful when aimed at an unsupported or dangerous target.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify a usable Town corpse
- Choose its ability for a current need
- Record the borrowed action and actual outcome

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Raise a protective Town corpse when it can preserve a credible threatened asset, or a useful investigator when it can test a decisive unresolved case.
2. Use a killing corpse only for a supported evil and with its attack, friendly-fire, and visiting risks understood.
3. Track used bodies, corpse-specific feedback, and actual destinations so later choices follow remaining eligibility rather than a copied will template.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Retributionist_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Plan a decisive information-first Raise

**Best when:** A legal investigative Town body becomes available and its result can resolve an important conflict more safely than a speculative attack.

**Action:** There is no alternate Raise in a normal fresh N1. After a suitable body exists, choose it and a legal living target for the borrowed investigative action.

**Target rule:** Name the precise question first: a disputed destination, an unresolved crime account, or another ability-specific check. Avoid unsafe visits and respect that body’s restrictions.

**Why:** A modest investigator can be the highest-value corpse when its result changes today’s case. Picking by the job avoids choosing a killing body solely because attacks feel stronger.

**Change plan when:** If a credible Town asset faces an urgent attack, use a compatible protective body instead. If corpse competition causes failure, record that actual failure.

**Tradeoff:** Another corpse user may take priority, and an information result can remain limited or affected by its original role’s mechanics.

**Checkpoints:**

- Check the body is truly usable
- Name the information question
- Log body and living target separately

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Report actual borrowed feedback with the corpse role identified.
2. Reassess the current job before selecting the next body.
3. Use lethal bodies only with supported targets and their attack restrictions understood.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Retributionist_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Retributionist role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Retributionist (TS) — Truthful role · recommended

Your action depends on actual dead Town roles; a generic N1 raise would be impossible.

- Start with the legal opening: In a normal fresh match there is no eligible corpse to Raise on N1. Prepare a truthful “no usable body” opening.
- Write No usable corpse on ordinary N1 instead of inventing a visit.
- List the dead player and their usable true role, followed by the living destination.
- Your own visit goes to the corpse; the zombie goes to the living target. Keep that direction clear.

**Best when:** You are Retributionist in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not use stoned bodies as a standard Town Ret. Do not self-protect through a corpse. Do not claim actions from an unsuitable body.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Retributionist_(ToS2)).

## Socialite

TS — Town Support.

### Route 1: Party with a safe visitor plan (recommended)

**Best when:** Party at a credible useful target only when blocking uninvited visits helps more than it obstructs necessary Town work.

**Action:** If Party is enabled, Party at a credible vulnerable Town target whose expected friendly visitors are absent or safely covered by your guest list.

**Target rule:** Prefer a useful supported claim with low friendly traffic. With no trustworthy target or visitor plan, hold rather than blindly obstruct a mass TP/LO destination.

**Why:** Blocking an enemy visit can preserve a key player, but an uncoordinated Party can also block Town protection and information.

**Change plan when:** Choose another house when friendly visitors need access and are not guests. Never Party at the same player on consecutive nights; a rejected-visitor message does not identify an evil.

**Tradeoff:** A Party can block allies and permanently invited enemies retain access; rejected-visitor feedback gives no identities.

**Checkpoints:**

- Track invitations separately
- Party for a clear access-control purpose
- Adjust the next house and guest plan

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Invite a justified trusted useful Town visitor when a permanent guest slot gives them valuable access; do not spend slots on persuasive strangers.
2. Party at the credible threatened house where blocking uninvited visits helps more than it disrupts Town; rotate to a different target each night.
3. Use actual obstruction feedback and destination claims to investigate conflicts, without naming unseen rejected players or calling every blocked visit evil.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Socialite_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Hold until friendly access is clear

**Best when:** The only worthwhile house is expecting necessary Town protection or investigation and those visitors are not safely covered by your Guest List.

**Action:** Hold Party on N1 and keep guest slots unspent if no visitor has earned trust. Ask for enough coordination on D2 to make a later assignment useful.

**Target rule:** No host is selected tonight. Identify which genuine friendly action would be blocked before deciding where the next Party should go.

**Why:** Preserving Town’s necessary visits can be better than placing an obstacle merely to act. This is a deliberate route for a bad traffic situation, not an instruction to remain inactive forever.

**Change plan when:** Party once a useful house has a reasonable friendly-access plan. Never choose the same host on consecutive nights.

**Tradeoff:** Holding gives no protection from your Party, so reassess promptly when a defensible target becomes available.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify necessary friendly visitors
- Keep untrusted guests off the list
- Prepare a safe later host

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use limited guest invitations for genuinely supported useful visitors.
2. Party where obstruction has a concrete protective or disruptive benefit.
3. Treat blocked-visit feedback as an obstacle event, not a named evil visitor.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Socialite_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-season-5-patch-notes-r1-5-00/).

### Route 3: Admit one trusted essential visitor

**Best when:** A credible protector or other necessary Town visitor needs access to the intended Party house and is a justified permanent guest.

**Action:** During D1, add that player to the Guest List if the day action is available; then Party at the chosen host on N1 if enabled and the remaining traffic is acceptable.

**Target rule:** Choose the guest and host separately. Invite for a specific needed visit, not to prove friendliness or because a stranger promises to protect you.

**Why:** Allowing an essential trusted visitor through can make a valuable-house Party useful while still blocking uninvited direct visitors. The invitation is the key extra commitment.

**Change plan when:** If trust is weak or several necessary uninvited Town visitors still need entry, use another house or hold. Invitations are permanent and cannot be removed.

**Tradeoff:** The guest may be an enemy and will retain future access; Party has bypasses and cannot guarantee a safe host.

**Checkpoints:**

- Separate guest from host
- Justify the permanent access
- Check other friendly traffic

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Preserve the remaining guest slot unless another concrete access need arises.
2. Rotate hosts legally as threat changes.
3. Compare real obstruction feedback with claims without inventing rejected names.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Socialite_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-season-5-patch-notes-r1-5-00/).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Socialite role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Socialite (TS) — Truthful role · recommended

Blocking an enemy visit can preserve a key player, but an uncoordinated Party can also block Town protection and information.

- Start with the legal opening: If Party is enabled, Party at a credible vulnerable Town target whose expected friendly visitors are absent or safely covered by your guest list.
- Keep daytime invitations separate from night parties: at most two manual guests total, one added per day.
- Never ordinarily party at the same house on consecutive nights.
- Write rejected someone only when the game reports it; that feedback does not identify who was rejected.

**Best when:** You are Socialite in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not automatically invite rejected visitors. Do not repeat the same party house. Do not report identities you did not receive.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Socialite_(ToS2)).

## Tavern Keeper

TS — Town Support.

### Route 1: Drink with a plausible unresolved enemy (recommended)

**Best when:** Drink with an unresolved plausible harmful actor when the disruption value outweighs blocking Town or visiting danger.

**Action:** Drink with a credible unresolved suspect on N1; avoid blocking an openly useful Town night role just to test the claim.

**Target rule:** Prefer a specific behavioral or claim concern. With no information, choose an eligible unknown away from obvious Veteran bait, while acknowledging that the random block may hit Town.

**Why:** An effective roleblock may disrupt an evil action, while blocking a real investigator or protector costs Town useful work.

**Change plan when:** If the only plausible target is a likely Serial Killer or a dangerous full-moon Werewolf, reassess visiting risk or hold. Do not repeat a successfully blocked Hungover target immediately.

**Tradeoff:** A random block can suppress Town, while some killers retaliate or ignore disruption.

**Checkpoints:**

- Get one honest disruption record
- Compare the target’s account
- Rotate targets under Hungover

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Disrupt the supported enemy night actor whose next action most threatens Town, unless immunity or visiting danger makes the attempt ineffective.
2. After a successful Drink, rotate to another eligible target while Hungover prevents an immediate repeat block.
3. Compare roleblock feedback with claims and deaths, then ask targeted questions; a night without a kill is a lead, not proof you found the killer.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tavern_Keeper_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Hold against a likely retaliating killer

**Best when:** Your only meaningful suspect is a likely uncautious Serial Killer or a full-moon Werewolf, and a direct visit would be unreasonably dangerous.

**Action:** Hold Drink on N1 if no safer useful disruption target exists. Record the suspected hazard and use D2 discussion to obtain a safer supported assignment.

**Target rule:** No target is selected for this route. If another credible unresolved harmful actor exists, blocking that player is preferable to a purely passive night.

**Why:** Tavern Keeper needs to survive to provide repeat disruption. Walking into a well-supported retaliation trap is a poor trade when the block does not safely remove the threat.

**Change plan when:** Return to Drink when a useful eligible target appears. After a successful block, rotate while Hungover prevents an immediate repeat.

**Tradeoff:** Holding allows a possible enemy action; the route requires a concrete visiting hazard rather than fear of every unknown.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify the actual retaliation risk
- Look for a safer useful suspect
- Record a truthful hold if needed

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Prefer supported functioning enemy night actors.
2. Distinguish successful roleblock feedback from a missing kill.
3. Avoid assuming Drink disables a day shot or prosecution.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tavern_Keeper_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Tavern Keeper role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Tavern Keeper (TS) — Truthful role · recommended

An effective roleblock may disrupt an evil action, while blocking a real investigator or protector costs Town useful work.

- Start with the legal opening: Drink with a credible unresolved suspect on N1; avoid blocking an openly useful Town night role just to test the claim.
- Write Drink and the chosen target; do not describe every selected visit as a confirmed successful block.
- After a successful Drink, rotate targets because Hungover prevents an immediate repeat block.
- Separate failed or immune interactions from successful disruption.

**Best when:** You are Tavern Keeper in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not claim to block day abilities. Do not confuse Hangover with a new successful block. Do not assume risky killers are safe targets.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tavern_Keeper_(ToS2)).

## Catalyst

Town Outlier — Town Outlier.

### Route 1: Boost only a trusted useful actor (recommended)

**Best when:** Hold without a trusted useful beneficiary; Overcharge when you can name a concrete extra Town action that is worth enabling.

**Action:** Overcharge a well-supported useful Town night actor if one exists; with no trustworthy target on N1, hold.

**Target rule:** Prefer a credible investigator or protector with a useful following-night plan. With no information, preserve Town safety by holding rather than upgrading a random claim.

**Why:** Overcharge can also double an enemy’s ability, including book attacks. Randomly maximizing activity is not the same as helping Town.

**Change plan when:** Act promptly once a trusted useful candidate appears; do not wait forever for absolute certainty. Avoid upgrading a role whose doubled action is unknown or whose extra lethal action is unsupported.

**Tradeoff:** Overcharge can double enemy abilities, and the interaction table is incomplete for some role-specific effects.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify a supported recipient
- Understand that role’s Overcharge
- Confirm timing through actual feedback

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Overcharge the best-supported Town actor whose additional next-night action can answer an important question or preserve a key ally.
2. Confirm the actual timing and role-specific effect with the recipient’s feedback before planning another charge around it.
3. Reassess trust and the next night’s job every day; move the boost when a different Town action has higher value rather than permanently feeding one friendly claim.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Catalyst_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Overcharge a trusted investigator

**Best when:** A supported investigator has two useful legal questions for the affected later night and the setup permits Catalyst.

**Action:** Overcharge that eligible Town actor on N1, then confirm their actual timing and role-specific feedback before planning the extra checks.

**Target rule:** Prefer concrete information work with legal targets, such as two unresolved Sheriff searches. Mere confidence or a request for more actions is not enough trust.

**Why:** A planned additional information action is easier to evaluate than blindly doubling an unknown ability. It can accelerate resolving claims without choosing a speculative second killing target.

**Change plan when:** If the recipient’s faction or effect is doubtful, hold. Do not promise that every role receives identical double-action behavior.

**Tradeoff:** A false investigator may be a book holder, turning your help into another attack; some interaction details require client feedback.

**Checkpoints:**

- Check the role is supported by setup
- Name the extra useful checks
- Confirm when the boost applies

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Compare the extra feedback with the planned ability.
2. Move the boost as the most valuable trusted job changes.
3. Avoid reapplying a boost that provides no additional effect.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Catalyst_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-season-5-patch-notes-r1-5-00/).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Catalyst role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Catalyst (Town Outlier) — Truthful role · recommended

Overcharge can also double an enemy’s ability, including book attacks. Randomly maximizing activity is not the same as helping Town.

- Start with the legal opening: Overcharge a well-supported useful Town night actor if one exists; with no trustworthy target on N1, hold.
- Record Overcharge target, actual application result, and the expected affected cycle.
- Do not automatically claim a same-night extra action; follow that recipient’s ability and client feedback.
- Keep observed extra actions separate from merely applying the charge.

**Best when:** You are Catalyst in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not assume it can spawn from any normal bucket. Do not promise every role behaves identically. Do not use a guessed same-night timing rule.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Catalyst_(ToS2)).

## Pilgrim

Town Outlier — Town Outlier.

### Route 1: Build a truthful claim ledger (recommended)

**Best when:** Use the claim-ledger and evidence route in an open or uncertain setup; you have no night ability to substitute for reasoning.

**Action:** No N1 ability. Record claims and prepare one precise D2 question. In an explicitly constrained Pilgrim setup, track the available Pilgrim slots.

**Target rule:** No night target exists. With no information, ask for consistent action histories and compare claims to known role slots; do not equate being uncounterclaimed with universal confirmation.

**Why:** Your contribution is social deduction and voting; claiming fake investigative results gives Town a false evidence base.

**Change plan when:** If slot counts are fixed, a competing excess Pilgrim claim can be valuable evidence; in open lists, the absence of a counterclaim says much less.

**Tradeoff:** Being uncounterclaimed in an open role list does not confirm Town, and invented investigative results mislead allies.

**Checkpoints:**

- Keep an honest action history
- Ask one evidence-based question
- Vote using a reason you can explain

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Keep a claim ledger and ask the strongest unresolved contradiction question each day.
2. Vote with supported cases and compare the claim space with the actual known setup; preserve Town’s vote control.
3. Update your reads when genuine results, deaths, and actions arrive, and leave useful concise reasoning in your will rather than fabricated visits.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pilgrim_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Audit a fixed Pilgrim slot

**Best when:** The roledeck explicitly restricts Pilgrim to a known number of possible slots and competing claims exceed that space.

**Action:** No N1 action exists. Record the actual roledeck and each competing claim, then ask for those histories and explain the slot conflict on D2.

**Target rule:** Focus on genuinely incompatible slot claims. Include random or dual buckets and modifiers before saying a claim is impossible.

**Why:** In a constrained list, claim arithmetic can produce a concrete contradiction without any active ability. It is stronger than treating the loudest competing claimant as automatically false.

**Change plan when:** If the deck is open or other slots can legitimately contain Pilgrim, return to ordinary evidence gathering. A counterclaim identifies a conflict, not which individual is evil.

**Tradeoff:** Misreading random buckets creates false certainty; this route applies only when the displayed setup actually limits the claims.

**Checkpoints:**

- Count the real available slots
- Record each competing claim
- Keep the accusation conditional

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Compare the conflicting players’ action histories and voting.
2. Update slot counts after genuine reveals and deaths.
3. Use supported cases to preserve Town vote control.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pilgrim_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-season-5-patch-notes-r1-5-00/).

### Claim strategy

Use your actual Pilgrim role and truthful feedback. A missed action or quiet night is worth explaining accurately; another role’s sample is not an ability you possess.

#### 1. Pilgrim (Town Outlier) — Truthful role · recommended

Your contribution is social deduction and voting; claiming fake investigative results gives Town a false evidence base.

- Start with the legal opening: No N1 ability. Record claims and prepare one precise D2 question. In an explicitly constrained Pilgrim setup, track the available Pilgrim slots.
- Record public claims or vote changes with D labels; these are observations, not ability results.
- Write No night ability instead of choosing a fictitious protective or investigative target.
- Separate another player’s report from something you personally learned.

**Best when:** You are Pilgrim in the normal-faction setup; report what actually happened.

**Exposure/limits:** Do not fabricate results as the beginner default. Do not claim the role in an ineligible setup. Do not treat silence as helpful play.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pilgrim_(ToS2)).

## Archmage

CPow — Coven Power.

### Route 1: Make the team kill; do not Retrain just because you can. (recommended)

**Best when:** The book attack is enabled and no specific team problem justifies giving up the kill.

**Action:** With the book, coordinate one useful enemy kill. Without it, preserve the current team roles unless Retrain solves a specific problem.

**Target rule:** Choose a nonally with useful enemy value and no obvious visitor trap. With no information, coordinate an eligible unknown; never attack a teammate by accident.

**Why:** A kill usually advances Coven more directly than replacing a useful ally with an unfamiliar role.

**Change plan when:** Retrain instead when the team has a concrete need for the replacement and accepts losing the ordinary attack that night. If Grace Period disables the attack, plan rather than invent a kill.

**Tradeoff:** A direct attack can hit protection or a visitor trap. Retrain changes real abilities and can break an ally’s established cover.

**Checkpoints:**

- Coordinate the first team action
- Preserve a compatible public cover
- Use Retrain for a specific team need

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Remove an exposed high-value enemy when the team can realistically reach them.
2. Use Retrain to repair a weak team composition or solve a named obstacle, not as a nightly routine.
3. Reassess the book holder, protection, and vote count before each later attack.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Archmage_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Retrain for a specific team need

**Best when:** The current composition cannot solve a named obstacle, or an ally has agreed that replacing their ability is worth losing this ordinary kill.

**Action:** Use Retrain on yourself or an agreeing ally; choose an available legal Coven role that solves that problem.

**Target rule:** Name the missing utility first. Another ally can receive only an eligible Common Coven role; self-Retrain has broader valid choices.

**Why:** A deliberate role change can add lasting utility, while a random replacement can remove the very ability the team needed.

**Change plan when:** If the desired role is unavailable, the ally declines, or a useful kill matters more tonight, return to the attack plan.

**Tradeoff:** Retrain and the ordinary book attack are alternative actions. Do not promise both; do not silently replace an ally’s useful role.

**Checkpoints:**

- Agree which ability the team needs
- Check the offered replacement is legal
- Record which role will actually act next night

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Verify the role change actually resolved
2. Build the next team plan around the replacement
3. Update the public cover when future movement changes

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Archmage_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is the simplest direct-book visiting story. Oracle fits genuinely nonvisiting periods instead. The lower claims add invented investigative work or harder protection promises; none receives a real Town ability from Archmage. Retrain can change your future actions, so the current route matters more than a permanent universal ranking.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Low upkeep · direct-book footprint

A book attack and Barrier can share one selected living-player destination; this supports the visit, not the protection.

- On a direct book night, keep the claimed Barrier destination consistent with your real attack destination; do not move it to a convenient survivor after dawn.
- Say TP under alignment claims and give the exact Cleric history when requested; a routine target line does not require an invented saved message.
- If your target dies, examine the actual cause, obstacles, and feedback. Do not claim they were saved simply because you intended to protect them.
- Retrain is not Barrier. Reassess the cover before replacing an attack night with a role change or after your own role changes.

**Best when:** You expect to perform normal direct book attacks and want a short, stable target history.

**Exposure/limits:** You supply no Barrier. A dead target, direct protective retaliation, or later no-visit Retrain can contradict the history.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Archmage_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Oracle (TP) — Conditional · nonvisiting nights

An idle nonbook period can fit the absence of a normal player visit from Oracle Aegis.

- Write a Town role choice such as Aegis Sheriff, not a player number. Archmage does not learn or protect holders of that role.
- Do not claim consecutive identical Aegis choices; Oracle itself is a once-only choice.
- A quiet night or no detected movement is compatible with this story but is not independent evidence of a save.
- Before accepting book duty, check whether a new visible attack would contradict the already public Oracle history.

**Best when:** You are genuinely not making direct visits; this is much weaker during the ordinary book-attack default.

**Exposure/limits:** A direct book visit cannot be explained by astral role-targeted Aegis, and no real protection exists.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Archmage_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Sheriff (TI) — More pressure · invented checks

On direct attack nights your selected destination can match one Sheriff check, and team knowledge can inform a restrained case.

- Commit to the visited player before dawn; a book attack on 4 does not support a will claiming you checked 9.
- Post the Sheriff-shaped report early from D2 rather than waiting to copy every claim. An actual held night cannot magically create a check.
- Knowing a teammate is Coven does not guarantee Suspicious: the book holder and Illusion can alter that result, and many enemies are Innocent.
- Use one limited claim about suspicion, not an exact-role announcement; if movement evidence fails, a stronger accusation does not repair it.

**Best when:** The team needs discussion influence and you can maintain early daily reporting without exposing secret information.

**Exposure/limits:** An attack produces no Innocent or Suspicious feedback. Team role knowledge can be stale or altered, and earlier nonvisiting nights may not fit.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Archmage_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Psychic (TI) — More influence · higher upkeep

One direct book destination can serve as the chosen player in a claimed vision.

- Include your actual selected destination in each claimed group instead of choosing an unrelated supposedly checked player after dawn.
- N1 uses a Good pair; N2 uses an Evil trio. Keep the chosen target labeled so a Tracker account can be compared.
- Post near day start and discuss at-least-one group logic; do not label one member confirmed Town or evil from the group alone.
- A Retrain or idle night is not a genuine vision. If future movement no longer fits, do not silently rewrite old groups.

**Best when:** You are making compatible direct visits and accept the ongoing daily group-report obligations.

**Exposure/limits:** Archmage supplies no vision. Parity, group size, early posting, deaths, and a missing visit can each expose an invented group.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Archmage_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Bodyguard (TP) — Conditional fallback · no real interception

A normal direct book target can match the house in a Guard line.

- Use the same living-player destination as the real direct attack; Guard is a visiting action, not a role-wide Aegis.
- Do not report a successful interception without evidence of that event. Surviving at a quiet house is weak support.
- Bodyguard guards against one direct harmful visit, not every passive or astral attack. Explain a death from actual mechanics rather than claiming universal protection.
- Two Self Protect uses are the limit for a Bodyguard story; repeated no-visit Archmage nights cannot all become unlimited self-shields.

**Best when:** You understand interception and need a visiting TP alternative when Cleric is less suitable.

**Exposure/limits:** You neither cancel a harmful visitor nor perform the Bodyguard counterattack; a protected-house death is stronger pressure than a quiet target list.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Archmage_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

## Hex Master

CPow — Coven Power.

### Route 1: Hex a likely survivor the team is not killing. (recommended)

**Best when:** You are Hexing without the book and can mark a living opponent the team expects to leave alive.

**Action:** Without the book, Hex a useful unmarked nonally expected to remain alive. Avoid duplicating the agreed Coven kill if another eligible target exists.

**Target rule:** Use an unpressured eligible nonally when there is no D1 evidence. Prefer a player likely to live over someone already headed for execution.

**Why:** Your final threat needs living opponents Hexed; a mark on someone who immediately dies usually buys less progress.

**Change plan when:** With the book, the Hex also attacks and is astral: choose kill value and reconsider any cover that claimed an ordinary visit.

**Tradeoff:** Every death can change the completion count. A successful mark on tonight’s kill usually contributes less lasting progress.

**Checkpoints:**

- Mark a likely survivor
- Track live progress rather than attempts
- Reassess cover and targeting with the book

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Hex useful living unmarked opponents rather than revisiting completed progress.
2. Coordinate kills so they do not unnecessarily erase the mark you needed to retain.
3. Near completion, identify the remaining unhexed opponents and preserve your survival through the vote.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Hex_Master_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Use the astral book attack

**Best when:** You hold the book and the team needs a kill more than slow progress on surviving Hex targets.

**Action:** Choose the best reachable enemy for the combined Hex and book attack.

**Target rule:** An exposed useful enemy with direct-visit protection can be attractive because this attack is astral; Powerful Defense can still stop its Basic damage.

**Why:** Astral targeting avoids ordinary visitor interception, so it can solve a kill route a direct attacker cannot safely use.

**Change plan when:** If the target survives and remains Hexed, update the live count; do not count an attack attempt as a guaranteed kill.

**Tradeoff:** The action no longer looks like an ordinary visiting Cleric or Psychic. Astral does not mean stronger attack or immunity to every investigation.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm you hold the book
- Separate defense from visit interception
- Recount living unhexed enemies

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use astral access against suitable protected houses
2. Preserve survival if the Hex Bomb is near
3. Reassess the remaining unhexed player after each death

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Hex_Master_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is the low-upkeep pre-book default; Psychic trades daily vision work for more discussion influence. With the astral book action, Oracle may fit movement better, but an earlier visiting history still needs explaining.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Low upkeep · pre-book visit

A normal pre-book Hex can share a claimed Barrier destination, with no daily invented investigative result.

- Use the same player in the public Barrier line as the actual ordinary Hex destination so movement evidence has a plausible explanation.
- Choose Hex targets for living mark progress, not merely to make a protective will look fashionable.
- Answer TP under alignment claims. Do not invent saved feedback or imply a quiet target proves your protection worked.
- When the book arrives, recheck this cover before claiming another ordinary visit; the new attack is astral rather than normal Barrier movement.

**Best when:** You are actively Hexing without the book and prefer a short TP account.

**Exposure/limits:** Hex gives no defense; the book makes the real action astral, so the ordinary visit resemblance then disappears.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Hex_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Psychic (TI) — More influence · pre-book visit

One ordinary Hex destination can be the selected player in a claimed Psychic vision.

- Include the real Hex destination as the chosen player in the claimed group; a visible visit to 4 cannot be explained by a selected target of 9.
- Keep odd-night Good pairs and even-night Evil trios, and preserve old names when later deaths change what Town knows.
- Post near day start and use at-least-one wording instead of treating a chosen Hex victim as mechanically proven evil.
- Track living Hex progress privately; publishing those marks or a sudden exact-role read leaks information Psychic cannot supply.
- Do not keep claiming ordinary Psychic visits on astral book nights without accounting for movement contradictions.

**Best when:** Your pre-book Hex route visits normally and you want more day influence than a quiet TP claim.

**Exposure/limits:** Hex provides no vision; fabricated parity or groups are vulnerable, and the book removes the normal visit footprint.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Hex_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Oracle (TP) — Conditional · astral book route

With the book, your Hex attack is astral, like Oracle’s lack of an ordinary visible player visit.

- Name a Town role for Aegis rather than the player you attacked; role choices and your actual kill destination are different targeting methods.
- Rotate the named role on consecutive nights and reserve the one Oracle self-role choice.
- Do not turn an astral kill’s success into claimed save feedback or pretend you learned the selected role’s holder.
- Review any earlier ordinary Hex visits before choosing this cover. A later astral action does not erase earlier direct movement.

**Best when:** You began with the book or already have a history that genuinely lacks ordinary visits.

**Exposure/limits:** You create no Aegis, and prior direct Hex visits can contradict a lifelong Oracle story.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Hex_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Sheriff (TI) — Pressure fallback · invented checks

An ordinary pre-book Hex destination can also be the subject of a one-player Sheriff report.

- Match the checked number to the actual ordinary Hex destination before posting.
- Use Innocent or Suspicious as a limited report; Hex status does not tell you which Sheriff result a player should have.
- Post early enough to contribute an independent story, rather than changing the result after the target claims.
- An astral book night is not an ordinary Sheriff visit. Keep the mark plan and any invented investigation separate.

**Best when:** You need a focused accusation and accept the daily reporting and pre-book movement constraints.

**Exposure/limits:** Hex does not reveal suspicion; astral book movement and known Sheriff detection exceptions can defeat the report.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Hex_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Investigator (TI) — Harder fallback · crime bookkeeping

A pre-book direct Hex can match the destination of one Investigate action.

- Keep the investigated number aligned with the ordinary Hex visit; this does not work as a simple movement match after the book turns your action astral.
- Do not write Murder because a player is generally suspicious or killed on a previous night.
- A nonvisiting or purely astral action cannot support a casual Trespassing accusation. Ask for the target’s history rather than assuming evil equals crime.
- Post a limited crime report early; do not leak the private living-Hex count as though Investigator knows marks.

**Best when:** You can explain the crime vocabulary and have useful team evidence without pretending it is an actual result.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no crime result; Murder is night-specific and Trespassing depends on cross-faction direct visits, not your Hex mark.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Hex_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Investigator_(ToS2)).

## Witch

CPow — Coven Power.

### Route 1: Control a useful enemy action; keep it away from Coven. (recommended)

**Best when:** You lack a confirmed role to exploit, so information plus a safe nonally destination has the best general value.

**Action:** Control a suspected useful nonally and send their action to another nonally after checking the team plan.

**Target rule:** With no role evidence, use eligible unknown nonallies for both selections and avoid likely Veteran houses or an allied kill plan. Only the first selected player is your personal visit.

**Why:** You can redirect value and learn useful role information, but an uncontrolled destination can sabotage your own faction.

**Change plan when:** If you know an enemy killing or protective role, use that specific information to create a better destination. Respect consecutive-target restrictions and actual immunity feedback.

**Tradeoff:** The controlled player knows Control happened. Hangover blocks consecutive Control, and the second selection is their destination, not a second personal visit.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose both numbers deliberately
- Separate personal movement from redirection
- Use learned role information in the next team plan

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Exploit the role learned from Control to redirect the highest-impact legal enemy action.
2. Keep dangerous controlled actions away from allies and useful team targets.
3. Recheck target legality, book effects, and the personal-visit story your cover must support.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Witch_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Redirect a known protector

**Best when:** You have reliable reason to think an enemy has useful targeted protection and want to divert it from the team’s intended victim.

**Action:** Control that protector toward an eligible ally or another destination the team wants protected.

**Target rule:** Keep the controlled player as your first selection. Pick the second destination for the actual protective ability, including restrictions and visitor danger.

**Why:** This can both strip the enemy’s intended protection and use the displaced protection for Coven.

**Change plan when:** Change the plan if they are Hungover, immune, out of uses, or the redirected action did not resolve.

**Tradeoff:** Some abilities cannot be forced and special-button behavior differs. A protected ally is not automatically saved; the controlled player can report being Witched.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify the actual protective ability
- Coordinate the team kill destination
- Keep first target and redirected destination separate

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Rotate around Hangover
2. Use learned roles to choose valuable redirections
3. Do not expose extra role knowledge in a cover will

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Witch_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Exploit a known dangerous visitor

**Best when:** You know an enemy’s usable attack or other consequential visit, and have a specific destination where it benefits Coven.

**Action:** Control that enemy toward a useful nonally; coordinate a Jinx trap only if that visitor and house create a sensible ambush.

**Target rule:** Never direct a dangerous action into an ally for convenience. Check that the first target can actually perform the action tonight.

**Why:** A known ability is more valuable than random redirection because you can predict what the forced destination changes.

**Change plan when:** If the target cannot act, is immune, or is Hungover, choose a different eligible actor rather than repeating the same Control.

**Tradeoff:** A Werewolf or Serial Killer interaction can be lethal. Your personal footprint is still the first selected player; do not claim two Seer visits.

**Checkpoints:**

- Check action and charge availability
- Choose a safe destination for Coven
- Record success versus mere selection

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Exploit confirmed role knowledge
2. Share genuine stolen feedback privately
3. Reassess book damage against the first target

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Witch_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is simpler to maintain, but Control is a distinctive effect. Sheriff can use learned role knowledge for a more informed invented story; Psychic creates more daily obligations. You personally visit only the controlled player, not both selected numbers.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Low upkeep · conspicuous real effect

Your Control visit to the first selected player can share one Barrier destination. The second selection is not your personal visit.

- If you Control 4 into 9, the movement-compatible Barrier line is on 4, not 9. Your public will cannot claim two protective visits.
- Say TP under alignment claims and keep ordinary target continuity; do not invent a save because the controlled player survived.
- Separate the controlled role and stolen feedback into private notes. Cleric has no exact-role reveal or access to the target’s results.
- Expect the controlled player to report being Witched. A matching visit does not hide that event, especially if independent movement evidence links you.

**Best when:** You want minimal daily writing and understand this fits movement only, not the distinctive effect.

**Exposure/limits:** The target receives Control feedback rather than protection. A reported Witch event correlated with your visit is a serious contradiction.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Witch_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Sheriff (TI) — Informed case · still invented result

You learn the controlled player’s role, which can inform a plausible limited Sheriff-shaped story about that first target.

- The checked number must be your first selected controlled player. The forced destination is not a second Sheriff check.
- Use learned role information cautiously: neutral killers, book holders, Illusion, and frames prevent simple Town-equals-Innocent or evil-equals-Suspicious rules.
- Post the limited report early and do not announce the exact role merely because Witch learned it.
- Do not claim a completed check from failed actions without actual support; a Control notification plus your visible visit can still expose you.

**Best when:** A useful first-target read supports a restrained case and you accept the exposure from the Control event.

**Exposure/limits:** Role knowledge is not Sheriff feedback. Control is announced to the target, and detection exceptions or framing can change the expected result.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Witch_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Tracker (TI) — Conditional · known forced destination

After a successful legal Control, you know where the controlled first target was directed, which can support a narrow outgoing-movement account.

- A Tracker-shaped line is 4 to 9 when you personally visited 4 and successfully sent their direct action to 9.
- Do not write the destination from button selection alone. Confirm Control worked and account for the target’s actual visit type.
- Do not claim a full two-target destination list for Seer-like roles when Control affected only one selection, or invent the portion you did not learn.
- Post near day start, keep role knowledge and stolen results private, and expect the target’s Control notification to make this a conditional rather than clean disguise.

**Best when:** Control actually resolved on a simple direct visitor and you can distinguish the intended destination from observed or reliable feedback.

**Exposure/limits:** Immunity, Hangover, failed movement, astral actions, or a second visit can make the selected destination differ from a complete Tracker result.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Witch_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tracker_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Psychic (TI) — More influence · conspicuous visit

Your first target can fit the selected player in one normal Psychic vision.

- Place the controlled first target in the vision group and label that player as selected; do not use both Witch selections as two personal visits.
- Maintain N1 Good pairs and N2 Evil trios. The learned exact role does not supply the other names or guarantee apparent alignment.
- Post near day start and distinguish at least one from an exact culprit; avoid leaking stolen feedback that no Psychic could see.
- Reconsider the story when the controlled player or a Tracker links you to the Witch event. More invented group detail does not repair direct exposure.

**Best when:** You want group-based discussion and can maintain a valid daily format despite the obvious Control effect.

**Exposure/limits:** The real action supplies no Psychic group, and a surviving controlled target can link your visit to Witch feedback.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Witch_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Investigator (TI) — Harder case · partial role knowledge

The controlled first target and some stolen action feedback may inform a cautious crime story about the house you actually visited.

- Use the first selected player as the investigated target. Sending them elsewhere does not mean you personally investigated that destination.
