- A known killing role has Murder only when the relevant attack occurred that night; having a weapon is not sufficient.
- Treat cross-faction visiting history separately from an attack. A single stolen result does not reveal every earlier Trespassing interaction.
- Post a limited report without exact-role leaks and accept that the visible Control event remains a stronger counterclaim than a convenient crime narrative.

**Best when:** You understand Murder versus cumulative Trespassing and can avoid turning exact-role knowledge into an impossible crime result.

**Exposure/limits:** You do not receive an Investigator report. Control changes that night’s movement, and the notified victim can expose the connection.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Witch_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Investigator_(ToS2)).

## Conjurer

CK — Coven Killing.

### Route 1: Save Meteor for a valuable exposed opponent. (recommended)

**Best when:** You do not hold the book. There is no separate night ability, so your meaningful choice is the later Meteor timing.

**Action:** Without the book you have no ordinary N1 attack; prepare a cover and save the later day Meteor. With the book, coordinate the enabled team kill.

**Target rule:** No N1 target is required without the book. Watch for a supported leader or dangerous investigator; do not choose a future Meteor target from player number alone.

**Why:** The day kill is most valuable when it removes a real threat or changes voting control, not when spent to look active.

**Change plan when:** If the day vote is about to remove a teammate or enemy voting power is decisive, Meteor timing can become urgent. It is unavailable on D1.

**Tradeoff:** Meteor has one use, a distinctive public death event, and no D1 use. It cannot demonstrate a Deputy shot.

**Checkpoints:**

- Keep day and night actions distinct
- Identify a useful later Meteor target
- Track the single day charge

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Meteor an exposed high-value enemy when removing them changes the day outcome.
2. Coordinate the day kill with the team’s vote and night plan rather than duplicating an inevitable execution.
3. After the one charge is spent, maintain the cover and use any actual book action.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Conjurer_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Take an enabled book kill

**Best when:** You actually hold the Necronomicon and the team has assigned you its night attack.

**Action:** Coordinate a Basic attack on one useful nonally while retaining the unused day Meteor.

**Target rule:** Choose a reachable enemy rather than a likely direct-visit trap. Grace Period can make an N1 attack unavailable.

**Why:** Using the book supplies real night kill value without spending the separate one-use day resource.

**Change plan when:** If the night action is disabled, hold it; reassess the Meteor at the next legal day window instead.

**Tradeoff:** A night book kill is a direct visit. You cannot retroactively present Meteor as a normal Deputy shot or count night protection as daytime safety.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm book ownership
- Agree the target with Coven
- Keep the day charge separate

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use Meteor when it changes the day outcome
2. Avoid duplicating an inevitable execution
3. Update a nonvisiting cover before later visible attacks

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Conjurer_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Oracle fits a genuinely inactive nonbook night with fewer daily result obligations. Cleric becomes a visiting fallback with the book. Deputy looks simple on paper but creates a day-shot demand Meteor cannot satisfy.

#### 1. Oracle (TP) — Low upkeep · inactive night fit

A nonbook Conjurer has no normal night visit, which can fit Oracle’s astral, role-targeted action footprint.

- Write a legal Town role choice, such as Aegis Sheriff, rather than pretending to protect a numbered player.
- Respect consecutive-role restrictions and the one Oracle self-role choice. No movement is compatible with this cover but does not prove it.
- Keep the actual Meteor charge and intended victim private; Oracle has no day attack or exact-role feedback.
- Before taking the book, reassess whether the visible attack will contradict the Oracle history. Do not write a saved message simply because your chosen role survived.

**Best when:** You are genuinely inactive at night without the book and want fewer fabricated daily results.

**Exposure/limits:** There is no Aegis, and later direct book attacks conflict with the no-visit story. Meteor cannot be passed off as protection.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Conjurer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Cleric (TP) — Conditional · book-attack visit

When you actually make a direct book attack, its destination can match one claimed Barrier target.

- Match each claimed Barrier target to the actual direct book destination rather than another player selected after dawn.
- Keep truly idle nonbook nights separate; they cannot all be represented as completed normal player visits.
- Answer TP under alignment claims. Write actual-compatible target history without inventing save feedback.
- Meteor is a separate distinctive day event, not a Cleric action. Choosing this cover does not explain its occurrence or any later direct kill contradictions.

**Best when:** You expect ordinary book visits and can explain the existing movement history.

**Exposure/limits:** Nonbook idle nights supply no ordinary Barrier visit. Book damage does not provide protection and can kill the person supposedly guarded.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Conjurer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Deputy (TK) — Little writing · major demonstration risk

Both roles have no normal nonbook night action and an important day resource, so the empty-night schedule looks superficially similar.

- Use a simple no-night-action history; do not invent nightly investigations for Deputy.
- A successful Meteor must not be presented as a successful Deputy shot. The public animation and death event distinguish them.
- Do not rely on unlimited excuses that every requested shot missed. Deputy has one charge and defense or Town status can explain a miss, not prove the claim.
- If Town demands demonstration, recognize that you lack the promised ability. Reassess the team’s day plan instead of treating the two day attacks as interchangeable.

**Best when:** You accept a short-lived or contested no-night-action story rather than expecting long-term proof.

**Exposure/limits:** Meteor has a different public event and cannot demonstrate Deputy’s shot. A private claimed miss is not independent role evidence.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Conjurer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Sheriff (TI) — Pressure fallback · book nights only

A direct book target can match a single Sheriff check when the actual night route visits normally.

- Set the claimed checked number to the real book destination before the result of the night is known.
- Post early with Innocent or Suspicious as limited vocabulary; avoid exact-role claims from team secrets.
- Account for failed or held nights explicitly. An empty Conjurer night does not produce a completed Sheriff check.
- Use known detection exceptions and preserve old reports. A new Meteor death cannot retroactively validate an invented Sheriff result.

**Best when:** You are taking book duty and have a reason to accept early daily reporting instead of a quiet TP story.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no suspicion result and cannot perform a Sheriff visit during a genuinely idle night.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Conjurer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Psychic (TI) — High upkeep · visiting book route

A normal book destination can fit the chosen player in a Psychic group.

- Include the book destination as the selected player, while preserving the claimed group’s original names.
- Use N1 Good pairs and N2 Evil trios, posting near day start rather than constructing the list after all claims.
- Do not claim a vision from an actual nonvisiting inactive night as though the movement match exists.
- Keep the Meteor plan separate. Psychic has neither exact-role knowledge nor a daytime kill, and your will cannot manufacture either.

**Best when:** Your actual route visits and the team wants more daily group influence despite higher maintenance.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no vision, and nonbook idle nights or a distinctive Meteor do not supply Psychic evidence.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Conjurer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

## Ritualist

CK — Coven Killing.

### Route 1: Hold guesses until you have a supported exact role. (recommended)

**Best when:** There is no well-supported exact legal role, so preserve both scarce Ritual attempts.

**Action:** On a low-information N1, preserve your two Ritual attempts. Use a legal exact-role guess only when the evidence is unusually strong.

**Target rule:** Look for a supported exact non-TI claim. With no reliable role evidence, hold. TI and Illuminated targets are not legal Ritual targets.

**Why:** A random guess wastes a scarce chance; role knowledge is more valuable than merely having an available button.

**Change plan when:** Team role information may make a guess worthwhile, but check Illumination first. If you hold the book, weigh its alternative attack rather than assuming both actions happen.

**Tradeoff:** Wrong guesses still spend a charge. TI and Illuminated players are ineligible, and an alignment such as TP is not an exact role.

**Checkpoints:**

- Preserve both attempts without evidence
- Confirm guess eligibility
- Spend a guess on a supported exact role

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use a well-supported legal exact role guess against a valuable enemy.
2. Preserve remaining attempts when the role or eligibility is uncertain.
3. Coordinate around Illumination and book ownership; do not guess from a role-family claim alone.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Ritualist_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Spend a supported Ritual

**Best when:** A valuable non-TI target has a reliable exact role and is not currently Illuminated.

**Action:** Use Blood Ritual with that exact role after checking eligibility and the team’s plan.

**Target rule:** Prefer a verified or strongly supported exposed enemy. Do not substitute a role-family guess or stale role information after a possible change.

**Why:** A correct legal Ritual is an astral Unstoppable attack, which can solve protection a Basic book attack cannot.

**Change plan when:** If Illumination or uncertainty appears, keep the attempt and use a different legal plan.

**Tradeoff:** Even a correct guess cannot kill Invincible Defense. A wrong guess creates no usable result and still costs a charge.

**Checkpoints:**

- Verify exact role rather than alignment
- Check Illumination
- Record remaining attempts

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Spend only on supported eligible enemies
2. Recheck role changes before reusing information
3. Keep the unused book alternative distinct

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Ritualist_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Use the book instead of guessing

**Best when:** You hold the book but lack a good legal Ritual guess, or a Basic attack is sufficient.

**Action:** Select the ordinary book attack against a useful reachable enemy.

**Target rule:** Check direct-visit protection and choose a target whose death helps the team; do not guess just to use the more unusual button.

**Why:** A coordinated Basic attack can advance Coven while preserving both Ritual attempts for better role information.

**Change plan when:** If a high-confidence protected legal target emerges, compare an actual Ritual before submitting the final action.

**Tradeoff:** The book attack is an alternative to Blood Ritual and creates a normal visit. Do not claim both resolved that night.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose one actual night mode
- Check the direct destination
- Preserve the Ritual count

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Reserve Rituals for exact-role opportunities
2. Track book ownership and visit evidence
3. Reassess the cover when switching from astral to direct action

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Ritualist_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Oracle is my lower-drama astral fallback. Deputy has little writing but severe demonstration pressure. A direct book attack can break a no-visible-visit history, so reassess rather than calling either permanent cover.

#### 1. Oracle (TP) — Low upkeep · astral or held nights

Blood Ritual is astral, and a held night also lacks an ordinary visible player visit, like Oracle’s footprint.

- Choose a Town role in the Aegis line, not the numbered Ritual victim. Oracle protects role holders without identifying them.
- Alternate roles on consecutive nights and use the Oracle self-role choice at most once.
- Do not convert exact-role guess knowledge into a claimed Oracle investigation. Quiet survival is not proof of protection.
- Reassess the no-visit account before choosing the book alternative; that direct attack changes the movement footprint.

**Best when:** You are holding scarce guesses or using actual astral Rituals rather than direct book attacks.

**Exposure/limits:** Neither produces Aegis. A direct book attack, a repeated role choice, or invented save feedback can contradict the story.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Ritualist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Deputy (TK) — Low writing · proof problem

A Ritualist can have a genuinely quiet night without an ordinary visit, resembling Deputy’s night inactivity.

- Keep the public history about a daytime ability, not invented night checks or a Ritual presented as a Deputy shot.
- A successful night Ritual does not spend or demonstrate the claimed Deputy day charge.
- Deputy has one real shot; do not build repeated alleged misses into a believable routine.
- If Town asks for a shot, you lack that button. Choose the real team plan with that limitation in mind rather than claiming the cover is confirmed.

**Best when:** You need a sparse history and accept demonstration pressure as a significant cost.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot supply a real day shot. Ritual kills are nighttime events with distinct mechanics, and a claimed miss proves nothing.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Ritualist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Cleric (TP) — Conditional · direct book mode

A normal direct book attack can share a reported Barrier destination.

- Use the actual book target as the claimed Barrier destination rather than the victim of a separate astral Ritual.
- Keep the real mode selected that night in private notes; you cannot claim ordinary Cleric movement from every Ritual or hold.
- Answer TP under alignment claims and avoid fictional saved feedback. A routine target list has low writing cost and little independent proof.
- If you return to Blood Ritual, reassess the movement story before presenting another ordinary visit.

**Best when:** You are taking direct book duty and have not already committed to an incompatible no-visit account.

**Exposure/limits:** A held or astral Ritual night is not a normal Cleric visit, and no actual Barrier is supplied.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Ritualist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Sheriff (TI) — Harder fallback · direct book mode

A direct book destination can also be the target in a one-person Sheriff line.

- Tie the reported check to the actual direct book visit; do not treat a guessed exact role as a genuine Sheriff investigation.
- Post early and use only Innocent or Suspicious. Exact roles, the book, Illusion, and framing complicate expected suspicion.
- Do not fabricate a visit for a quiet hold or an astral Ritual. Those modes are why this is a lower conditional choice.
- If secret team information motivates a public accusation, make sure the claimed role could plausibly support the limited statement rather than exposing the Ritual plan.

**Best when:** You perform direct book visits and accept a discussion-oriented invented check history.

**Exposure/limits:** A successful Ritual guess reveals no Sheriff result, and the usual held or astral night has the wrong movement footprint.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Ritualist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Psychic (TI) — Harder fallback · direct book mode

A book visit can provide the chosen destination for a claimed Psychic vision.

- Use the direct book destination as the selected vision member. Do not build the group around an unrelated astral Ritual victim.
- Respect N1 Good pairs and N2 Evil trios, preserving the selected target and old groups.
- Post near day start and discuss group constraints, not exact-role knowledge from a private Ritual plan.
- When switching back to a held or astral mode, acknowledge that the movement resemblance ends; a valid-looking group cannot fix that.

**Best when:** You actually visit with the book and want group influence despite the added upkeep.

**Exposure/limits:** Rituals and holds do not provide normal Psychic visits or real groups; the role requires fresh early daily reports.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Ritualist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

## Jinx

CK — Coven Killing.

### Route 1: Ambush planned enemy traffic; otherwise a hold can beat a random trap. (recommended)

**Best when:** You can predict useful enemy visitor traffic without creating a crowd of surviving witnesses; otherwise a nonbook hold is reasonable.

**Action:** Jinx a house likely to draw useful non-Coven visitors after coordinating the team’s traffic plan. Without a useful traffic read, hold rather than trap an arbitrary house.

**Target rule:** Prefer a nonally house with a concrete reason for enemy traffic. An unknown house is not automatically good; an obvious crowded call can also contain dangerous visitors.

**Why:** A visitor ambush can remove one non-Coven direct visitor, but other surviving direct visitors may learn your identity. Coven allies are normally excluded from the visitor attack.

**Change plan when:** If the likely traffic gives no useful non-Coven victim or leaves witnesses who can expose you, choose another house or hold. Check Grace Period and the book before planning damage.

**Tradeoff:** The passive attack hits one eligible non-Coven direct visitor, not every visitor, and surviving direct visitors can identify you. Normal Coven allies are excluded; Town Traitor is an exception.

**Checkpoints:**

- Plan around visitor traffic
- Limit surviving witnesses
- Reassess the cover after actual exposure

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Choose houses whose expected visitors are valuable enemies, with team traffic kept clear.
2. After any survivor exposure, prioritize a realistic survival/kill plan rather than repeating a supposedly safe cover.
3. Weigh an enabled book attack against the visitor ambush as the remaining player count shrinks.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jinx_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Coordinate one predictable visitor

**Best when:** A Witch knows a useful eligible enemy visitor and can legally redirect them into your chosen house.

**Action:** Agree a house and a controlled enemy visitor, then Jinx that house without adding unnecessary allied or public traffic.

**Target rule:** Choose a non-Coven resident and a visitor worth attacking. Both players must have a legal, working action; do not treat a control attempt as certainty.

**Why:** One planned enemy visitor can be a better ambush than hoping a crowded protection call contains the right target.

**Change plan when:** If the Witch target is Hungover, immune, protected, or cannot make the visit, choose a different route or hold.

**Tradeoff:** The visitor can survive Basic damage and identify you. Coven visitors are normally spared by the passive attack, but their presence does not make the cover believable.

**Checkpoints:**

- Agree on the same house
- Check the expected visitor can move
- Plan for a witness who survives

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Prefer predicted enemy traffic over random houses
2. Reassess immediately after identity exposure
3. Coordinate the book and remaining visitor value

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jinx_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Witch_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Prioritize the book’s direct kill

**Best when:** You hold the book and a useful target kill matters even if no visitor ambush occurs.

**Action:** Attack the chosen nonally with the book; treat the possible visitor attack as an additional conditional benefit.

**Target rule:** Pick a reachable enemy first. Add visitor value only when there is evidence, rather than sacrificing the important kill for speculative traffic.

**Why:** Late in a game, a reliable direct kill can matter more than an ambush that nobody visits.

**Change plan when:** If direct protection makes the kill poor or surviving witnesses will decide the next vote, compare another target with the team.

**Tradeoff:** Book attacks can trigger direct protective retaliation. A Jinx death label or witness is not explained away by calling yourself Crusader.

**Checkpoints:**

- Separate direct and visitor attacks
- Check direct interception risks
- Plan the next day if identified

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use actual kill feedback to update the plan
2. Avoid calling the resident protected
3. Spend survival and vote control deliberately after exposure

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jinx_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is simpler paperwork but only fits a real direct visit. Crusader explains visitor danger better while promising protection Jinx never grants. Psychic and Sheriff add invented investigations; Lookout is a difficult witness-based option, not a real visitor-list ability. Normal Coven visitors are spared by Jinx’s passive attack, but surviving non-Coven direct visitors can identify you.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Low upkeep · weak effect support

A Jinx destination can match one Barrier destination when you really visit. This is a movement overlap rather than protective support.

- Keep the claimed Barrier target equal to the house you actually Jinxed. If you held, do not pretend this created an ordinary visit.
- Answer TP under alignment claims. A plain target line is enough for a routine entry; do not manufacture a saved message.
- Do not call a Jinx visitor death evidence that Cleric worked. Cleric neither attacks visitors nor receives a full visitor list.
- If the resident dies, examine the actual attack and protection story instead of switching targets after dawn.
- A surviving direct visitor may name you as Jinx. Treat that as direct exposure requiring a realistic team survival plan, not something extra will detail repairs.

**Best when:** You are visiting and want a short account without inventing daily investigation results.

**Exposure/limits:** Jinx provides no defense. Distinctive deaths or a surviving witness can identify you, and a held night does not match a completed Barrier visit.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jinx_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Crusader (TP) — Partial effect fit · serious limits

Both create a dangerous house for visitors, so a visitor attack is closer to Crusader than to an ordinary Cleric story.

- Record the actual chosen house as the Fortify destination; a real held night has no ordinary visit to support that line.
- Separate resident protection from the visitor attack. Jinx can support the idea of visitor danger, never the promised Powerful Defense.
- Do not claim you know a complete visitor list or which visitor was hit unless reliable feedback or public evidence supplies it.
- Crusader can hit friendly Town visitors; Jinx normally excludes Coven visitors but can hit a Town Traitor. These are different eligibility rules.
- If the resident dies or a survivor names you as Jinx, do not double down by declaring that every visitor should have died. One attack is not a house wipe.

**Best when:** Actual visitor danger is relevant and you understand why the resident is not genuinely Fortified.

**Exposure/limits:** Jinx grants no resident defense, targets one eligible non-Coven visitor, and can reveal its identity to surviving witnesses. Death attribution can differ.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jinx_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Crusader_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Psychic (TI) — More influence · invented groups

Your chosen Jinx house can be the selected destination of a normal one-target vision.

- Include the house you actually Jinxed as the chosen member; do not create a different selected visit after seeing who died.
- Write an odd-night Good pair and an even-night Evil trio, then post near day start.
- Discuss at-least-one constraints rather than claiming the visitor kill reveals which vision member is evil.
- Keep attack feedback and any witness information out of the Psychic result. Psychic does not learn who visited or whom Jinx hit.
- If you held the Jinx action, the expected ordinary Psychic visit is absent. If a witness identifies you, group plausibility cannot erase it.

**Best when:** You want day influence and can maintain a real direct visit plus early daily group reports.

**Exposure/limits:** You have no genuine vision, and identity witnesses or a distinct Jinx death can expose you independently of a valid-looking group.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jinx_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Sheriff (TI) — Pressure fallback · no real check

An actual Jinx visit can fit the destination of a simple Sheriff check.

- Check the same house you actually visited in the public account; do not name the attacked visitor as though you visited them personally.
- Post the limited Sheriff report early. Not attacking a visitor tells you nothing about the resident’s suspicion result.
- Do not upgrade a Jinx victim or surviving visitor into an exact-role or faction reveal. Public deaths can inform a deduction, not a fabricated mechanical certainty.
- Keep held nights and exposure separate. A surviving visitor who names you as Jinx is a stronger problem than whether the Sheriff line looks tidy.

**Best when:** You prefer a focused accusation to maintaining Psychic groups and accept invented-result risk.

**Exposure/limits:** Jinx attack feedback is not Innocent or Suspicious; a witness or wrong selected visit can directly contradict the cover.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jinx_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Lookout (TI) — Difficult · witness evidence only

A Jinx visit puts you at the house a Lookout would watch, and occasional independent evidence may identify particular visitors.

- Use the actual Jinx house as the watched house; normal Lookout does not watch itself.
- Only reliable evidence can support a named visitor. Knowing that one visitor was attacked does not reveal every visitor or the attacker’s victim identity.
- A normal Lookout report is a list of visible visitor identities, not their exact roles. Avoid turning the known Coven roster into a supposedly complete observation.
- If another visitor contradicts the list or directly identifies you, the information gap is real. This is why the option ranks below simpler covers.

**Best when:** You already have reliable independent visitor identities and understand this is a narrow conditional story rather than a natural information fit.

**Exposure/limits:** Jinx does not supply a full visitor list. Unseen visitors, astral actions, and survivors identifying Jinx make an exhaustive invented list especially fragile.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jinx_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Lookout_(ToS2)).

## Dreamweaver

CD — Coven Deception.

### Route 1: Dreamweave an isolated useful Town target. (recommended)

**Best when:** A useful Town target seems relatively unlikely to receive a Town visit on the following night.

**Action:** Without the book, Dreamweave a likely useful Town opponent who is less likely to receive a Town visit next night.

**Target rule:** Prefer an isolated claim with real night value. With no evidence, choose an eligible nonally and accept that faction eligibility or a later cure can stop progress.

**Why:** Your effect needs time to develop; a target routinely visited by Town is more likely to be cured before the payoff.

**Change plan when:** If the target is a crowded protection hub, choose a less visited opponent. With the book, prioritize the actual attack plan and retain the earlier pending-state record.

**Tradeoff:** Dreamwoven is a pending effect, not immediate Insomnia. A later Town cure, your death, or Retrain can remove the disruption.

**Checkpoints:**

- Apply a legal Dreamweave
- Track cure versus progression
- Use state changes without inventing certainty

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Apply the effect where the next-night Town cure is less likely.
2. Track cured versus progressed targets rather than assuming every weave became Insomnia.
3. Use the book or a new weave according to the current team need and live state.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Dreamweaver_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Coordinate denial of a likely cure

**Best when:** The team has access control or communication disruption that can meaningfully reduce a particular target’s next-night cure.

**Action:** Dreamweave that useful Town target, then coordinate the next night’s Smog or an appropriate Silence plan to make the cure harder.

**Target rule:** Use a target whose lost ability matters. Confirm that any Smog does not obstruct the team’s own important visit plan.

**Why:** The delayed payoff is more achievable when the team plans around the following night rather than simply marking a busy public hub.

**Change plan when:** If a Town astral cure or visible cure removes the pending state, record that failure and choose a new plan.

**Tradeoff:** Smog does not block every astral Town action. Silence hides communication rather than physically preventing a cure.

**Checkpoints:**

- Track the pending night
- Agree how to reduce cure traffic
- Distinguish Dreamwoven from Insomniac

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Check actual cure or progression
2. Avoid duplicating disruption on an already disabled enemy
3. Stay alive if persistent Insomnia matters

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Dreamweaver_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Poisoner_(ToS2)), [source 3](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Voodoo_Master_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Make the book kill

**Best when:** You hold the book and removing a reachable enemy now has more value than selecting only for delayed Insomnia.

**Action:** Choose the combined book attack and Dreamweave target for immediate kill value.

**Target rule:** Avoid an ally and weigh actual defense. Retain earlier pending targets in private notes instead of assuming they disappeared.

**Why:** A dead enemy no longer contributes abilities or votes; the delayed effect should not distract from the team’s enabled kill.

**Change plan when:** If damage fails, keep the real pending effect and its potential cure separate from the unsuccessful kill.

**Tradeoff:** The direct attack can expose your visit and the distinctive dream effect still does not supply the protection promised by a TP cover.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm the direct attack is enabled
- Check enemy defense and traffic
- Keep previous pending states

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Prioritize live threats with the book
2. Track survivors who may still progress
3. Do not invent an immediate roleblock result

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Dreamweaver_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is the simpler visiting cover when you want less daily paperwork. Psychic offers more case-making influence but needs early valid visions. Neither hides the correlation between your visit and a distinctive dream effect.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Low upkeep · direct visit only

A Dreamweave destination can match a one-player Barrier destination.

- Use the real Dreamweave target as the claimed Barrier destination, including book nights when you directly attack that same player.
- Answer TP and preserve the selected number. Do not write saved feedback from a dream effect or ordinary survival.
- Track the following-night cure requirement privately. A genuine Town protective visit can cure a pending Dreamweave; your Coven visit does not provide that support.
- If the target reports a dream effect and movement evidence points to you, matching one destination has not hidden the distinctive effect.

**Best when:** You want a simple direct-visit cover without daily invented investigations.

**Exposure/limits:** You grant no Barrier, do not cure the target as a Town visit would, and the dream effect can correlate with your arrival.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Dreamweaver_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Psychic (TI) — More influence · invented vision

One direct Dreamweave destination can fit a selected Psychic vision target.

- Include the real selected destination in the group, not the person whose later Insomnia would make the most convenient accusation.
- Keep N1 Good pairs and N2 Evil trios and post near day start; do not treat a Dreamwoven immunity outcome as a Psychic result.
- A Psychic group says at least one fits the indicated appearance. It does not identify every Town player or every target eligible for Dreamweave.
- Keep delayed-effect tracking private and expect a promised Town visit on a cure night to create additional verification pressure.

**Best when:** You accept early daily groups and want more discussion influence than a quiet TP story.

**Exposure/limits:** Dreamweave reveals no vision, and the target’s pending state or later cure can challenge the claimed Town visit.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Dreamweaver_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Sheriff (TI) — Pressure fallback · invented check

Your ordinary visit can fit one Sheriff check on the Dreamweave target.

- Use the target you actually visited; do not move the check to a later Insomniac player after dawn.
- Write a limited Innocent or Suspicious report early. Dreamweave working or failing is not a Sheriff result.
- Account for detection immunity and frames instead of claiming every non-Town must be Suspicious.
- On the following cure night, a claimed genuine Town visit may be testable against whether the pending effect persists; a normal-looking will line supplies no cure.

**Best when:** You want short investigative vocabulary and can maintain a consistent actual destination.

**Exposure/limits:** A role’s eligibility for Dreamweave is not the same as Sheriff suspicion, and the target can report the distinctive effect.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Dreamweaver_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Spy (TI) — Conditional · one known evil event

You know your own Dreamweaver visit, so that role event at the selected house can inform one narrow Bug story.

- A claimed Bug placement can share your actual direct Dreamweave destination. The possible report concerns the role Dreamweaver, not an invented visitor player identity.
- Keep the placement night and the reported trigger night explicit; older Bugs would remain active, so every later night adds obligations.
- Knowing your own effect does not give every other evil role that visited. Do not label a partial known event a complete observed list.
- Post useful information promptly and reassess if a pending target fails to be cured despite the Town visit you claim to have made.

**Best when:** You can keep placement and trigger history coherent and have a reason to reveal a limited known event rather than fake a full list.

**Exposure/limits:** You have no genuine Bugs, no continuing trigger feed, and no knowledge of every other evil visitor. Later promised Town cure visits can contradict the story.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Dreamweaver_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Spy_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Investigator (TI) — Harder fallback · no crime feed

A normal Dreamweave visit can match a one-player Investigate destination.

- Keep the public investigated number aligned with the real direct destination.
- Do not use Murder for an ability simply because it is harmful to Town; that label concerns an attack that night.
- Trespassing concerns prior cross-faction direct visits, not whether your dream effect worked. Nonvisitors need particular care.
- Post early without leaking Dreamweave eligibility or private cure tracking as if Investigator receives it.

**Best when:** You understand the crime timing rules and have a reason to use a more demanding case-making cover.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no Murder or Trespassing report; dreams and Insomnia are neither crime label, and independent observers can link the visit.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Dreamweaver_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Investigator_(ToS2)).

## Enchanter

CD — Coven Deception.

### Route 1: Frame the player most likely to be checked, not an arbitrary headline target. (recommended)

**Best when:** A plausible investigation target exists and you have no specific coordinated death forgery to justify spending Alterate.

**Action:** Enchant a nonally likely to receive an investigation. Keep Alterate unless the team has a concrete death-forgery plan.

**Target rule:** Use a disputed claimant or an expected investigation target. With no D1 evidence, choose an eligible unknown nonally but do not claim the frame already produced a result.

**Why:** A frame needs an evidence channel to notice it. Spending limited forgery charges without a likely death can accomplish little.

**Change plan when:** If no likely investigation exists, prioritize a later coordinated frame rather than building a loud unsupported push. Alterate can spend a charge even when the victim survives.

**Tradeoff:** A frame needs a detection channel to matter. It does not rewrite Tracker, Lookout, Spy, or Coroner observations.

**Checkpoints:**

- Frame for a specific evidence channel
- Preserve Alterate charges deliberately
- Compare the public reaction with independent evidence

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Place a frame before a relevant detection check to create a case the team can actually use.
2. Use Alterate with a planned death and a coherent role/faction/will story.
3. Reassess unaffected observers and the book; do not double down on a frame contradicted by independent evidence.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Enchanter_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Forge a planned death

**Best when:** The team expects a particular victim to die and a coherent false role, faction, and will would change Town’s next decision.

**Action:** Use Alterate with an internally consistent public story tied to the agreed victim.

**Target rule:** Choose a credible victim with a realistic death plan. Check that the forged claims and results fit earlier public events.

**Why:** Deleting or replacing a real information trail can be worth a scarce forgery charge when it causes a concrete misleading inference.

**Change plan when:** If the kill is unlikely or a Medusa mark will take priority, preserve the charge instead.

**Tradeoff:** A charge can be wasted if the victim lives. Stone Gaze can supersede the displayed forgery, and Coven knowledge is not the same as Town’s view.

**Checkpoints:**

- Agree the likely death
- Check the false will against public facts
- Track the two-charge budget

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Keep later public claims consistent with the forgery
2. Expect unaffected observers to challenge it
3. Avoid spending charges on speculative survivors

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Enchanter_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Medusa_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Use the book to make the death happen

**Best when:** You hold the book and a direct kill or attack-plus-forgery plan is more valuable than a frame alone.

**Action:** Choose a reachable enemy for the book attack; add Alterate only if a remaining charge has a useful coherent purpose.

**Target rule:** Choose for kill value and actual defense before choosing the false death story.

**Why:** An enabled attack can turn a death forgery from a guess into a more concrete team plan.

**Change plan when:** If protection or an obstacle prevents the death, retain that actual outcome rather than presenting the intended forgery as completed.

**Tradeoff:** Book damage is Basic and can fail. An invented personal investigation is still invented even if the target was genuinely Enchanted.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose the useful kill
- Check whether a forgery charge is worth spending
- Separate intended and actual death information

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Reconcile forged evidence with later claims
2. Do not dismiss unaffected observations as Enchant
3. Recheck book ownership and remaining charges

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Enchanter_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Sheriff is my active-pressure choice because framing can support part of a suspicious-looking case. Cleric is lower upkeep if you want a quiet story. Investigator is more demanding because crimes and timing must remain coherent; framing is not a real personal investigation.

#### 1. Sheriff (TI) — Active pressure · framing support

A real Enchant can make a target appear Suspicious to a real Sheriff, so your invented accusation can align with a manipulated detection channel.

- Keep the claimed Sheriff check on the actual Enchant destination; this makes your movement story coherent.
- A genuinely Enchanted target can produce a Suspicious result for another Sheriff, but your own line remains authored rather than received feedback.
- Post early and ask for claims instead of declaring every investigator or observer must agree with you.
- Enchant does not fool Tracker, Lookout, Spy, or Coroner. If those channels contradict the case, do not call them all frameable.
- Do not rewrite a previous result when the frame is consumed or the target dies Town; prepare for scrutiny of why your visit tracked the false evidence.

**Best when:** The target is likely to be checked and a restrained accusation has a useful team purpose.

**Exposure/limits:** You still did not receive a Sheriff check. Frames can be consumed, and unaffected movement or role information can connect the manipulation to you.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Enchanter_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Cleric (TP) — Lower upkeep · no defense

Enchant or Alterate can share one claimed Barrier destination without requiring a fresh investigative result each morning.

- Use the actual visited player as the claimed Barrier target, whether you used Enchant, Alterate, or a direct book attack.
- Say TP under alignment claims and avoid inventing saved feedback merely because the target survived.
- Keep forgery details private; Cleric cannot know a target’s hidden true role or original will from an altered graveyard entry.
- If the target dies, investigate the actual compatibility rather than moving the protection after dawn. Easy writing supplies little independent proof.

**Best when:** You prefer quiet target bookkeeping to leading an active framing case.

**Exposure/limits:** Neither gives Powerful Defense. A forged death is not a save, and separate observer evidence is unaffected.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Enchanter_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Investigator (TI) — Crime pressure · extra bookkeeping

Enchant can create Murder and apparent faction effects relevant to a real crime check, giving the story a concrete manipulated channel.

- Tie the claimed Investigate number to the player you actually Enchanted.
- Do not automatically write both Murder and Trespassing for every frame: purely nonvisiting or astral histories do not support ordinary Trespassing.
- Murder is night-specific, so do not carry an old planted attack label forward without the relevant current-night effect.
- Post the limited report early and remember that Spy, Tracker, Lookout, and Coroner can supply independent contradictory information.
- A successful-looking false case is not a reason to alter old will lines after the target’s death reveals more context.

**Best when:** You understand the difference between planted Murder and conditional Trespassing and can maintain early reports.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no personal crime result. Trespassing still depends on direct-visit history; unaffected observers are not rewritten.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Enchanter_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Investigator_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Psychic (TI) — Group pressure · high upkeep

The Enchant visit can match a chosen vision member, and a frame can affect apparent Evil membership in an actual Psychic group.

- Place the actual Enchant destination in the group as the chosen player, preserving movement consistency.
- Use odd-night Good pairs and even-night Evil trios; do not simply label every framed target an Evil vision on N1.
- Post near day start and discuss at-least-one membership rather than falsely naming an exact culprit from a vision.
- Keep Alterate scripts and exact hidden-role knowledge private. Psychic cannot read an original will or every player’s faction.
- A consumed frame or unaffected observation can overturn the intended case; do not rewrite past groups to fit the new graveyard.

**Best when:** You want broader discussion pressure while accepting strict parity, group, and posting obligations.

**Exposure/limits:** You obtain no vision or other group names, and a frame is not proof that an invented group satisfies all constraints.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Enchanter_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Spy (TI) — Conditional · own-event knowledge

You know an Enchanter visited your selected house, which can support one narrow role-event story at a claimed Bug destination.

- Match the claimed new Bug house to your actual visit; if reporting the known event, name the role Enchanter rather than inventing a visitor identity.
- Do not say the framed player’s fake apparent faction or role is what Spy must see. Spy reports the actual visiting role through this deception.
- Keep older Bugs active in the history and distinguish a new placement from a later trigger.
- A quiet line is not proof no other evil visited, and a real Spy can contradict an incomplete invented feed. This is a difficult fallback rather than the default frame cover.

**Best when:** You can maintain a placement timeline and report limited known events without pretending they are exhaustive.

**Exposure/limits:** You do not obtain the complete trigger feed or persistent Bugs, and Spy is itself not fooled by Enchant.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Enchanter_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Spy_(ToS2)).

## Illusionist

CD — Coven Deception.

### Route 1: Conceal the acting Coven member; save the teamwide charge. (recommended)

**Best when:** One ally is carrying out the team’s most exposed important action, usually the active book holder.

**Action:** If not attacking with the book yourself, Illusion the ally whose action is most exposed to investigation, usually the agreed book holder.

**Target rule:** Coordinate which ally is acting and likely to be checked. With no specific read, the acting book holder is a sensible starting recipient rather than a random ally.

**Why:** This protects a real team action from some detection instead of spending a broad limited ability without a reason.

**Change plan when:** Use Phantasm when several allies face meaningful detection pressure. Do not assume Illusion and a normal book attack are simultaneous alternatives you can take together.

**Tradeoff:** Illusion is astral and hides from some investigations; current rules do not let it bypass Bodyguard, Trapper, or Deputy.

**Checkpoints:**

- Coordinate concealment with a real ally action
- Distinguish Illusion, Phantasm, and Disguise
- Avoid relying on removed concealment effects

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Conceal the ally most likely to be checked while carrying out an important action.
2. Spend Phantasm for broad detection pressure, not simply because it is available early.
3. Update Disguise and the cover as book ownership or expected death information changes.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Illusionist_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Spend Phantasm on broad detection pressure

**Best when:** Several Coven members face meaningful detection pressure tonight and one Illusion cannot cover the important risk.

**Action:** Use the single Phantasm charge for the eligible faction-wide Illusions.

**Target rule:** Name the allies and detection channels this protects. A lone vague worry is a reason to keep the charge.

**Why:** The scarce teamwide ability is worth spending when it covers several real risks at once.

**Change plan when:** If only one important ally is threatened, return to ordinary Illusion and preserve Phantasm.

**Tradeoff:** Phantasm excludes Town Traitors. Illusion does not make an ally safe from every form of observation or direct protective retaliation.

**Checkpoints:**

- Count concrete detection risks
- Check the one-use charge
- Keep the actual book attacker coordinated

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Exploit the protected information window
2. Remember unaffected observers and protective counters
3. Keep Disguise consistent with the public cover

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Illusionist_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Attack with the book instead

**Best when:** You hold the book and the team needs your direct kill more than an Illusion that night.

**Action:** Choose the actual book attack against a useful nonally and verify the final selected mode.

**Target rule:** Avoid direct-visit traps; choose a target whose removal matters.

**Why:** An important kill can outweigh concealment, but it changes your own movement and the cover you can sustain.

**Change plan when:** If you decide to Illusion instead, actively select that mode; do not assume canceling one automatically restores the other.

**Tradeoff:** Illusion and the ordinary book attack are alternative actions. A direct attack is not covered by claiming an astral Oracle history.

**Checkpoints:**

- Submit one intended ability
- Check the direct target
- Reassess any no-visit cover

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Switch back to Illusion when another ally attacks
2. Expect direct visit evidence after book nights
3. Keep the chosen death Disguise coherent

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Illusionist_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Oracle is the sensible movement-oriented starting cover during astral Illusion. Deputy is low writing but difficult to demonstrate. Book attacks change the footprint; neither cover recreates a real protective effect or public shot.

#### 1. Oracle (TP) — Low upkeep · astral footprint

Ordinary Illusion is astral, fitting Oracle’s lack of a normal visible player visit.

- Write a Town role choice for Aegis, not the numbered Coven recipient of Illusion.
- Alternate the role choice on consecutive nights and use Oracle itself at most once.
- A recipient appearing Innocent or Town from your Illusion is not Oracle feedback. Do not leak that manipulation as though Aegis revealed it.
- If book duty begins, recheck the prior no-visit story; a direct attack does not become astral merely because Illusion was astral before.

**Best when:** You are using Illusion or Phantasm rather than performing a direct book attack.

**Exposure/limits:** Illusion does not give Aegis. A later direct book attack or illegal Oracle role-repeat can break the account.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Illusionist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Deputy (TK) — Little writing · high proof risk

A night without normal visible movement can resemble Deputy’s lack of night activity.

- Keep the history free of invented nightly checks; Deputy acts by day rather than choosing a protective target at night.
- Do not treat choosing Deputy in Disguise as gaining its shot. The selected death role changes no living ability.
- Deputy has one charge, and a claimed miss is not independent evidence. Repeated alleged attempts cannot form an unlimited routine.
- If asked to demonstrate, you cannot. Choose the actual survival or team plan with that limitation rather than expecting Illusion to fool a real Deputy’s eligibility test.

**Best when:** You accept demonstration pressure and need a sparse inactive-night story.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot perform a Deputy shot, and Disguise changes a death appearance rather than demonstrating a living day ability.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Illusionist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Cleric (TP) — Conditional · direct book route

A direct book attack can share one claimed Barrier destination when you choose attack instead of Illusion.

- Use the actual direct attack target as the claimed Barrier destination; do not use the ally you would otherwise Illusion.
- Keep one selected mode per night. Illusion plus a separate ordinary book attack is not a pair of actions you can assume both occurred.
- Answer TP and avoid invented save messages. A neat target list does not establish that Powerful Defense existed.
- Returning to astral utility changes the movement fit again. Reassess rather than silently treating every night as Barrier.

**Best when:** You have book duty and a history that can explain direct visits without contradicting earlier astral nights.

**Exposure/limits:** Astral Illusion nights are not ordinary Cleric visits, and the attack supplies no protection.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Illusionist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Sheriff (TI) — Harder fallback · direct book route

The selected direct book destination can fit a one-player Sheriff check.

- Match the claimed check to the direct attack target, not the ally who received an earlier Illusion.
- Post early with limited Innocent or Suspicious vocabulary; manipulating a teammate’s appearance does not give you a real check.
- Do not assume every enemy is Suspicious or use private Disguise choices as knowledge a Sheriff would receive.
- A switch back to utility can leave no normal visit. No plausible result can repair a contradicted selected destination.

**Best when:** You are actually performing direct book attacks and need a discussion-oriented alternative.

**Exposure/limits:** You have no Sheriff result; astral Illusion and Phantasm nights have the wrong ordinary movement footprint.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Illusionist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Coroner (TI) — Conditional · quiet N1 then book visits

An idle or astral N1 can fit the lack of a first-night Autopsy target; later direct book visits can match living Examine destinations.

- Write no usable Autopsy on a normal empty N1. Choose an actual eligible dead body during the claimed D2 Autopsy timeline before any N2 Examine.
- A later Examine target must match the living player you actually directly visited with the book, not an Illusion recipient on an astral night.
- Team kill-role knowledge can inform a coherent story about one death, but it is not a full Coroner feed or license to announce an enemy’s exact role.
- Do not dismiss an actual Coroner counterclaim as fooled by Illusion or Enchant; that channel is unaffected by those deceptions.

**Best when:** You kept an honest empty N1 and later take compatible direct visits after a real death.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no genuine Autopsy or Examine feed. Illusion does not change Coroner results, and later astral utility nights may not fit claimed direct examinations.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Illusionist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Coroner_(ToS2)).

## Medusa

CD — Coven Deception.

### Route 1: Stonemark the planned victim when hiding that death has value. (recommended)

**Best when:** The team has a credible kill plan and hiding that victim’s role or will has useful information value.

**Action:** Coordinate a Stonemark on the team’s likely nonally kill when denying the role/will is useful. Hold a charge if there is no credible death plan.

**Target rule:** Use the agreed victim, avoiding waste on an already marked player. With no coordinated or likely kill, preserve the charge and prepare the cover.

**Why:** Three active charges should remove useful information, not be spent nightly on people who will probably remain alive indefinitely.

**Change plan when:** If a persistent mark on a useful future victim offers more value than tonight’s uncertain kill, mark that player instead. The book changes the action by adding an attack.

**Tradeoff:** Stone Gaze is a permanent mark, not an immediate kill. You have three active charges and already start Stoned yourself.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose a useful concealment target
- Track persistent marks and charges
- Reassess marks alongside the team kill

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Hide an informative enemy death when the team can make the kill happen.
2. Track persistent marks and remaining charges so you do not duplicate work.
3. After the book arrives, plan the attack and concealment together while checking observer exposure.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Medusa_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Mark an important future victim

**Best when:** Tonight’s agreed kill is unlikely to land, but a different informative enemy is likely to die later.

**Action:** Place one persistent Stone mark on that future victim after agreeing why hiding their later information matters.

**Target rule:** Prefer a useful information holder expected to die eventually; do not re-mark an existing target.

**Why:** A permanent mark can still pay off on a later death, so the target need not die the night you visit.

**Change plan when:** If the player is likely to survive indefinitely or the charge has no foreseeable information value, keep it.

**Tradeoff:** You grant no protection and do not learn a living target’s role simply by marking them. Later death or observer evidence may connect the visit.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm the target is not already marked
- Name the information you want hidden
- Track the charge and persistence

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Coordinate a later meaningful kill
2. Avoid duplicate marks
3. Distinguish a marked survivor from a completed concealment

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Medusa_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Hold when no useful death plan exists

**Best when:** You have no credible victim or no reason that concealment would help the team.

**Action:** Save the active Stone charge and help plan the team’s next informative kill.

**Target rule:** Do not select a random house just to create a visiting will line. If you hold the book and can attack, compare that real kill separately.

**Why:** A limited charge kept for a meaningful death is worth more than one spent without a useful consequence.

**Change plan when:** Once a useful likely death emerges, choose a fresh mark or a coordinated book attack.

**Tradeoff:** Holding produces no direct visit. Do not write a performed Barrier or Psychic target as though matching movement has been established.

**Checkpoints:**

- Keep an accurate charge count
- Prepare a cover consistent with holding
- Identify which enemy information is dangerous

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Spend marks for actual information denial
2. Keep prior marks in the plan
3. Reassess after book ownership changes

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Medusa_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is the lower-upkeep direct-visit cover. Psychic can influence discussion but requires a legal vision every morning. A Stonemark is neither a Barrier nor a genuine vision, and book/death information can expose both stories.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Low upkeep · direct visit only

Your active Stone or direct book destination can match one claimed Barrier destination.

- Keep the claimed Barrier destination equal to the player actually Stoned or directly attacked that night.
- A held charge means you did not make that normal visit. Do not write an invented completed Barrier merely to fill N1.
- Answer TP and avoid saved messages without real supporting feedback; a permanent mark is not protective.
- The true role and will you can read after a Stoned death are private actual-role information. Cleric cannot mechanically reveal them.

**Best when:** You are actively visiting and want minimal daily result invention.

**Exposure/limits:** Stone grants no defense; a Stoned death or missing visit on a held night can expose the mismatch.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Medusa_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Psychic (TI) — More influence · invented group

One active Stone visit can fit the selected target of a normal Psychic group.

- Include the actual active Stone destination as the selected group member; do not choose a different target after the night’s deaths.
- Keep N1 Good pairs and N2 Evil trios and post near day start.
- Do not use secretly readable Stoned roles as though Psychic revealed exact roles; group logic is only at least one of the stated appearance.
- Track limited charge holds separately. If you did not visit, an ordinary selected vision is not supported by your actual movement.

**Best when:** You visit normally and accept early daily vision upkeep for greater day influence.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no vision, and concealment of a corpse’s role does not validate the invented group.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Medusa_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Sheriff (TI) — Pressure fallback · invented result

A real active Stone visit can match the checked player in a Sheriff account.

- Use the real active Stone target as the checked number before dawn.
- Post a limited Innocent or Suspicious story early; do not announce an exact role from the hidden Stoned corpse information.
- Know that book holders, Illusion, and many neutral roles can alter the simple evil-equals-Suspicious assumption.
- A charge-saving hold has no normal visit. A Stoned death may conceal some information but does not guarantee nobody observed you.

**Best when:** You prefer a short one-player report to maintaining Psychic groups and are actually visiting.

**Exposure/limits:** Stone provides no suspicion check, and private knowledge after death is not feedback received on the visit.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Medusa_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Spy (TI) — Conditional · known Medusa event

You know a Medusa visit occurred at the house you actively Stoned, which can inform one narrow Bug role-event story.

- A new Bug placement can share the destination of your real active Stone visit.
- If reporting the known Medusa event, name the visiting role rather than inventing its player identity or claiming every other visitor was absent.
- Older Bugs would remain active, so preserve their later trigger obligations and do not reset the will to one convenient new house nightly.
- A passive starting self-Stone is not an active visit to yourself, and a held Stone charge cannot explain a completed new Bug placement.

**Best when:** You can maintain new-placement and later-trigger history without treating known self-events as complete observation.

**Exposure/limits:** You have no continuing Bug feed or complete evil visitor list, and your secret ability to read Stoned wills is beyond Spy.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Medusa_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Spy_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Coroner (TI) — Conditional · held N1 then direct visits

A genuine N1 hold fits Coroner’s empty first-night state; subsequent direct actions can share a living Examine destination after a real Autopsy.

- Start with no usable Autopsy on an ordinary empty N1; do not fabricate a dead body to justify an early check.
- Use a real eligible body for the claimed daytime Autopsy before naming a later living Examine target.
- Your ability to read a Stoned corpse’s true role is not the same as learning the killer role through Autopsy. Keep those channels distinct.
- Coroner is unaffected by Enchant and Illusion. If a real Coroner contradicts your invented killer account, neither deception explains it away.

**Best when:** You held N1 and can maintain eligible real-body chronology plus later matching direct visits.

**Exposure/limits:** An active N1 Stone contradicts the usual no-N1-examination footprint. You do not actually receive Coroner’s killer records.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Medusa_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Coroner_(ToS2)).

## Necromancer

CU — Coven Utility.

### Route 1: Wait for a real corpse, then borrow the most useful legal ability. (recommended)

**Best when:** A normal fresh N1 has no usable corpse and you are not making a book attack.

**Action:** In a normal fresh game there is no corpse to raise. Without the book, hold and prepare a Retributionist cover; with the book, coordinate its alternative attack.

**Target rule:** At D2, inspect actual bodies. Prefer a usable Town corpse whose ability answers the team’s current need if sustaining a Ret cover.

**Why:** An honest empty N1 fits the real corpse prerequisite and the cover; inventing a corpse creates an avoidable contradiction.

**Change plan when:** An evil or Stoned corpse, restricted target, exhausted use, or a book attack can break the Ret overlap. Choose the actual useful action before deciding what the cover can explain.

**Tradeoff:** Retributionist overlap only works with a corpse and destination that Town Retributionist could legally use. Dead evil or Stoned bodies break that overlap.

**Checkpoints:**

- Inspect the real graveyard
- Select a usable ability
- Keep corpse use compatible with any Ret cover

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use an eligible useful corpse for real information, disruption, protection, or a kill the team needs.
2. Keep corpse role, availability, destination, and usage consistent across nights.
3. Reassess the Ret cover when the best real action requires an incompatible corpse or the book.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Necromancer_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Use the book’s direct attack

**Best when:** You hold the book and no available corpse action offers greater immediate value.

**Action:** Choose a useful nonally for the ordinary book attack rather than inventing a corpse action.

**Target rule:** Check direct-visit protection. On N1, a direct attack can be useful even though the graveyard is empty.

**Why:** The book supplies immediate kill value when your ordinary ability has no resource to use.

**Change plan when:** When a useful corpse becomes available, compare that ability against the book attack for the next night.

**Tradeoff:** You cannot perform an ordinary book attack and Reanimate in the same night. A visible living-player attack does not match an empty Ret night.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm which night mode is selected
- Keep the empty graveyard chronology honest
- Record the direct target separately

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use eligible Town corpses for a sustained Ret cover
2. Reassess before using evil or Stoned corpses
3. Keep each corpse’s actual uses and availability

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Necromancer_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Retributionist is the only close recurring corpse-ability cover here: legal Town corpses can create actual compatible effects. Oracle fits a genuinely empty or held night. Cleric and Sheriff are conditional direct-book alternatives, not explanations for ordinary corpse visits. Four useful options are shown rather than padding the list with incompatible night footprints.

#### 1. Retributionist (TS) — Strongest mechanical overlap · track legality

Both visit a dead player and send that body to act, so using an eligible Town corpse can reproduce the actual borrowed Town ability.

- A normal empty N1 can honestly be no usable corpse. Do not invent a body before any relevant death occurred.
- Record the corpse and its destination separately: you visit the dead player, while the raised body makes the living-target visit.
- Use only a Town corpse and destination that the claimed Town Retributionist can legally use. A legal real effect is much stronger than an arbitrary invented target line.
- Keep charge and availability state from the corpse’s actual ability, including competition for a body. Do not assume every role is reusable without limits.
- If the best real play uses an evil or Stoned body or your direct book attack, accept that it breaks this close overlap instead of labeling the action Ret-compatible.

**Best when:** You plan to use legal Town corpses and can maintain the corpse’s role, availability, and restrictions.

**Exposure/limits:** Evil or Stoned corpses, illegal destinations, unavailable charges, and direct book attacks exceed what Town Retributionist promises.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Necromancer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Retributionist_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Oracle (TP) — Conditional · empty or held nights

With no corpse and no book attack, you make no normal player visit, which can fit Oracle’s footprint.

- Name a Town role for Aegis, not a dead body or the body’s living destination.
- Rotate role choices on consecutive nights and use Oracle itself only once; quiet movement supplies no protection evidence.
- Do not leak corpse feedback as an Oracle result. Oracle neither raises bodies nor learns exact roles of its protected holders.
- Before using a corpse or accepting book duty, reconsider the existing no-visit account; a short convenient N1 is not a permanent disguise.

**Best when:** You genuinely hold and need a low-writing temporary account, with clear awareness that ordinary Necromancer activity will break it.

**Exposure/limits:** A later visit to a dead corpse or a direct book target cannot be explained by astral role-targeted Aegis.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Necromancer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Cleric (TP) — Conditional · direct book action only

When you choose the direct book attack, your living destination can match a claimed Barrier destination.

- On a book attack night, put the actual living attack destination in the Barrier line.
- Do not claim your own Barrier visit to the zombie’s living target when your personal movement went to a dead body.
- A raised Cleric can create real Barrier feedback, but its corpse visit remains a Retributionist-shaped action rather than proof you are a living Cleric.
- Say TP and avoid invented saves; compare the actual target death and direct protection evidence before extending this cover.

**Best when:** You are actually using direct book attacks instead of Reanimate and have compatible prior movement.

**Exposure/limits:** Ordinary Reanimate visits the corpse, not the living Barrier recipient, and neither mode inherently supplies Cleric protection.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Necromancer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Sheriff (TI) — Conditional · direct book action only

A direct book attack can share the living destination of a Sheriff check.

- Only a real direct book visit naturally matches your own selected living check. Do not turn a corpse visit into a hidden direct visit.
- Post early with limited Innocent or Suspicious wording rather than exact roles learned through a raised corpse.
- If you raise a Town Sheriff, its real check can inform the team, but visible corpse movement still fits Ret better than a living Sheriff claim.
- Keep normal empty N1 and later mode switches coherent. This is a situational book-mode option, not a reason to abandon real legal Town-corpse overlap.

**Best when:** You need pressure while performing direct book attacks and accept invented checks as a weaker alternative to genuine Ret support.

**Exposure/limits:** Reanimate has corpse-directed personal movement, and borrowed feedback is not an ordinary living Sheriff investigation by you.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Necromancer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

## Poisoner

CU — Coven Utility.

### Route 1: Drink first; reserve Smog for a specific access-control plan. (recommended)

**Best when:** A useful enemy night actor can be Drunk and there is no coordinated reason to block access to a different house.

**Action:** Without the book, Drink with an eligible useful enemy night actor; with no role evidence, coordinate an unknown nonally and avoid dangerous visitor bait.

**Target rule:** Prefer an enemy whose action matters. Keep allies clear and remember that an uncautious Serial Killer can retaliate against your Drink.

**Why:** An ordinary roleblock immediately disrupts an action and supports a Tavern Keeper cover without inventing investigative information.

**Change plan when:** Use Smog when denying access to a house is more valuable than blocking its owner, and ensure it will not obstruct allied visits. With the book, its direct attack also roleblocks.

**Tradeoff:** Hangover prevents a consecutive roleblock. Immune targets still act, and an uncautious Serial Killer can retaliate.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose a mode with a reason
- Keep the public cover compatible with actual effects
- Rotate Drink targets and recheck book state

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Drink a useful new enemy after a successful block; Hungover prevents a consecutive lock.
2. Use Smog to deny a specific enemy visit or protection route with team coordination.
3. When holding the book, prioritize the kill and compatible block; do not plan ordinary Smog plus book attack.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Poisoner_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Smog a specific access route

**Best when:** Stopping incoming visits at a house helps the team more than blocking the resident’s own action.

**Action:** Smog that house after coordinating every planned allied visit and the intended enemy visit to deny.

**Target rule:** A Dreamwoven target awaiting a cure or a house receiving known protective traffic can be useful. The resident’s own action is not roleblocked by Smog.

**Why:** Access denial can prevent a cure or protection route that ordinary Drink would leave open.

**Change plan when:** If an essential allied action is blocked too, change the house or use Drink instead.

**Tradeoff:** Smog can obstruct Coven visits, including interactions that otherwise look astral; Ritual is a documented exception. It is not a complete Socialite guest-list system.

**Checkpoints:**

- Name the incoming visit to deny
- Clear the plan with allies
- Keep Smog distinct from Drink

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Track obstacles without inventing visitor identities
2. Coordinate the next needed kill or cure denial
3. Change routes as enemy traffic adapts

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Poisoner_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Dreamweaver_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Combine book damage with a roleblock

**Best when:** You hold the book and an enabled enemy kill is the highest-value action.

**Action:** Use the book attack on a useful nonally; the action also attempts to roleblock that resident.

**Target rule:** Pick an enemy whose death or stopped action matters, checking actual immunity and defense.

**Why:** This can remove a target and interrupt their action rather than spending the night on access denial.

**Change plan when:** If damage fails, preserve the distinction between the blocked action, immunity, and the failed kill.

**Tradeoff:** You cannot use ordinary Smog and the book attack together. Retaliation and direct protection can still matter.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm the book action
- Check defense and roleblock immunity
- Keep Smog off the same-night plan

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Rotate around Hangover and immunity
2. Use Drink when another ally should attack
3. Avoid treating no Coven death as proof a blocked target is the killer

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Poisoner_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Tavern Keeper is my beginner default because Drink can reproduce the expected roleblock. Socialite is a higher-rule-load Smog story. Cleric has easier paperwork but worse effect fit; do not discard genuine roleblock support just to type fewer lines.

#### 1. Tavern Keeper (TS) — Genuine effect overlap · default Drink

Your Drink uses the same roleblock effect and Hangover constraint as Tavern Keeper, giving real target feedback to support the cover.

- Put the real Drink target in the will. If the target confirms being roleblocked, that is genuine effect support rather than only matching movement.
- After a successful block, rotate targets because Hangover prevents the consecutive lock. Do not invent a second successful block through immunity.
- Answer TS during alignment claims and do not treat a missing Coven kill as proof the blocked player was the only killer.
- Smog does not roleblock the resident’s own action, so do not report it as Drink. A book night may block but the added kill still creates scrutiny.
- Remember that an uncautious Serial Killer can retaliate against Drink; the cover does not remove your actual role’s danger.

**Best when:** You are using ordinary Drink on a useful enemy night actor.

**Exposure/limits:** Smog is a different action; book attacks add damage, and immunity or Serial Killer retaliation still apply.
