Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Poisoner_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tavern_Keeper_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Socialite (TS) — Conditional Smog resemblance · extra rules

Smog and Party can both deny incoming visits at a selected house, giving a real obstacle event to discuss.

- Use the actual Smog house as the claimed Party house, not the resident of an ordinary Drink block.
- Explain that incoming visitors are obstructed while the resident can still act; that is different from roleblocking the resident.
- A real Socialite invites guests during the day and must change houses on consecutive nights. Smog does not create those guest exemptions for you.
- Unknown Obstacle feedback is compatible with several causes and does not confirm Socialite. Track which visitor exceptions could contradict the invented guest plan.
- Do not silently switch Drink or a book kill into a Party entry. That changes the effect you are claiming to reproduce.

**Best when:** You are deliberately using Smog for access denial and can sustain the more demanding Party and guest-list history.

**Exposure/limits:** Smog has no Socialite daytime Guest List, differs on eligible visitors, and cannot be combined with the ordinary book attack.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Poisoner_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Socialite_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Cleric (TP) — Low writing · weaker than real Drink fit

A direct Drink or book destination can match a single Barrier target on paper.

- Keep the public Barrier number equal to the actual directly visited resident, not a visitor blocked by Smog.
- Do not call a roleblock or obstacle a save. Cleric supplies Powerful Defense and does not stop the resident’s own action.
- Answer TP, keep stable target history, and do not create routine saved messages from survival alone.
- If the target confirms being blocked and a Tracker links the visit to you, more Cleric formatting does not erase the wrong effect.

**Best when:** You need a quiet visiting TP alternative and accept giving up the stronger genuine Tavern Keeper effect overlap.

**Exposure/limits:** No Barrier exists. A roleblock notice or Smog obstacle can link the visit to a disruptive rather than protective action.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Poisoner_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Psychic (TI) — Discussion fallback · fabricated groups

A real direct Drink destination can be the selected player in a normal Psychic vision.

- Use the player you actually Drank with as the selected vision member; Smog victims and denied visitors are different entities.
- N1 is a Good pair and N2 is an Evil trio. Keep the selected target and old group names unchanged.
- Post near day start and discuss at-least-one logic, not the unsupported conclusion that a roleblocked player must be evil.
- Do not claim a fresh complete vision from a held or incompatible route. The real block is evidence for Tavern Keeper, not Psychic.

**Best when:** You want daily group influence and can maintain format constraints alongside the real roleblock history.

**Exposure/limits:** Drink gives no vision, and the recipient’s roleblock notice can expose the disruptive visit.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Poisoner_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Spy (TI) — Conditional · known Poisoner event

You know your own Poisoner visit at the selected house, which can inform one limited Bug role-event account.

- A claimed new Bug placement can share your actual direct destination; a known trigger would name Poisoner rather than an invented player identity.
- Keep Drink’s resident block separate from Smog’s incoming-visit obstacle. Spy does not let you convert either into a full visitor list.
- Maintain all earlier claimed Bugs, since genuine Bugs remain active after a new placement.
- Report useful limited information promptly, but do not label unknown other evil visitors absent or call the own-event overlap complete independent proof.

**Best when:** You can maintain placement and later-trigger chronology and have a reason to use a more complex information cover.

**Exposure/limits:** You have no true persistent Bugs or complete trigger feed, and block versus Smog feedback may expose the real action.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Poisoner_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Spy_(ToS2)).

## Potion Master

CU — Coven Utility.

### Route 1: Reveal an unresolved enemy unless an ally genuinely needs Barrier. (recommended)

**Best when:** No ally faces a concrete immediate threat, so one unresolved useful enemy role is worth Revealing.

**Action:** Without a concrete ally threat, Reveal a useful nonally to improve the team’s next kill and role information. Choose Barrier instead when keeping an important ally alive has immediate value.

**Target rule:** With no D1 role information, Reveal an eligible nonally after coordinating with Coven. Avoid a target the team is already confidently eliminating if the information will be wasted.

**Why:** Reveal creates actionable knowledge; an unneeded Barrier gives less value simply because Cleric is an easy cover.

**Change plan when:** A threatened book holder or vital utility ally can make Barrier the highest-value mode. Recheck the book for the harmful alternative and Illumination before any Ritualist follow-up.

**Tradeoff:** Reveal grants Illumination, which temporarily stops Ritualist from using that information for a Ritual. Knowing a role does not grant its action history.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose one useful potion mode
- Keep special role knowledge private
- Coordinate the next action without breaking the cover

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use role knowledge to select a valuable reachable enemy or expose a dangerous ability.
2. Barrier a key ally when the expected survival gain beats another reveal.
3. Choose the one appropriate mode each night and keep its consequences compatible with the cover.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Potion_Master_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Barrier an ally under real threat

**Best when:** Keeping a valuable ally alive now matters more than one extra role reveal.

**Action:** Use Barrier on that ally and record genuine attack/save feedback.

**Target rule:** Prefer an ally with concrete danger and important remaining utility, not a random recipient. Self-Barrier is possible outside its documented endgame restriction.

**Why:** A real Powerful Barrier can preserve an important teammate and genuinely support part of a Cleric story.

**Change plan when:** If the threat disappears or would bypass Powerful Defense, compare Reveal or the team’s book plan.

**Tradeoff:** Barrier cannot stop Unstoppable damage. Repeated self-protection does not match a Cleric’s single self-Barrier allowance.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose the threatened ally
- Know what Powerful Defense can stop
- Keep actual feedback separate from a guessed save

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Protect important remaining utility
2. Return to Reveal when no concrete threat exists
3. Do not imply every potion night supplied Barrier

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Potion_Master_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Use the Harmful Potion

**Best when:** You hold the book and the team needs your enabled kill more than protection or information.

**Action:** Select the Harmful Potion against a useful nonally.

**Target rule:** Check the destination’s actual defense and direct-visit dangers; do not target an ally or assume Reveal and Barrier also resolve.

**Why:** An enemy removed now may matter more than a role learned or an ally protected without a threat.

**Change plan when:** If another ally should carry the kill, coordinate that plan and select one genuinely useful utility potion instead.

**Tradeoff:** One selected potion is one action. A kill night is not also a genuine Cleric save or Sheriff investigation.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm the selected potion
- Choose a reachable useful enemy
- Keep mode history accurate

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Switch potions for real team needs
2. Account for book nights in the cover
3. Preserve verified role information without leaking it as impossible TI results

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Potion_Master_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is simplest and has genuine support on Barrier nights. Sheriff can use role knowledge for a selective story but still invents the check result. Seer has high payoff pressure and a normal two-visit requirement your one-target potion does not reproduce.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Best beginner fit · real Barrier nights

Your Barrier Potion genuinely gives the same Powerful Defense and save feedback as Cleric Barrier, with one direct destination.

- On actual Barrier nights, use the same target and genuine attacked/protected feedback; this is real effect support, not merely a visiting resemblance.
- Record Reveal or Harmful Potion privately. Do not describe those nights as if they also gave defense.
- If protecting yourself in the public Cleric account, reserve the one allowed self-Barrier; Potion Master’s broader self-use does not transfer to the claimed role.
- Answer TP under alignment claims and do not leak exact-role Reveal knowledge into a protective will.
- A saved ally does not prove your Town faction. Keep the cover’s legal target history and the actual potion mode separately coherent.

**Best when:** An ally genuinely needs protection or you want a cover whose selected Barrier nights can create real compatible feedback.

**Exposure/limits:** Reveal and Harmful Potion nights supply no Barrier. Potion Master’s repeat self-protection does not match Cleric’s single self-use.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Potion_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Sheriff (TI) — Role-informed pressure · invented check

Reveal learns an exact role at the same single-player destination, which can inform a limited Sheriff-shaped report.

- Tie the claimed check to the player you actually Revealed, keeping movement consistent.
- Do not announce an exact role merely because Reveal supplied it. Sheriff only reports Innocent or Suspicious.
- A known evil role can still be Innocent, while frames can make Town Suspicious. Use the actual detection rules rather than faction alone.
- Post early and preserve old reports, including nights when Barrier or Harmful Potion supplied no real investigative result.
- Keep Illumination and the team’s Ritual eligibility private; Sheriff does not mechanically learn those from its check.

**Best when:** You are Revealing useful enemies and want discussion influence without Psychic’s group bookkeeping.

**Exposure/limits:** Exact role is not Sheriff feedback; book holders, neutrals, Illusion, and framing complicate expected suspicion.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Potion_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Investigator (TI) — Role-informed crime story · extra care

Reveal gives useful role context at a single direct destination that can match an Investigate target.

- Use the actual potion destination as the claimed investigated player; keep the selected mode private and accurate.
- A revealed killing role has Murder only when it actually attacked that night. Do not infer it solely from the role’s potential.
- Trespassing requires the relevant cross-faction direct-visit history; a newly learned role gives no complete lifetime movement log.
- Post a limited crime account early and keep Illumination and exact-role Reveal feedback out of the public Investigator result.

**Best when:** You understand crime timing and have evidence about the target’s action beyond its role name.

**Exposure/limits:** You get no crime output, and knowing the role does not establish whether it attacked that night or ever crossed factions.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Potion_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Investigator_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Psychic (TI) — More group influence · high upkeep

A potion’s one selected destination can be a chosen vision member, and Reveal can inform some group reasoning.

- Include the real potion target as the selected group member; do not fabricate a second personal visit.
- Use odd-night Good pairs and even-night Evil trios and post near day start.
- Do not announce exact roles or imply every member’s alignment is known. Psychic gives an at-least-one constraint, not a list of faction reveals.
- Barrier or Harmful Potion nights still create no vision. Keep the mode in private notes and preserve the public group chronology.

**Best when:** You accept fresh daily groups and want broader pressure rather than short Sheriff reports.

**Exposure/limits:** You get no genuine vision or other group names, and knowledge can be stale or affected by apparent-alignment effects.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Potion_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Spy (TI) — Conditional · known potion event

Your actual Potion Master visit supplies one known evil role event at the house where a Bug placement could be claimed.

- Match a claimed new Bug placement to your real potion destination; the known visiting-role event is Potion Master, not an invented visitor identity.
- Do not present the exact role learned by Reveal as the role of a visitor the Bug saw. The revealed resident and visiting Potion Master are different subjects.
- Carry old Bugs forward and separate placement nights from later trigger nights.
- Do not report a complete empty trigger list merely because you know only your own visit. Other evil visits and astral exceptions remain outside your information.

**Best when:** You can maintain a continuing Bug timeline and deliberately limit public reports to supported role-event information.

**Exposure/limits:** You do not gain persistent Bugs or every trigger. Reveal’s exact-role information and Barrier feedback exceed a simple Bug event.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Potion_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Spy_(ToS2)).

#### 6. Seer (TI) — High upkeep · weak movement match

Exact-role and known-faction information can help you reason about an apparent Friends or Enemies pair.

- Do not call one Reveal destination a complete two-player Seer action. An observer can expose the missing second visit.
- Friends means apparent same faction and Enemies means apparent different factions. It is not a Town/evil label on either person.
- Track Seer’s target-reuse restrictions and post the pair early rather than retrofitting it to later claims.
- A previously Revealed exact role can be stale after role or faction changes, and framing can change a real Seer comparison.
- If the visit footprint cannot be explained, choose a single-target claim instead; confident faction reasoning does not repair incompatible movement.

**Best when:** You have a specific explanation for the two-visit requirement and accept this as a difficult conditional option, not a natural fit.

**Exposure/limits:** A normal potion visits only one player, while normal Seer visits both selected players; role knowledge still does not guarantee apparent faction through frames.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Potion_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Seer_(ToS2)).

## Voodoo Master

CU — Coven Utility.

### Route 1: My default N1: Silence; Blind a known investigator instead. (recommended)

**Best when:** You lack a reliable investigative target and a useful nonally is likely to contribute to the next day’s discussion.

**Action:** With little role information, Silence a nonally likely to contribute to D2 discussion. If you already have a credible investigative target, prefer Blind to deny that night’s results.

**Target rule:** Pick a vocal organizer or likely reporter the team is not already expecting to kill. With no D1 lead, use an eligible unknown nonally; do not pretend a random player must be a TI.

**Why:** Silence has broad value before you know roles: it removes a speaker’s next-day contribution and restricts vote changes. Blind has greater targeted information-denial value once you know who receives useful results.

**Change plan when:** Deafen is my situational third choice: use it when isolating someone from other players’ information matters more than suppressing their speech or results. They can still post their own results. With the book, choose the kill for team value first.

**Tradeoff:** Silence suppresses communication, not the night ability. The same player cannot receive any doll on consecutive nights.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose the intended disruption
- Preserve the mode in private notes
- Rotate targets even when changing dolls

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Blind a fresh credible TI when denying new results matters most; the ability still executes, so this is not a roleblock.
2. Silence a vote organizer or valuable speaker when suppressing tomorrow’s discussion is the bigger threat.
3. Deafen to isolate a player from chat/whispers in a specific coordination plan; rotate targets every night even if changing dolls.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Voodoo_Master_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Blind a known information role

**Best when:** A credible enemy investigator or other feedback-dependent player is likely to produce consequential results tonight.

**Action:** Choose Blind on that player to suppress that night’s visible feedback and results.

**Target rule:** Prefer a supported useful information role over an unknown chatty player; check the previous night’s target regardless of doll mode.

**Why:** Denying a real report can be more valuable than Silence when you know where the information will come from.

**Change plan when:** If the target has no useful feedback to deny, is your previous target, or is acting in a way Blind will not stop, return to another legal disruption.

**Tradeoff:** Blind does not stop the player from acting. A protection or attack can still resolve even when the recipient sees no result.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify the result you want to deny
- Check the last targeted player
- Record Blind rather than roleblock

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Rotate to useful fresh information targets
2. Coordinate day pressure around missing reports
3. With the book, plan damage plus the selected effect

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Voodoo_Master_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Deafen a coordination-dependent opponent

**Best when:** An enemy needs to receive claims or whispers to coordinate the next day, and preventing their replies is less valuable than denying incoming information.

**Action:** Choose Deafen on that player and let the team plan around the disrupted communication channel.

**Target rule:** Use an organizer or player relying on private coordination; they must be a different player from your last doll target.

**Why:** Deafen can isolate a coordinator from new discussion while they remain able to speak.

**Change plan when:** If the objective is to stop public reporting use Silence; if it is to deny night results use Blind instead.

**Tradeoff:** Deafen does not mute the player or roleblock them. It is narrower than a complete communication shutdown.

**Checkpoints:**

- Name the incoming communication to deny
- Remember the target can still speak
- Check the consecutive-player rule

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Choose the doll for the next specific obstacle
2. Avoid treating Deafened speech as proof the effect failed
3. Reassess book damage and target legality

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Voodoo_Master_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is my simpler beginner cover: one plausible target line, TP under VFA, and no invented vision each morning. Psychic can contribute more to discussion but is higher upkeep and contradiction risk. Both remain weak on actual ability support: your doll effect is not protection or a vision.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Low upkeep · effect contradiction risk

One doll target can share a claimed Barrier destination, avoiding invented daily TI results.

- Use the real doll recipient as the claimed Barrier destination; do not pick a convenient living player after dawn.
- Say TP during alignment claims. Keep a plain target line instead of inventing attacked-and-saved feedback.
- Choose the real doll for team value: Silence limits outgoing day speech, Deafen limits incoming communication, and Blind hides night feedback without roleblocking.
- Track the actual previous target because changing dolls does not bypass the consecutive-player restriction.
- If the target reports the doll effect and an observer links your visit, a supposed Barrier does not explain it. Reassess the survival plan instead of adding fake saves.

**Best when:** You want the simplest direct-visit cover and understand that ease of writing is not actual protection support.

**Exposure/limits:** Dolls grant no defense. Silence, Deafen, or Blind can identify the disruptive visit, especially with movement evidence.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Voodoo_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Psychic (TI) — More influence · more upkeep

Your ordinary doll destination can be the selected player in a Psychic vision, providing a compatible visit-shaped story.

- Include the actual doll target as the selected group member, even when another player would make an easier invented group.
- Use N1 Good pairs and N2 Evil trios. Psychic does not identify which member satisfies the vision.
- Post near day start; do not use the target’s Silence as an opportunity to rewrite old groups or claim they confirmed a report they cannot discuss.
- Keep all doll-mode and missing-feedback information private unless learned publicly. Psychic does not roleblock, silence, or diagnose every night effect.
- Rotate actual targets across nights and account for failed or held actions. A direct witness plus a doll report remains a serious contradiction.

**Best when:** You want day influence and can post valid groups early every morning.

**Exposure/limits:** You have no true vision, while the target’s distinctive disruption can expose you independently of the group’s plausibility.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Voodoo_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Sheriff (TI) — Short TI story · no real check

A doll visit can match one selected Sheriff check, with simpler result vocabulary than Psychic groups.

- Use the actual doll recipient as the claimed checked player and post the limited report early.
- Do not label a Blinded player Suspicious because they lack a result, or a Silenced player evil because they cannot answer. Those are your effects, not Sheriff evidence.
- Respect book-holder, neutral, and framing exceptions instead of treating every enemy as mechanically Suspicious.
- A roleblock claim is not a replacement for a Sheriff result: Blind hides feedback but can leave the target’s action fully active.
- If movement links you to the doll effect, evaluate the actual exposure rather than expecting a tidy two-word result to prove Town.

**Best when:** You want a focused case and accept invented-result risk instead of the quiet TP route.

**Exposure/limits:** The doll supplies no Innocent or Suspicious result, and the recipient can identify Silence, Deafen, or Blind.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Voodoo_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Investigator (TI) — Harder fallback · crimes not disruption

The one-target doll visit can match an Investigate destination, but supplies no actual crime information.

- Align the public investigated number with the real doll target; a direct book attack still does not grant an actual investigation.
- Do not write Murder merely because a target’s ability was harmful or they were affected by your doll.
- Trespassing concerns cross-faction direct visits, not missing day chat or missing night feedback. Purely nonvisiting histories need particular care.
- Post early, preserve the result you committed to, and keep your real doll-mode diagnosis out of the claimed Investigator feedback.

**Best when:** You understand current crime timing and want a more detailed case-making alternative.

**Exposure/limits:** Doll effects are not Murder or Trespassing, and an unexplained crime claim or actual disruption witness can contradict the story.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Voodoo_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Investigator_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Spy (TI) — Conditional · known Voodoo event

You know your own Voodoo Master visit at the selected house, which can inform a narrow Bug role-event story.

- A claimed new Bug placement can share the doll’s actual destination. A trigger-style statement names Voodoo Master, not a visitor player number you were never given.
- Do not treat Silence, Deafen, and Blind as identical. Publicly observed effects can corroborate an event without independently proving you were Spy.
- Keep earlier Bugs active in the history and label later trigger nights; one new target does not erase the old ongoing obligations.
- Do not declare nobody else evil visited merely because your own doll is the only event you know. This is a conditional extra-work cover, not free confirmation.

**Best when:** You can maintain Bug placement chronology and want to describe a known role event without inventing a full visitor map.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no persistent Bug triggers or complete evil-visitor list. The target’s disruption and an observer can also point to you.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Voodoo_Master_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Spy_(ToS2)).

## Wildling

CU — Coven Utility.

### Route 1: Use Sense to build a real Tracker story. (recommended)

**Best when:** A useful enemy’s outgoing action is likely to provide a clear, limited Tracker-style report.

**Action:** Sense a useful eligible nonally and keep the destination channel separate. Prefer a player whose action claim you can check tomorrow.

**Target rule:** With no opening lead, choose an eligible unknown nonally outside obvious visitor traps. Do not assume your visitor list, destination list, and whispers are interchangeable.

**Why:** Real movement information is more useful for a believable cover than inventing a quiet protective history without any actual protection.

**Change plan when:** Use Lookout instead when visitor information genuinely supports a better story and the watched-house footprint fits. The book also attacks the selected target and can change the account.

**Tradeoff:** Sense shows visible movement, not every astral action, and also gives extra information a Tracker cover must keep private.

**Checkpoints:**

- Capture separate information channels
- Choose one public cover scope
- Reassess the target and story with the book

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Sense players whose movement can answer a relevant claim or identify a useful enemy action.
2. Publish only the chosen cover’s legitimate information subset, with normal TI timing.
3. Reassess the target and cover after book acquisition; never leak private whispers into an ordinary Tracker report.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Wildling_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Build a real Lookout account

**Best when:** Incoming traffic at a useful house is more informative than following its resident’s destination.

**Action:** Sense that house and keep only the visible visitor-identity channel in the public Lookout account.

**Target rule:** Choose a house likely to receive strategically useful visits without knowingly entering a lethal traffic trap. Do not select yourself as though normal Lookout permits self-Watch.

**Why:** Real visitor observations give more support than invented protective claims when the traffic channel is the valuable one.

**Change plan when:** If useful evidence mainly concerns outgoing movement, keep the Tracker route instead; do not blend both roles’ outputs in one will.

**Tradeoff:** A missing name is not proof nobody visited astrally. Book damage and private whisper knowledge can expose information beyond the claimed role.

**Checkpoints:**

- Separate visitors from destinations
- Choose a legal Lookout-shaped target
- Keep whisper knowledge private

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Report only the visible visitor channel
2. Do not infer exact roles from visitor identity
3. Reassess if the book attacks the same watched target

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Wildling_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Kill while retaining Sense

**Best when:** You hold the book and the team wants a direct attack more than observation of a survivor.

**Action:** Attack and Sense the same useful nonally target; retain the actual movement feedback.

**Target rule:** Choose a valuable reachable enemy, recognizing the book forbids an allied target and removes the option to observe a different house at the same time.

**Why:** You retain real information while performing the team kill, so the night is not observation-only.

**Change plan when:** If the direct visit is too dangerous or a different observation is essential, coordinate book ownership before assuming both actions can split.

**Tradeoff:** Sense and the book attack share a target. An accurate report does not turn the associated attack into a Town ability.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm the combined target
- Separate the kill from the observed channel
- Check direct protection

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Keep only the cover’s permitted information
2. Use surviving traffic to refine later choices
3. Expect death details to challenge a quiet observation story

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Wildling_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Tracker and Lookout remain the strongest beginner choices because Sense gives real matching information channels. Lower claims use only partial context or require invented results and are weaker. Choose one channel before posting, preserve its target and timing, and never label unobserved astral activity absent.

#### 1. Tracker (TI) — Best information fit · outgoing channel

Sense supplies the selected player’s visible destinations, which is the central information a Tracker promises.

- Write the selected player’s actual visible destinations, such as 4 to 9, rather than a list of people who visited 4.
- Post near day start and compare the destination to the target’s claim; no visible visit does not prove they did nothing or are Town.
- Keep incoming visitors, exact private whispers, and team-only role knowledge out of the mechanical Tracker result.
- If two visible destinations appear, preserve both; do not simplify a real multi-visit into a misleading single-target report.
- With the book, Sense and attack the same target. Accurate movement information does not explain away the associated Coven kill.

**Best when:** Outgoing movement from your selected Sense target is the most useful public evidence.

**Exposure/limits:** Astral actions can be unseen. Book damage, whisper knowledge, and the separate visitor channel can reveal powers beyond Tracker.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Wildling_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tracker_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Lookout (TI) — Strong information fit · incoming channel

Sense supplies visible visitor identities at the selected house, matching Lookout’s incoming-traffic channel.

- Use the actual Sense house as the watched house and publish only the visible visitor identities.
- Normal Lookout does not watch itself. A legal actual Wildling target is not automatically a legal Lookout will target.
- Do not label visitor identities as exact roles. A role inference from public claims belongs in discussion, not as a mechanical Watch result.
- Post early, preserve the actual list, and do not interpret no visible visitors as proof no astral action occurred.
- Book attacks share the same target as Sense. Keep kill consequences and private whisper knowledge out of the Lookout result.

**Best when:** A useful house’s visitors are more valuable than following the resident’s outgoing action.

**Exposure/limits:** Sense does not reveal astral visitors, and leaking destinations, whispers, or the book attack exceeds the claimed role.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Wildling_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Lookout_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Sheriff (TI) — Lower fit · invented suspicion

Your ordinary Sense visit can match a one-player check, while observed behavior may help choose a discussion target.

- Keep the checked player equal to the actual Sense target, not one of their visitors or destinations.
- A person visiting an evil player is not automatically Suspicious, and no visible visit does not guarantee Innocent.
- Post a limited report early and do not leak the actual visitor list or private whispers as information Sheriff received.
- If exact movement is needed to defend the story, remember that supplying it would reveal powers beyond the claimed check. This weaker fit is why Tracker ranks first.

**Best when:** You deliberately prefer short suspicion reports and accept giving up genuine Tracker or Lookout evidence.

**Exposure/limits:** Sense gives movement, not Innocent or Suspicious. An apparently inactive or suspicious destination does not establish a Sheriff result.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Wildling_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Psychic (TI) — Lower fit · fabricated group

A single Sense destination can serve as the selected member of a claimed vision, with real traffic context informing discussion.

- Include the actual Sense target as the chosen vision member, preserving the real direct visit.
- Use N1 Good pairs and N2 Evil trios and post near day start.
- Do not mistake the target’s visitor list for the vision group or use a private whisper as an exact mechanical Psychic result.
- Discuss at-least-one constraints and preserve old groups. Accurate real traffic elsewhere does not prove an invented vision was genuine.

**Best when:** You want group pressure and accept daily invention despite having better-supported movement covers.

**Exposure/limits:** Sense supplies no vision or group names. Movement and whisper knowledge cannot establish the exact apparent-alignment constraint.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Wildling_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Spy (TI) — Conditional · known visiting-role subset

Sense can identify a known Coven visitor at a selected house, and your team knowledge can identify that visitor’s role for a narrow Bug-style report.

- Match a claimed new Bug placement to the house you actually Sensed. Do not put the resident’s outgoing destination in the Bug target field.
- A known visitor’s actual role can inform the event, but Spy normally reports visiting roles rather than their player identities.
- Do not label that known subset the complete trigger list. Sense can show unknown visitor identities whose roles you do not know.
- Maintain previous Bug placements and later trigger obligations; Sense only observes your current selected house each night.
- Keep whispers private. They can guide target selection but are not a Bug result and do not fill every gap in the ongoing feed.

**Best when:** Reliable team-role knowledge and a compatible placement history support a limited event, with explicit awareness of missing information.

**Exposure/limits:** You do not obtain the complete persistent Bug feed or every visiting evil role, especially for unknown players or unseen astral actions.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Wildling_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Spy_(ToS2)).

## Covenite

Coven Outlier — Coven Outlier.

### Route 1: Prepare a sustainable cover; attack only with the actual book. (recommended)

**Best when:** Another ally is already using the book effectively and you have no independent night ability.

**Action:** Without the book, there is no special N1 action. Agree on the team plan and use a cover that does not invent an unavailable ability.

**Target rule:** No target is needed without the book. With it, choose a coordinated eligible nonally and account for direct movement.

**Why:** Your value is team coordination and later book use; fake utility records do not create real disruption.

**Change plan when:** Oracle can fit a genuinely nonvisiting opening, but no Aegis is supplied. A direct book visit can make a simple visiting cover easier to explain; do not keep an impossible no-visit story by habit.

**Tradeoff:** An empty night has no natural effect evidence. A low-writing claim can still require public proof later.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose a cover legal in this setup
- Avoid invented first-night results
- Update the plan when the book changes hands

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Help the team use private information and day votes without exposing its roster.
2. When holding the book, remove a useful reachable enemy with the agreed attack.
3. Update the cover when movement or demonstration demands outgrow the inactive opening.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Covenite_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Carry the team’s book

**Best when:** Giving you the book frees a stronger ally’s utility, and the team accepts the direct attacker’s exposure risk.

**Action:** Coordinate book ownership and attack a useful nonally if the kill is enabled.

**Target rule:** Choose a reachable enemy while useful allies spend their own actions on utility. Never assume you have the book simply because this is your preferred route.

**Why:** Using the otherwise idle Covenite for the ordinary kill can preserve the team’s more valuable specialist actions.

**Change plan when:** If another book holder has a specific superior attack route, let that plan take priority and remain available for later.

**Tradeoff:** Being expendable does not make a bad attack good. Book ownership and direct traffic still constrain the plan.

**Checkpoints:**

- Agree who actually holds the book
- Coordinate a useful target
- Keep utility allies free to act

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Take book work when it preserves better utility
2. Reassess traps and defense before attacks
3. Update the public footprint after each action

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Covenite_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Trade cover longevity for early pressure

**Best when:** The team needs a plausible public case or a deliberate trade more urgently than your long-term quiet survival.

**Action:** Prepare a restrained TI cover before the night, using coordinated real team knowledge only where the claimed role and movement could explain it.

**Target rule:** Choose one interpretable unresolved enemy. Do not invent an impossible N1 visit while you actually stayed home.

**Why:** A less valuable specialist can sometimes take the social risk of a contested case, leaving stronger allies a safer path.

**Change plan when:** If there is no supported story or the case would expose several teammates, keep the lower-pressure cover instead.

**Tradeoff:** This is a riskier discussion route, not a new Covenite ability. A fabricated result can cause an immediate execution and is not evidence-based confirmation.

**Checkpoints:**

- Agree the intended social trade
- Check the claimed visit footprint
- Post a coherent limited report early

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Reassess when the target’s role becomes known
2. Do not leak secret role information as a magical result
3. Keep the next book action consistent with the actual plan

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Covenite_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Oracle fits a genuinely inactive nonbook start; Cleric fits an ordinary direct book visit more naturally. Both are invented-protection stories. Simple empty-night roles with public buttons create much larger demonstration demands later.

#### 1. Oracle (TP) — Low upkeep · inactive nonbook nights

Without the book you have no night ability or ordinary visit, matching Oracle’s no-normal-visit footprint.

- Write a Town role choice for Aegis, not an invented numbered-player visit.
- Rotate role choices on consecutive nights and use the Oracle self-role option only once.
- Do not claim saved feedback from a role simply surviving. Covenite has no information about actual Aegis outcomes.
- Before taking the book to free stronger team utility, reassess whether the direct attack will contradict your existing no-visit account.

**Best when:** Another ally attacks and your own nights are genuinely inactive.

**Exposure/limits:** No Aegis exists, and a later direct book attack conflicts with the current story. Quiet nights alone are not protection evidence.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Covenite_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Cleric (TP) — Conditional · direct book footprint

When you carry the book, one direct attack destination can match one claimed Barrier destination.

- Match the Barrier number to the actual book target before seeing the night outcome.
- Keep genuinely idle nights distinct. Do not invent routine visits while an Investigator or Tracker could establish no movement.
- Answer TP and avoid fictional saves. A living target list is easy to write and supplies little independent proof.
- If the target dies or direct protection catches the attacker, the intended cover does not change what happened; reconsider the survival and vote plan.

**Best when:** You expect to carry the ordinary team kill and want a simple direct-visit story.

**Exposure/limits:** No protection is supplied, and inactive nonbook nights do not create normal Cleric visits.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Covenite_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Sheriff (TI) — Pressure option · fabricated checks

On direct book nights your target can also be the subject of a short Sheriff report; team context can inform a limited accusation.

- Choose the case and claimed destination before dawn; do not place a completed check on a night you actually stayed home.
- Post early with Innocent or Suspicious, not an exact role derived from private team information.
- Know that book holders, Illusion, and many neutrals can be Innocent; a convenient enemy accusation is not automatically a valid expected result.
- A deliberate one-for-one social trade can cost your survival. Treat that cost as a team choice, not as a claim proven by a short will.

**Best when:** You accept a contested public case to help stronger teammates, while keeping movement compatible.

**Exposure/limits:** Covenite supplies no suspicion feedback, and nonbook idle nights have no normal check visit.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Covenite_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Psychic (TI) — More influence · fabricated groups

A direct book destination can fit the selected player in a claimed vision, giving more discussion material than quiet TP.

- Include the actual direct destination as the selected member instead of choosing a convenient group after others claim.
- Use N1 Good pairs and N2 Evil trios, posting near day start.
- Do not expose the known Coven roster as a magical complete faction read. A vision only states an at-least-one apparent-alignment constraint.
- Preserve group history after deaths and acknowledge that returning to idle nonbook nights removes the movement resemblance.

**Best when:** You are actually visiting with the book and can maintain early daily groups.

**Exposure/limits:** No real vision exists, and idle nonbook nights lack normal Psychic movement.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Covenite_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Deputy (TK) — Little writing · demonstration risk

A truly inactive nonbook night resembles Deputy’s lack of night actions.

- Keep the will about a day-shot role, not imaginary night checks or a night book kill described as a Deputy shot.
- Deputy has one charge; repeated alleged misses are not an unlimited believable action history.
- A real public Deputy event is independent evidence you cannot reproduce with Covenite.
- If asked to demonstrate or given the book, reassess the actual team plan. Having no special utility does not remove the cover’s proof problem.

**Best when:** You accept a sparse but hard-to-demonstrate cover and do not expect long-term mechanical proof.

**Exposure/limits:** You have no day shot. A claimed miss proves little, and direct book visits add a separate contradiction.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Covenite_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)).

#### 6. Pilgrim (Town Outlier) — Setup-dependent · no ability evidence

In a setup that actually permits Pilgrim, both roles can have no ordinary night action without the book.

- Check the role list rather than assuming Covenite’s presence proves Pilgrim can spawn.
- Keep the account honestly no-night-action in format; this cover cannot explain an actual direct book visit.
- Contribute through questions, claim comparisons, and votes. No ability means no mechanical result to manufacture or use as proof.
- Expect setup and vote-based scrutiny. A claim with zero writing can still be a low-credibility place for Town to execute.

**Best when:** The setup permits Pilgrim and you genuinely remain inactive at night; otherwise do not choose it.

**Exposure/limits:** Pilgrim is Town Outlier and must be possible in the role list. A direct book attack contradicts its no-ability promise.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Covenite_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pilgrim_(ToS2)).

## Cultist

Coven Outlier — Coven Outlier.

### Route 1: Save the conversion for a useful supported recruit. (recommended)

**Best when:** You are not attacking with the book and lack a useful supported Town recruit worth spending the one conversion on.

**Action:** Your special ability is daytime Indoctrinate, not another night action. Without the book, use N1 to prepare a cover and identify a worthwhile Town recruit.

**Target rule:** Use real role claims and expected survival to choose a recruit once the daytime control is enabled. With no evidence, wait; do not invent a guaranteed D1 conversion opportunity.

**Why:** One conversion is most valuable when it adds a useful surviving role to the team, not when spent on an arbitrary opening name.

**Change plan when:** If waiting risks losing a uniquely valuable reachable recruit, act sooner. At night the book can provide an alternative attack; the new recruit retains their role and changes faction.

**Tradeoff:** Indoctrinate is a daytime action, changes faction without replacing the recruit’s role, and removes your Basic Defense once successful.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose a useful recruit from evidence
- Track one actual conversion
- Coordinate the retained role under its new faction

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Convert a useful legal Town target with a credible survival/ability payoff.
2. Coordinate the recruited role under its new faction instead of assuming a Town-looking role stays Town.
3. Reassess your defense and book action from actual conversion feedback.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cultist_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Carry the book while preserving conversion

**Best when:** You actually hold the book and a useful enabled kill advances Coven more than remaining idle tonight.

**Action:** Attack a useful nonally; retain Indoctrinate for the next legal day opportunity with a worthwhile Town recruit.

**Target rule:** Pick for reachable kill value and avoid destroying the only useful intended recruit without considering the trade.

**Why:** The ordinary night kill and the scarce day conversion solve different problems; keeping the charge does not require wasting an available attack.

**Change plan when:** When a supported valuable Town recruit emerges, reassess conversion before the day vote or ability window closes.

**Tradeoff:** A surviving attack before conversion is not proof of a Town protective role. The direct book visit also breaks an Oracle-shaped no-visit story.

**Checkpoints:**

- Verify book ownership and kill availability
- Keep the recruit list separate from the kill target
- Track whether Basic Defense remains

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Recruit useful Town ability or vote value
2. Plan around losing your initial defense
3. Keep the recruit’s real role and new faction separate

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cultist_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Oracle fits the nonbook night footprint; Cleric is a visiting fallback with the book. The daytime conversion is not a protective night action. Recruits keep their role while changing faction, so do not leak that private transition in a public will.

#### 1. Oracle (TP) — Low upkeep · nonbook night footprint

Your special conversion is by day, so a nonbook night can have no ordinary visit like Oracle’s astral action.

- Write a Town role choice for Aegis, not the numbered player you might later Indoctrinate.
- Alternate role choices and use Oracle itself only once. A quiet night does not independently support a save.
- Keep the recruit’s role and changed faction private; Oracle does not reveal exact identities or conversions.
- Reassess the no-visit history before taking book duty. Your Basic Defense before conversion is a separate real ability, not proof Aegis protected you.

**Best when:** You preserve the conversion and are not performing direct book attacks.

**Exposure/limits:** No Aegis exists. A later book attack or a surviving hit from your pre-conversion defense can draw scrutiny.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cultist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Cleric (TP) — Conditional · direct book route

A book attack can share one claimed Barrier destination on an actual direct-visit night.

- Use the actual night book destination as the claimed Barrier target, not the player selected for a daytime conversion.
- Answer TP and keep quiet target bookkeeping without inventing saved feedback.
- Do not leak the new Coven member’s retained Town ability as though Cleric mechanically learned their role.
- After successful Indoctrinate you lose your initial Basic Defense. Do not imply this is a permanent Cleric protection state or unlimited self-Barrier.

**Best when:** You expect to carry the book and have a compatible direct-visit history.

**Exposure/limits:** Neither conversion nor the book grants Barrier. Nonbook idle nights lack normal Cleric visits.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cultist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Bodyguard (TP) — Conditional · early survival resemblance

Before conversion your Basic Defense can resemble one limited Bodyguard Self Protect survival, and a book visit can share a Guard destination.

- If explaining an early survived Basic hit as Self Protect, keep the Bodyguard two-use limit; Cultist’s pre-conversion defense is not an unlimited shield story.
- A self-shield night does not also Guard another player, and a direct book visit is a separate actual action.
- Do not invent a successful counterattack or saved resident. Cultist does not cancel an enemy visitor’s action as Guard would.
- After conversion your Basic Defense disappears. Plan around the real loss and do not claim the retained defense proves Town.

**Best when:** A specific early survived Basic attack or a direct book route makes the limited resemblance relevant.

**Exposure/limits:** Your defense is not a Bodyguard counterattack or Guard interception. Repeated self-shields and promised protection can be disproved.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cultist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Sheriff (TI) — Pressure fallback · direct book only

A direct book target can fit a single Sheriff check, with private team information sometimes guiding discussion.

- Use the actual book destination as the checked player; a daytime recruit is not a night-visited Sheriff target.
- Post early with Innocent or Suspicious, keeping exact private recruit or role knowledge out of the result.
- Remember that a converted Town role changes faction but retains its role. Do not assume old public role confirmation means current Town faction.
- Do not write a completed check on a genuinely idle nonbook night. This is a route-dependent fallback, not evidence supplied by conversion.

**Best when:** You are making real direct book visits and accept a more demanding early report history.

**Exposure/limits:** Cultist provides no night suspicion result, and idle nights or daytime conversion do not create a check visit.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cultist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Deputy (TK) — Sparse history · proof risk

A nonbook night with no action resembles Deputy’s night inactivity.

- Keep the public history about a day resource rather than invented nightly investigation results.
- Do not present Indoctrinate as a shot or imply the recruit’s changed faction is visible Deputy confirmation.
- Deputy has one real charge; an alleged miss is not independent proof and cannot be repeated as an unlimited excuse.
- If the book is passed to you, its direct visit no longer fits a completely inactive night story. Reassess before making that switch.

**Best when:** You accept demonstration pressure and want a sparse inactive-night account.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot produce a day shot, and conversion is not a Deputy demonstration. Your Basic Defense can also complicate a casual story.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cultist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)).

#### 6. Psychic (TI) — High upkeep · direct book only

A direct book destination can be the selected member in a normal one-target Psychic vision.

- Include the actual direct night destination as the selected member, not the person converted during the day.
- Keep N1 Good pairs and N2 Evil trios and post near day start.
- A recruit retaining a confirmed Town role can now have Coven faction; do not confuse role identity with apparent faction when reasoning about group constraints.
- Keep conversion and exact-role team knowledge private. Psychic does not announce a complete roster, and a valid-looking invented group does not supply protection or proof.

**Best when:** You take book duty and want day group influence despite significant ongoing maintenance.

**Exposure/limits:** No genuine group is supplied, and genuinely idle nonbook nights have the wrong movement footprint.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cultist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

## Baker

NA — Neutral Apocalypse.

### Route 1: Feed likely survivors (recommended)

**Best when:** Use the survivor-first opening when you have no reliable threat map and need dependable Bread progress.

**Action:** Feed one eligible other player. Your first checkpoint is one living Bread recipient, not a kill or a save.

**Target rule:** Prefer an unpressured player likely to stay alive. With no evidence, pick an eligible unknown outside obvious visiting danger; avoid a player already facing a strong execution case.

**Why:** Bread has no protective effect. Feeding a player who immediately dies loses useful transformation progress; your own starting Bread does not count as one of the ordinary three other recipients.

**Change plan when:** Replace a dead recipient with a fresh living target. Once transformed, stop planning Feed actions and use the Famine guide.

**Tradeoff:** A recipient can still die, and feeding a protected Town leader may preserve a powerful opponent for the Famine endgame. Bread supplies no protection.

**Checkpoints:**

- Keep one other recipient alive
- Reach the required living-recipient threshold
- After transformation, survive the vote and pressure remaining bread-holders

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Feed a new surviving nonrecipient rather than wasting a visit on existing Bread.
2. Preserve enough living recipients to reach three others at dawn; check the reduced-population exception in your card.
3. After transformation, survive the vote and accelerate a Bread-holder’s starvation.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Feed a quieter future opponent

**Best when:** You can distinguish a likely survivor from the obvious protected leader, and neither is already facing execution.

**Action:** Feed the quieter eligible survivor instead of automatically feeding the most prominent Town claim.

**Target rule:** Prefer a low-pressure player likely to survive tonight but unlikely to control the vote after transformation. With no such read, return to the default.

**Why:** You need living recipients now, then those same surviving votes can become obstacles after Famine appears. This trades a little survival confidence for an easier later voting position.

**Change plan when:** If that player becomes the likely execution or night victim, use your next Feed on another living nonrecipient rather than defending the original choice forever.

**Tradeoff:** Quiet does not mean safe or weak; this route relies on a real public read and can slow transformation if you guess wrong.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose a living nonrecipient
- Avoid preserving an obvious vote controller unnecessarily
- Keep Bread progress private

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Replace dead recipients before refreshing existing Bread.
2. Reach the actual living-recipient requirement at dawn.
3. After transformation, judge votes and Starve targets using the Famine guide.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric and Psychic remain the starter choices: Cleric requires less invented information; Psychic creates more discussion influence. The lower options reuse a direct Feed destination but promise effects or results Feed never supplies. These are conditional cover choices, not equivalent ability matches. After becoming Famine, use the transformed action guide. Starve accelerates Bread loss; it does not keep supplying any claimed Town protection or vision.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Low upkeep · no real protection

Feed is a real one-person visit, so its destination can support the travel part of a Barrier account while you quietly build living Bread recipients.

- Use the actual Feed destination as the claimed Barrier destination so a movement report does not force you to rewrite the night.
- Write N1: Barrier 4, for example; do not add saved 4 unless genuine public evidence supports that separate claim.
- Choose likely surviving recipients for your Baker objective, then explain why each could reasonably have been a protection choice.
- A Bread-holder dying is a warning for the cover and your progress; inspect the cause instead of claiming Barrier makes every death impossible.

**Best when:** You want one stable nightly target line and can tolerate weak independent proof.

**Exposure/limits:** Bread never grants a Barrier. An incompatible attack, a failed-save story, or the Famine transition can expose the missing protection.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Psychic (TI) — More influence · invented visions

A Feed recipient can be the selected player in a Psychic vision, giving you a real destination while the group report creates daytime leads.

- Include the real Feed recipient as the selected target; you cannot claim a different selected visit merely because another player looks useful at dawn.
- N1 Good uses two names with at least one appearing Good; N2 Evil uses three with at least one appearing Evil. Neither identifies exactly which name fits.
- Publish the whole cover vision near day start and keep previous lists unchanged; assembling it after every claim is easier to challenge.
- Keep Bread recipients private: several living recipients do not become confirmed Town, and Famine does not validate any prior vision.

**Best when:** You can post promptly every morning and understand Good/Evil group logic.

**Exposure/limits:** Feed supplies no vision. Extra names, parity, group size and later public information can contradict the invented report.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Bodyguard (TP) — Simple target log · interception risk

Feed can follow the same one-player route as Guard without requiring a daily investigation report; quiet guarding nights are easy to describe.

- Claim Guard on the actual Feed destination rather than a post-dawn replacement house.
- Keep a quiet entry quiet: Guard 4 does not mean you blocked an attack or killed its source.
- Remember that Bodyguard is an interception role, not Cleric’s general shield; a resident surviving says little by itself.
- Before defending a target death, compare the real cause and any known harmful visitor. Do not invent a sacrifice or a self-vest to repair it.

**Best when:** Your visits fit a plausible guard route and nobody has supplied an event that demands a real interception.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot intercept an attacker. A visible harmful visitor, the resident dying, or an impossible interception-survival story can distinguish you.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Trickster (TK) — Quiet setup · later proof burden

The Feed visit can resemble placing Magic Mirror on one person; a mirror that never absorbs anything can produce a quiet history.

- Use Magic Mirror on the actual Feed target as the setup account, preserving the observed route.
- Do not write Absorb just because the protected-looking player lived: the role needs actual absorption feedback.
- Never add an Unleash before an alleged absorption, and remember that an Unleash night replaces ordinary mirror placement.
- Your private aim is keeping Bread recipients alive; if they are attacked, the missing mirror effect may make this cover much weaker than the destination match suggests.

**Best when:** A TK cover fits the deck and you can sustain a setup account without claiming a demonstration you never performed.

**Exposure/limits:** Bread absorbs no attack. An invented Absorb or Unleash introduces an effect and attack history Baker cannot create.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trickster_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Sheriff (TI) — Active pressure · no real check

A Feed destination can be the person you claim to Search, allowing a simple result vocabulary and a more active daytime account.

- Name the actual Feed recipient as the checked player; keep that choice fixed before reading the morning claims.
- Use only Innocent or Suspicious, not an exact Town role. An Innocent line is not a clear on a Bread-holder.
- Post the claimed check promptly and explain any missing check rather than using Baker feedback as an investigative result.
- Avoid repeatedly defending every recipient as Town: Bread membership supports your transformation, not their allegiance.

**Best when:** You prefer a simple TI format over protection claims and accept the daily posting burden.

**Exposure/limits:** You learn no Innocent or Suspicious result. Independent reports can expose the invented information even when the visit is genuine.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

## Berserker

NA — Neutral Apocalypse.

### Route 1: Prepare for the first kill (recommended)

**Best when:** Use the preparation opening in normal Apocalypse: N1 has no attack, and your first legal kill unlocks the next attack tier.

**Action:** You cannot make the normal Apocalypse attack on N1. Prepare a consistent cover and mark a likely killable target for N2 instead of inventing a night visit.

**Target rule:** For N2, prefer an unprotected, unresolved opponent over a public protection magnet or a player who already survived a Basic attack. With no reliable leads, choose an eligible unknown outside obvious Veteran danger.

**Why:** Your power grows from actual kills. An early attack into likely defense or heavy protection delays the first upgrade and leaves you exposed before transformation.

**Change plan when:** After a failed attack, reconsider defense and protection; do not repeat blindly. At three kills, switch to War’s two-target plan.

**Tradeoff:** A low-profile N2 target may be less strategically important. A missed Basic attack gives no kill upgrade.

**Checkpoints:**

- Keep the first-night history legal
- Secure real kill progress
- Reassess target count and rampage after each upgrade

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Secure the first actual kill to gain Powerful Attack.
2. After the second kill unlocks Rampage, favor a useful opponent likely to attract visitors.
3. Reach the third kill, then protect your daytime survival while War removes opposing votes.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Plan an early disruptor removal

**Best when:** A credible Witch, execution threat, or other dangerous opponent becomes identifiable before your first legal attack.

**Action:** N1 still has no attack: prepare your cover and mark the threat. From N2, attack them only if your current attack can plausibly kill through their likely protection.

**Target rule:** Prioritize a dangerous, apparently unprotected opponent over a random player. A public protection magnet is a reason to defer, not a reason to pretend Basic Attack beats every shield.

**Why:** Removing an opponent who can expose or stop you can be worth more than a quiet kill, provided you do not sacrifice the first upgrade to predictable defense.

**Change plan when:** If the threat is heavily protected, get a safer first kill and revisit them with the actual upgraded attack tier.

**Tradeoff:** High-profile targets attract protection and observers. A failed attack both delays transformation and can disclose your route.

**Checkpoints:**

- Respect the N1 attack restriction
- Identify why this opponent threatens you
- Check attack tier against likely defense

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Count actual kills, not attempted attacks.
2. Use the first upgrade to revisit otherwise unsuitable threats.
3. After the third kill, switch to War and confirm two legal targets.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-flexible-factions-update-patch-notes-r1-5-11/).

### Claim strategy

Vigilante preserves the quiet-N1 timing best among the starter options; Cleric is a simple destination story only on later direct-attack nights. Coroner and Trapper offer alternative inactive openings, while Deputy is short but easily tested. No Town cover reproduces growing attacks and Rampage indefinitely. After becoming War, a two-house Unstoppable Rampage no longer resembles a normal Vigilante shot or single-person protection visit.

#### 1. Vigilante (TK) — Quiet N1 fit · later kill mismatch

Your normal N1 inactivity resembles Vigilante loading before a later shot, so you can avoid inventing an opening visit.

- Write N1: Load as the cover, while privately recording that Berserker had no legal N1 attack.
- From N2, a claimed shot must fit an actual prior Load; you cannot report a shot every night as though Vigilante has endless ready ammunition.
- Keep claimed shot targets aligned with any observed direct attacks, but do not claim Berserker-labelled deaths prove you are Vigilante.
- Track the cover’s Town-hit gun penalty separately from your Berserker upgrades; a Rampage is not an ordinary single-player shot.

**Best when:** You want a TK cover with a mechanically plausible inactive N1 and can maintain the later load/shot history.

**Exposure/limits:** Berserker kills are not Vigilante shots. Attack upgrades, Rampage, death attribution and the load/ammunition sequence can expose the difference.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vigilante_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Cleric (TP) — Later visit fit · weak N1 fit

A later direct attack has a single-player destination that can be described as a Barrier visit, with little writing on quiet claimed nights.

- Do not claim an ordinary N1 Barrier visit if a movement report already establishes that you stayed home.
- For later nights, preserve the actual selected attack destination instead of naming a convenient survivor after dawn.
- Write only the alleged Barrier choice unless you can support additional feedback; your kill confirmation is not a saved-target message.
- As the attack upgrades, expect multi-victim or distinctive deaths to defeat the simple one-house protection account.

**Best when:** You are evaluating a later attack-night account and understand that the cover is weaker across the whole timeline.

**Exposure/limits:** The normal Berserker N1 is nonvisiting and no attack grants protection. Deaths, observed N1 inactivity and Rampage undermine the story.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Coroner (TI) — Inactive opener · crime-report upkeep

Coroner normally has no usable N1 autopsy record, matching your inactive opening without an invented early visit.

- Use N1: No usable autopsy rather than claiming to identify a killer before a legal body exists.
- Record a real eligible daytime Autopsy before the cover’s first related night Examine; a role name alone cannot supply a killer record.
- From N2, compare the claimed Examine destination with your real attack movement before committing the entry.
- Do not turn your knowledge of a Berserker victim into a Coroner match: you know your own attack, not the borrowed investigation’s result.

**Best when:** You can track genuine corpse timing and want a quieter N1 before taking on TI obligations.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive neither autopsy evidence nor killer comparisons, and your later direct attack need not match the claimed Examine destination.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Coroner_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Trapper (TP) — Build-night fit · persistent trap burden

A claimed Build on N1 fits your lack of a direct visit. The later placement sequence supplies a TP story with fewer routine result lines.

- Open with N1: Build, not a placed trap; that preserves the one clear timing overlap.
- A later Place Trap target should be compatible with any observed attack visit, but your Berserker attack creates no actual trap.
- Keep only one active trap in the story and do not invent visitor-role feedback from a quiet death report.
- Once later attack nights or Rampage conflict with rebuilding or leaving a trap active, the initial N1 match no longer carries the cover.

**Best when:** A TP setup cover fits your initial inactivity and you understand the build-before-placement sequence.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot place or trigger a real trap. Later victims, visiting roles and an impossible rebuild schedule can expose the invented device.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trapper_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Deputy (TK) — Minimal writing · immediate test risk

Deputy needs no night visit or investigation, so your N1 can be described without inventing a nightly target.

- Write No night ability for N1 rather than manufacturing a check or protection target.
- Explain a held shot as a decision based on insufficient evidence; do not claim a miss is public proof of Deputy.
- A real shot is a visible daytime event. Berserker’s N2 kill cannot substitute for it.
- Reassess immediately if someone observes you visiting or requests a justified demonstration; minimal typing does not make this a durable cover.

**Best when:** You need a short early account and accept that a reasonable shot request can make it collapse.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot produce the daytime shot. Later observed attack visits also conflict with a role that has no night action.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)).

## Plaguebearer

NA — Neutral Apocalypse.

### Route 1: Start a fresh visiting branch (recommended)

**Best when:** Use the visitor-first opening while there are many uninfected players and a plausible visiting target can start another spread branch.

**Action:** Check the existing infection markers, then Infect an eligible uninfected player likely to make a direct visit.

**Target rule:** Prefer an uninfected active visiting claim. Without claims, choose an uninfected unknown; avoid your already infected adjacent neighbors. Their starting infection is not evidence of a visit.

**Why:** A visiting infected player can spread Plague beyond your one direct target. Reinfecting an existing carrier usually gives less new progress than adding a fresh branch.

**Change plan when:** When only a few uninfected survivors remain, target the missing players directly instead of chasing speculative spread. On transformation, switch to Spread Pestilence.

**Tradeoff:** A claimed visitor may not visit or may act astrally. Starting adjacent infections do not establish any movement.

**Checkpoints:**

- Expand beyond seeded infections
- Track actual spread without inventing visits
- Switch correctly to Pestilence when notified

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Create new infection branches through uninfected direct visitors.
2. Use the actual infection map to finish isolated survivors who are not spreading naturally.
3. After transformation, remove the most threatening opponent while surviving the day vote.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Infect a player unlikely to spread naturally

**Best when:** A credible nonvisitor or isolated survivor is uninfected while the busy players are already connected to infected targets.

**Action:** Directly Infect that missing player rather than adding another speculative visitor branch.

**Target rule:** Check the actual infection markers first. Choose an uninfected person who is unlikely to visit an infected house or receive infected visitors.

**Why:** Transformation needs coverage of every living opponent, not the largest number of visits. An isolated uninfected survivor can become the final bottleneck.

**Change plan when:** If many uninfected players still remain and no isolated candidate is credible, return to the visitor-first default.

**Tradeoff:** This often produces less immediate spread than a new active visitor. Use it when the coverage problem is visible, not because a player is simply quiet.

**Checkpoints:**

- Check seeded and current infections
- Find a genuine missing branch
- Do not invent a complete visitor report

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Recheck living uninfected players after each dawn.
2. Target the last missing survivors directly.
3. After transformation, use Spread Pestilence rather than repeating the infection buildup.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-flexible-factions-update-patch-notes-r1-5-11/).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is the simplest direct-Infect cover. Lookout stays the second starter because actual spread can supply limited contact clues, but it is substantially harder and not a complete report. Psychic, Tracker, Bodyguard and Sheriff are lower conditional options. Seeded neighbors never count as observed visits. As Pestilence, Spread Pestilence has a different lethal effect. Do not continue describing the actual objective as finding the last uninfected player.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Low upkeep · no real Barrier

Direct Infect gives a real one-player visit that can support the route of a claimed Barrier without requiring invented daily information.

- Use the actual direct Infect target as the claimed Barrier destination; passive spread is not another visit you personally made.
- Keep any no-attack entry modest. A target surviving is not a real saved message and infection does not protect them.
- Choose productive uninfected targets for your objective, then keep a plausible protection reason for that same route.
- Separate every infection marker from your public notes; neither infected neighbors nor new carriers are confirmed Town.

**Best when:** You want a stable one-target cover while focusing privately on the infection map.

**Exposure/limits:** Infection supplies no defense. A real attack, observed spread or Pestilence transformation can expose the missing protection.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Lookout (TI) — Partial contact clues · high upkeep

Source-checked infection spread can reveal some contact relationships, which may help you reason about visitors rather than inventing every name blindly.

- Choose the watched house consistently with your actual direct Infect destination if observers may track your own visit.
- For each name, ask whether your evidence is truly an incoming visit to that house; a carrier visiting another house is a different direction.
- Never label your initial adjacent infections as N1 visitors. They began infected without needing a visit.
- A real Lookout owes the game-supplied visible visitor list, not whichever infection contacts are convenient. Disclose the limitation to yourself before selecting this difficult cover.

**Best when:** You have concrete contact evidence for a particular house and understand the difference between incoming visitors and outgoing movement.

**Exposure/limits:** Spread feedback is not a complete Lookout result. Seeded infections, outgoing spread, invisible visits and unrelated contacts can make the claimed house list wrong.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Lookout_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Psychic (TI) — Broad leads · invented group reports
