The direct Infect target can be the selected member of a vision, keeping one genuine movement point while a group account drives discussion.

- Include your direct Infect target as the chosen member of the claimed vision, not a passive spread recipient selected after dawn.
- Maintain N1 Good/two names and N2 Evil/three names, with at least one matching the category rather than exactly one.
- Post the cover list near day start and keep previous groups unchanged as roles are revealed.
- Do not use spread to call someone confirmed evil or Town. Infection records contact and progress, not faction.

**Best when:** You can manage early daily posts and strict group counts without treating infection as faction knowledge.

**Exposure/limits:** Plague gives no Good/Evil vision and no guaranteed allegiance information about any group member.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Tracker (TI) — Partial outgoing clues · movement risk

Some transmission feedback can support a narrow inference about where an infected player traveled, giving more grounding than a completely arbitrary destination guess.

- Claim to follow the same person you directly Infect if your own visit is visible; following an unrelated spread contact creates another mismatch.
- Distinguish X infected Y through an outgoing visit from Y visiting X; the claimed Tracker result needs the outgoing direction.
- A seeded infection or lack of a new infection does not establish no visit. The target could visit an already infected person or act astrally.
- Write only a legally structured destination history and recognize that a genuine Tracker would receive fuller information than your partial map.

**Best when:** One particular outgoing contact is well supported and you can resist filling missing movement with invented certainty.

**Exposure/limits:** It does not provide the full Tracker result for every chosen player. Your direct Infect route must also fit the person you claim to follow.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tracker_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Bodyguard (TP) — Simple guard line · no interception

A direct Infect can share Guard’s ordinary visit route and avoids the daily fabricated names required by an information cover.

- Use Guard on the actual Infect destination and keep passive spread contacts out of the target list.
- Do not claim an interception because a player stayed alive; you received infection progress, not Bodyguard feedback.
- If the resident dies, inspect the cause and known visits rather than treating all deaths as either automatic proof or automatically harmless.
- After Pestilence appears, a new lethal direct effect is not retroactive evidence of guarding anyone.

**Best when:** A simple visiting TP story is preferable to managing TI reports, with no known interception event to explain.

**Exposure/limits:** You never intercept a harmful visitor. Real attack and survival evidence can disprove the claimed Guard effect.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

#### 6. Sheriff (TI) — Simple vocabulary · no real suspicion check

The direct Infect visit can double as the alleged Search route, producing a compact TI account rather than a protection story.

- Check the same person you directly Infect in the public account; passive carriers are not additional Search targets.
- Use Innocent or Suspicious only, and remember Innocent is not a Town clear.
- Do not derive Suspicious from a new infection, a busy spread branch or an infected neighbor. Those are not suspicion checks.
- Keep a consistent early-posted history as other investigators and the eventual transformation put the account under pressure.

**Best when:** You want a familiar one-person TI format and accept prompt posting and independent contradictions.

**Exposure/limits:** Infections reveal no Innocent/Suspicious result. Confusing contact with allegiance creates unsupported accusations.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

## Soul Collector

NA — Neutral Apocalypse.

### Route 1: Mark the likely next death (recommended)

**Best when:** Mark the most likely upcoming death when an exposed victim or credible execution case gives you a reasoned prediction.

**Action:** Reap a likely night victim or tomorrow’s likely execution target. With no reliable lead, mark an eligible unknown rather than spending the night predicting certainty.

**Target rule:** Prefer exposed leaders, credible killing threats, or a player facing a supported execution case. Avoid refreshing an existing permanent mark when a fresh candidate is available.

**Why:** You receive one passive Soul each night, and a Reaped player’s later death adds another. Unlike Baker, you want your marked target to die; a durable protected survivor is often a slower choice.

**Change plan when:** If the likely victim changes, Reap the new candidate. At seven Souls, switch immediately to Death’s survive-today objective.

**Tradeoff:** A protected or unexpectedly spared target may survive. Reap is not an attack and supplies no genuine Town investigation.

**Checkpoints:**

- Track passive and extra souls separately
- Reap a reasoned death candidate
- On transformation, focus on surviving the day

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Spread permanent marks onto likely deaths, checking your existing Reaped list first.
2. Track the actual Soul count and preserve a coherent cover until the transformation.
3. As Death, survive the transformation day rather than planning another Reap.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Mark tomorrow’s likely execution

**Best when:** A supported unresolved case makes a particular living player more likely to die by trial than any visible night victim.

**Action:** Reap that player tonight and preserve the mark; contribute ordinary public reasoning to the case tomorrow.

**Target rule:** Choose someone with a concrete contradiction, competing claim, or independently reported concern. Do not manufacture a guaranteed guilty merely because you marked them.

**Why:** A marked player’s qualifying death can add a Soul during the day as well as at night. A daytime case can be easier to predict than the killers’ next targets.

**Change plan when:** If Town accepts their explanation or the case collapses, mark another plausible future death. Existing marks persist, so refreshing the same survivor is usually unnecessary.

**Tradeoff:** Overpushing a framed target can reveal your interest, and changing investigation appearance does not prove the target is actually evil.

**Checkpoints:**

- Name the public reason they may die
- Keep Reap separate from a TI result
- Track the actual Soul count

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Spread marks rather than repeatedly selecting an existing one.
2. Do not expose yourself through exclusive focus on every marked player.
3. At transformation, stop planning Reap and survive Death’s current day.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-balance-changes-and-bug-fixes-patch-notes-r1-5-05/).

### Claim strategy

Oracle matches astral Reap’s lack of an ordinary visit most cleanly; Sheriff can pressure a genuinely framed appearance but invents a check and a visible visit. Veteran, Coroner and setup-legal Pilgrim are progressively weaker alternatives for the nonvisiting history. Reap never supplies Town investigation or protection. After becoming Death, the only immediate route is surviving the current day. There is no next Reap or ordinary nighttime ability to promise.

#### 1. Oracle (TP) — Astral footprint · no real protection

Reap is astral, so the lack of an ordinary numbered-player visit is compatible with Oracle’s role-targeted Aegis history.

- Write Aegis Sheriff or another legal Town role, not Aegis 4. The private Reap target and public role choice are different kinds of selection.
- Rotate the claimed role on consecutive nights and keep the Oracle self-role choice to its one permitted use.
- Do not call a marked player saved or identified. Reap adds a Soul opportunity and changes apparent investigation identity; it is not a Barrier.
- At the Death announcement, answer scrutiny using the existing role-choice timeline instead of inventing a final protective effect.

**Best when:** You want a quieter cover that does not promise a visible visit every night.

**Exposure/limits:** You grant no Aegis. Reap targets are player numbers, while Oracle chooses Town roles; confusing those methods exposes the claim.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Sheriff (TI) — Framing interaction · visit mismatch

Reap can make a marked player appear Suspicious, so some other Sheriff evidence may align with a pressure story rather than contradicting it immediately.

- Use only legal Innocent/Suspicious vocabulary and post a stable cover check promptly; a Reap selection is not itself a result.
- Expect a Tracker or Lookout to challenge the missing normal visit to your alleged Search target.
- A marked player can genuinely appear Suspicious without being evil. Do not describe the framing effect as an exact discovery.
- Avoid treating every mark as a mandatory accusation: your actual gain comes from later deaths, while overfocusing exposes the connection.

**Best when:** Public investigative evidence already supports questioning a marked player, and you understand that the apparent result is not their true faction.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no Sheriff check and do not make its normal visible visit. Repeated Suspicious reports on your marked players can reveal the framing plan.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Veteran (TK) — Stays home · no real Alert

Astral Reap leaves no ordinary visit, which fits a stay-at-home Veteran account better than claiming to walk to a house every night.

- Separate the private Reap target from the public home-based ability: Veteran does not Alert at another player’s house.
- Keep an accurate claimed three-charge Alert count and do not write Alert automatically on every night.
- Do not invent kills or visitor identities from Souls gained; a marked player can die elsewhere with no connection to your house.
- A quiet stay-home account is possible but weak. If real direct visitors report surviving an Alert, the movement fit cannot repair the missing attack.

**Best when:** You want a nonvisiting TK cover and have no public event requiring a real Alert demonstration.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot produce Alert attacks or a valid visitor-kill event. An unharmed confirmed direct visitor during a claimed Alert can expose the missing effect.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Veteran_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Coroner (TI) — Quiet N1 · later visit problem

The ordinary Coroner has no usable N1 autopsy, giving a plausible inactive opening without pretending Reap is a visible visit.

- Start with no usable N1 Autopsy; never name a killer comparison before an eligible body exists.
- Record a real eligible daytime corpse before alleging the corresponding night Examine.
- Do not turn the death of a marked player into a killer match. Soul gain tells you about your objective, not Coroner evidence.
- Expect later movement checks to expose the missing Examine visit; the inactive first night is a limited overlap, not a full-role imitation.

**Best when:** You need a plausible N1 and are willing to manage a much weaker later TI history.

**Exposure/limits:** Later Coroner Examine normally requires movement and actual corpse records, neither of which Reap supplies.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Coroner_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Pilgrim (Town Outlier) — Setup-restricted · no ability proof

A setup-legal Pilgrim expects no active night visit or result, fitting astral Reap’s observed inactivity without promising an effect you cannot demonstrate.

- Check roledeck eligibility before selecting this claim; common Town buckets do not automatically permit every Outlier.
- Use dated public observations and vote reasons rather than pretending Soul counts are Town information.
- Do not add a night target or special result to make the will look busier; Pilgrim has no such ability.
- When Death appears, your public reasoning needs to stand on its own because the cover cannot independently confirm you.

**Best when:** The roledeck explicitly allows Town Outliers and you prefer an observation-based account to fabricated powers.

**Exposure/limits:** Pilgrim must be possible in the deck. A bare no-ability claim supplies little trust and can become a convenient execution target.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pilgrim_(ToS2)).

## Doomsayer

NE — Neutral Evil.

### Route 1: Build a reliable three-player set (recommended)

**Best when:** Use the mixed evidence-first approach when no single faction supplies three reliable eligible identities. N1 is preparation only.

**Action:** Record player numbers and claims. You may prepare the interface, but cannot confirm Doom guesses on N1.

**Target rule:** Prioritize publicly demonstrated non-TI Town roles and strongly supported Coven identities; Coven is guessed by faction. Do not spend a slot on a Town Investigative claim as though TI were guessable.

**Why:** The entire three-player set must be correct. One guessed identity based on a hunch can spoil two sound entries, so collecting precise eligible claims is your main early job.

**Change plan when:** From N2, submit and confirm three legal guesses once the set is defensible. Replace a dead target or invalid category before confirming; use the actual guess menu for disputed Outlier eligibility.

**Tradeoff:** Waiting for certainty can leave too few survivors. A whole-set failure does not identify which individual guess was wrong.

**Checkpoints:**

- Collect exact eligible role evidence
- Check all three pairs and confirmation
- Distinguish a failed guess set from a known result

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Collect three living eligible identities with evidence stronger than an unsupported claim.
2. Submit all three guesses and explicitly confirm from the first legal night.
3. Update survivors every day; do not wait until fewer than three other players remain.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Doomsayer_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Build around demonstrated Town abilities

**Best when:** Public abilities identify several living, guessable non-TI Town roles more firmly than ordinary claims do.

**Action:** Record those demonstrated roles on N1 if available; from N2, combine them with enough other legal supported identities and confirm the three-player set.

**Target rule:** A visible Mayor reveal or genuine daytime demonstration is stronger role evidence than someone merely typing the role. Exclude TI and check the current guess menu.

**Why:** Exact Town guesses are easier when the role has publicly demonstrated itself. You reduce one kind of uncertainty while still needing all three entries correct together.

**Change plan when:** If demonstrations do not supply three living legal identities, supplement with supported Coven or eligible Neutral guesses rather than forcing three Town entries.

**Tradeoff:** Waiting for three public reveals can be too slow, and conversion or role changes complicate the category you should enter.

**Checkpoints:**

- Separate claims from demonstrations
- Keep all three targets alive and eligible
- Confirm only from a legal night

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Update exact current roles after known role changes.
2. Replace dead or invalid targets before confirming.
3. Act before the living population makes a legal set impossible.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Doomsayer_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Use well-supported Coven identities

**Best when:** Several living players have strong Coven evidence but their exact Coven roles remain unknown.

**Action:** Prepare those identities on N1; from N2 use the Coven faction category for supported Coven members and fill the remaining legal slots with your strongest evidence.

**Target rule:** Use more than a convenient accusation: consider incompatible Town accounts, real reveals, or multiple independent reports. A suspicious Sheriff result alone is not exact Coven identification.

**Why:** Coven guesses do not require the exact Coven role. That removes a precision burden without making a faction hunch reliable by itself.

**Change plan when:** Recheck an accused player who demonstrates a different legal identity; do not keep a wrong faction guess because it made the set look tidy.

**Tradeoff:** Publicly pushing these players may get them executed before you can guess them. A team association or vote pattern is still an inference.

**Checkpoints:**

- Use the correct faction input
- Avoid treating Suspicious as Coven-only
- Preserve evidence for all three guesses

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Watch for exposed Coven dying before your legal attempt.
2. Mix categories rather than insisting on a three-Coven set.
3. After a failed set, reconsider every uncertain entry.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Doomsayer_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Oracle is the quieter no-visible-visit starter; Deputy is short but carries immediate demonstration risk. Veteran, Coroner and Trapper provide other conditional accounts, with increasing effect or chronology obligations. Gather claims through normal questions rather than pretending your guesses produce Town information.

#### 1. Oracle (TP) — Astral footprint · role-choice history

Private Doom guesses do not require an ordinary player visit, matching an Oracle-style absence of visible travel.

- Claim legal Town role choices such as Aegis Sheriff, not protection on a numbered player.
- Rotate consecutive role choices and respect the once-only self-role option; keep Doom targets in separate private notes.
- Ask claim questions with an understandable public purpose rather than demanding only the three exact roles you want to guess.
- Do not treat a failed Doom set as three investigative checks or pretend you know which individual entry was wrong.

**Best when:** You want to collect exact claims without taking on a visible nightly visit history.

**Exposure/limits:** You provide no Aegis. Incorrect role targeting, consecutive role reuse or unsupported saves can expose the story.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Doomsayer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Deputy (TK) — No night log · shot-demand risk

Deputy has no normal night action, so an empty opening history does not require a fabricated visit while you prepare guesses.

- Write No night ability instead of giving your three Doom selections as night checks.
- A real Deputy shot is a visible daytime event; do not invent a hit or describe a claimed miss as confirmation.
- Base daytime questions on a consistent public case so exact-role collection does not stand out as your only contribution.
- If a justified shot request arrives, recognize the cover’s central weakness rather than adding fictitious ammunition or nighttime powers.

**Best when:** You want the simplest early will and accept a short shelf life if Town demands proof.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot demonstrate a daytime shot. A held-shot story can lose credibility quickly when a strong target appears.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Doomsayer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Veteran (TK) — Nonvisiting cover · Alert verification risk

Doom’s astral footprint is compatible with staying home, and a Veteran cover can justify having no selected-player visit list.

- Keep the three-player guess list entirely private; a Veteran log describes home Alerts, not targets you visited.
- Track at most the legal three Alert charges and avoid writing an Alert merely to explain every quiet night.
- Do not infer an Alert kill from someone dying elsewhere, and do not claim to know every visitor’s identity.
- A known direct visitor surviving a claimed Alert matters more than how tidy your will looks; the cover does not give you that attack.

**Best when:** You prefer a home-based cover and no existing event forces you to claim a visitor kill.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot create Alert attacks. Visitor survival and an implausible charge count can expose the account.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Doomsayer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Veteran_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Coroner (TI) — Inactive opening · corpse-timing burden

Coroner’s ordinary N1 lacks a usable autopsy, providing a plausible preparation night before any real TI results would be expected.

- Use N1: No usable autopsy and do not invent an opening killer match.
- Choose a real eligible corpse during the correct day before adding a related Examine line to the cover.
- A Doom guess being right or wrong says nothing about a Coroner killer comparison; keep these evidence types separate.
- From later nights, watch for movement contradictions and a growing demand for an informative report; early inactivity does not sustain the cover indefinitely.

**Best when:** You can track public corpses precisely and want a slower-starting TI cover.

**Exposure/limits:** Later autopsies and killer comparisons are entirely invented, and normal Examine movement differs from your guesses.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Doomsayer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Coroner_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Trapper (TP) — Build-first opening · missing trap effect

A first-night Build is nonvisiting, fitting N1 preparation without a fabricated player destination.

- Start with Build, never N1: Trap on 4 in an ordinary unmodified opening.
- Keep one active trap and a legal build/placement sequence in the account; not every night can be a fresh placement.
- Do not convert a target’s exact role claim into trap visitor-role feedback; the source of the information matters.
- A verified visit or harmful action at the alleged trapped house may demand an effect you cannot supply. Treat this as a lower-ranked conditional cover.

**Best when:** You understand the build/place chronology and accept that later trap evidence is difficult to reproduce.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot place or trigger a trap, and later claimed placement travel conflicts with continued astral guesses.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Doomsayer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trapper_(ToS2)).

## Executioner

NE — Neutral Evil.

### Route 1: Build a public-evidence case (recommended)

**Best when:** Use the quieter public-evidence case when your assigned target has not already made a strong, easily verifiable Town demonstration.

**Action:** Read your assigned target and prepare a cover that can support daytime pressure. You have no target-selection or attack button to use on N1.

**Target rule:** Follow your assigned player’s claim, votes and contradictions. With no genuine lead, ask a specific claim question on D2 instead of announcing an unsupported guaranteed guilty.

**Why:** You need that specific player executed on the stand while you are alive. A dramatic accusation that gets them shot or killed at night does not accomplish the same goal.

**Change plan when:** If the target dies outside a qualifying trial execution, read your new Jester card and complete its Chatterbox requirement before attempting a hanging.

**Tradeoff:** A slow case can lose the target to another killer, and your early Basic Defense ends at D3. You must still be alive for the qualifying execution.

**Checkpoints:**

- Learn the assigned target’s claim
- Build survivable day pressure
- Recognize and follow any Jester transition

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Get the assigned target’s claim and compare it with public evidence.
2. Build enough believable daytime pressure for a qualifying execution while staying alive.
3. If transformed into Jester, immediately switch from targeting them to meeting your own hanging prerequisite.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Executioner_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Take an active TI cover for earlier pressure

**Best when:** You understand the chosen TI role, can maintain its legal history, and the target already has a public inconsistency worth questioning.

**Action:** N1 has no real check: prepare a specific TI cover. On D2 post its legal account early, then use the existing contradiction to request the assigned target’s full explanation.

**Target rule:** Focus on the assigned target, but link pressure to something other players can assess. A fabricated Sheriff or Investigator result remains a cover claim, not something Executioner learned.

**Why:** An active report can move discussion faster than a passive TP cover. It also creates a movement and result history that the lobby can challenge.

**Change plan when:** If the target is convincingly demonstrated or you are being read as their Executioner, reassess the case instead of escalating into an impossible result. If they die outside execution, switch to Jester.

**Tradeoff:** This is higher upkeep than the default. Your assigned Town target normally has not been visited by you, and observers or independent checks can expose the invented investigation.

**Checkpoints:**

- Name your cover’s actual result rules
- Ask for a qualifying trial rather than a shot
- Stay alive through the target’s execution

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Maintain early posting and an unchanged target history.
2. Use public contradictions without pretending a claim gives certainty.
3. After a Jester conversion, check that day’s Chatterbox requirement immediately.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Executioner_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Oracle supports a quiet survival cover while ordinary questions build a case. Sheriff gives more immediate pressure at the cost of a fabricated check and missing visit. Investigator, Tracker and Coroner are progressively more demanding ways to attach a public case to your assigned target; none changes the requirement for a qualifying execution while you live.

#### 1. Oracle (TP) — Quiet survival · public case required

Your lack of an ordinary night action is compatible with Oracle’s astral footprint, letting the assigned-target case rest on public reasoning rather than a fake investigation.

- Use legal role-based Aegis entries, rotating consecutive roles and limiting self-role choice, while privately keeping the assigned target separate.
- Ask the target for a claim and a specific explanation. Cite what they actually said or did rather than pretending Oracle investigated them.
- Seek a trial, Tribunal execution or Prosecution while you are alive; a Deputy shot or night kill does not accomplish your objective.
- If the target dies another way, read the Jester conversion and check Chatterbox instead of continuing the obsolete execution case.

**Best when:** You can question real claim or vote contradictions without needing an invented immediate guilty result.

**Exposure/limits:** No Aegis is supplied and the role itself gives no evidence against your assigned target. Obsessive focus can expose Executioner.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Executioner_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Sheriff (TI) — Direct accusation · invented evidence

A Sheriff cover can give Town a compact report to question alongside the assigned target’s public account.

- Post the cover check near day start using Innocent or Suspicious, not an exact role or guaranteed faction.
- An Innocent line plus a later public contradiction is lower commitment than immediately asserting a fake guaranteed evil discovery.
- If you choose an invented Suspicious report, understand that it promises a normal visit and may force a direct dispute with a genuine Town target.
- Keep pressure directed toward qualifying execution, not shooting the target; do not assume every method of killing them wins.

**Best when:** You understand TI posting and can maintain a plausible check without relying entirely on a dramatic unsupported accusation.

**Exposure/limits:** You did not perform an actual check or its normal visible visit. The assigned target is normally Town, so an unframed Suspicious claim may conflict with real evidence.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Executioner_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Investigator (TI) — Crime narrative · detailed upkeep

A crime-report cover can create a narrower question about an attack or cross-faction visit instead of pretending to know an exact role.

- Use the cover’s crimes, not old Blood wording or a generic evil verdict: Murder concerns an attack that night and Trespassing a relevant opposing-faction visit.
- Keep the alleged target and posting time stable; an Executioner target assignment is not a checked player visit.
- Do not call an odd vote Murder or label a quiet target No Crime as proof of Town. Separate public reasoning from the invented ability line.
- Compare the target’s exact claimed actions and independent reports, then seek a qualifying execution while preserving your own survival.

**Best when:** A genuine public action conflict offers a concrete crime-related discussion and you understand the current Investigator.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no crime check and make no natural investigative visit. Murder and Trespassing are specific mechanics, not synonyms for suspicious behavior.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Executioner_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Investigator_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Tracker (TI) — Specific movement dispute · observer risk

A Tracker cover can focus Town on a claimed destination contradiction when the assigned target’s own account already leaves a concrete movement question.

- Describe outgoing movement from the followed player; visitors arriving at their house are Lookout information instead.
- Preserve one consistent alleged follow target and result. Executioner’s assigned target is not automatic tracking evidence.
- Do not turn no visible visit into guaranteed evil: many legal roles load, build, hold or act astrally.
- Use an actual conflict between their story and public evidence to request a trial; a fabricated destination on its own can become a one-for-one against you.

**Best when:** There is a supported target-history inconsistency, rather than only your desire to eliminate that player.

**Exposure/limits:** You learn no actual destinations and do not naturally visit the target. A second observer can disprove both your travel and the invented report.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Executioner_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tracker_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Coroner (TI) — Slow-start TI · actual corpse needed

Coroner allows an ordinary inactive N1 before a later corpse-based case, fitting your lack of a real opening night action.

- Open with no usable autopsy rather than naming your assigned target as a killer on N1.
- A related Examine line requires a real eligible Autopsy first. Record the corpse and day before alleging the night comparison.
- Do not assume a target’s awkward defense establishes a killer match; the cover promises an actual mechanical comparison.
- A slow cover risks the target dying elsewhere before trial. Reassess each day and switch fully to Jester if conversion occurs.

**Best when:** You plan a slower case and can maintain exact public corpse chronology without inventing an N1 result.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot manufacture a valid autopsy or killer match, and your assigned Town target may have no compatible connection to the death.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Executioner_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Coroner_(ToS2)).

## Jester

NE — Neutral Evil.

### Route 1: Create modest plausible suspicion (recommended)

**Best when:** Use modest, plausible suspicion when Town is willing to trial weak claims. Complete the displayed Chatterbox requirement before judgment.

**Action:** Prepare a plausible weak claim or a modest inconsistency. You have no ordinary N1 night action and no D1 Chatterbox quota to manufacture.

**Target rule:** On D2, make the displayed number of qualifying public messages early, then engage with a supported case against you. With no pressure, answer claim requests imperfectly rather than refusing all participation.

**Why:** The win requires a trial execution after completing your quota. Obvious Jester behavior can make Town ignore you, while silence from D2 onward can invalidate the win or cause boredom death.

**Change plan when:** If someone threatens a day shot or night kill, offer enough plausible Town value to steer them toward a trial instead. Check the quota again each day.

**Tradeoff:** An obvious Jester performance can get ignored or shot. A night death, ordinary day shot, or hanging without the required quota does not win.

**Checkpoints:**

- Meet the active communication quota
- Reach a valid hanging rather than a night death
- Use only an actually enabled Haunt

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Complete the current day’s actual Chatterbox requirement before judgement.
2. Create believable doubt that makes a trial preferable to ignoring or shooting you.
3. After a qualifying hanging, select a legal Haunt target; the Haunt kill itself is not needed for your win.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jester_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Start credible; become the unresolved claim later

**Best when:** The lobby is quick to shoot obvious Jesters or currently has a stronger execution target than you.

**Action:** Prepare a coherent Town cover on N1. On D2 meet Chatterbox early, answer normal questions and contribute a little useful public reasoning; allow a weak explanation or unresolved claim to become the later trial case.

**Target rule:** Use a cover you actually understand. A quiet Deputy account is easier to maintain initially than an invented chain of exact investigations.

**Why:** Looking somewhat useful reduces the incentive to remove you by a day shot or night attack. You can become the plausible false claim once stronger cases are resolved.

**Change plan when:** If you become too trusted and are never considered for trial, allow a modest inconsistency rather than claiming impossible N1 powers. If a shot is threatened, make the trial route seem worth resolving.

**Tradeoff:** Delaying gives killers more time to remove you, and your early Basic Defense expires at D3. It is a conditional alternative, not a safer guaranteed win.

**Checkpoints:**

- Meet today’s quota before the trial gets urgent
- Keep enough credibility to avoid being dismissed as Jester
- Become unresolved rather than publicly confirmed

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Recheck Chatterbox every day, including after an Executioner conversion.
2. Build just enough doubt for a qualifying execution.
3. After the successful hanging, choose a legal Haunt target; Haunt is the reward, not a second victory requirement.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jester_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Offer a believable but disputed TI report

**Best when:** The lobby uses early TI posts to choose trials, and you understand the report format well enough to avoid an impossible result.

**Action:** Use N1 to prepare a Sheriff-style cover; there is no real Jester check. Post a legal-looking result early on D2, complete Chatterbox, and let a limited contradiction make your account questionable.

**Target rule:** A routine Innocent result on an unresolved player is a lower-commitment opening than an unsupported guaranteed evil accusation. Ordinary public disagreement can create suspicion without claiming impossible knowledge.

**Why:** A disputed report gives Town a concrete account to evaluate. Your objective is a plausible false-claim trial, not maintaining perfect investigative credibility forever.

**Change plan when:** If Town identifies the performance as Jester or chooses to shoot you, offer a coherent explanation and steer toward resolving the account by trial rather than escalating the absurdity.

**Tradeoff:** TI upkeep is higher, and a very obvious lie may convince Town to avoid hanging you. The claim cannot force another player’s verdict.

**Checkpoints:**

- Use a legal result vocabulary
- Do not skip Chatterbox while arguing
- Keep the desired death route a trial execution

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Keep old entries unchanged so suspicion concerns the story rather than random nonsense.
2. Avoid starting cases that permanently remove every reason to trial you.
3. Use the eligible Haunt menu only after the qualifying execution.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jester_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Deputy and Sheriff stay the two starter choices: each can become a plausibly unresolved Town claim without demanding impossible powers immediately. Lower options are ways to shape suspicion, not ways to become perfectly believed. Meet Chatterbox first, avoid looking like an obvious Jester, and prefer a trial over a day shot or night death.

#### 1. Deputy (TK) — Hanging setup · demonstration tension

Deputy has no night action to fake, while a reluctance or inability to take a justified daytime shot can make your Town claim look unresolved.

- Write No night ability and claim TK when appropriate; do not invent a nightly Deputy check.
- At first, hold the supposed shot for an understandable evidence reason. Immediate absurd excuses can advertise Jester rather than a plausible fake.
- When pressure grows, let the missing demonstration create doubt while offering enough coherent discussion to make a trial feel useful.
- Complete the displayed Chatterbox requirement before judgment. If a real shot threatens you, steer discussion toward trial resolution because a shot does not satisfy your goal.

**Best when:** You want minimal night writing and the lobby is willing to trial weak TK claims instead of automatically shooting them.

**Exposure/limits:** If Town identifies the Jester plan, it may ignore you or shoot instead. A stated miss does not prove Deputy or guarantee a hanging.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jester_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Sheriff (TI) — Hanging setup · limited report dispute

A simple Innocent/Suspicious account can create a believable report conflict that makes Town evaluate you as a possible false claimant.

- Use a legal-looking format such as N1: Search 4 — Innocent; Jester itself did not obtain that check.
- Innocent is not a Town clear. A limited disagreement or weak explanation can generate doubt without an impossible exact-role accusation.
- Post near day start, then keep the same history. A modest inconsistency is easier to mistake for a real false claim than repeated rewrites.
- Meet Chatterbox while discussing the report and seek a qualifying execution; dying at night over your accusation still loses.

**Best when:** You understand one-person TI results and can post early without turning the cover into random nonsense.

**Exposure/limits:** An impossible or theatrical result can make the Jester objective obvious. Independent investigators may instead clear the account or encourage a different death route.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jester_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Cleric (TP) — Quiet beginning · weak proof later

A simple Barrier list can let you appear useful initially, then become an unresolved protection claimant when Town compares target choices.

- Use a modest Barrier target account instead of claiming a dramatic save; there is no real Jester protection feedback.
- Expect movement observers to challenge a visible visit you did not make. A quiet survivor list does not independently verify the role.
- Answer why each supposed protection target was chosen with a short plausible reason; let the account be imperfect without becoming deliberately impossible.
- If Town never questions you, an unresolved choice can create doubt later, but keep meeting Chatterbox and avoid inviting a night kill.

**Best when:** You want the credible-until-pressure route and understand that weak proof may need time to produce a trial.

**Exposure/limits:** Jester normally made no visit and supplied no shield. A completely impossible save can expose the performance as Jester rather than persuade Town to hang.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jester_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Psychic (TI) — More discussion · high format burden

An early group report creates several natural questions and can produce a disputed claim that Town considers worth trialing.

- Use N1 Good with two names, then N2 Evil with three; each means at least one apparent match, not exactly one.
- Include a selected target in the story, remembering that Jester did not actually make the promised visit.
- Post the whole group early and keep old lists. A believable dispute is more useful for your objective than claiming five names or identifying an exact evil through a vision.
- Complete Chatterbox during the discussion and keep the desired outcome a trial; a noisy report can otherwise attract a shot or night attack.

**Best when:** You know current Psychic parity and want a more active route than waiting for a quiet TK claim to be challenged.

**Exposure/limits:** Jester has no real vision or selected-player visit. Wrong group sizes or an obviously impossible vision can advertise Jester and make Town refuse the execution.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jester_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Vigilante (TK) — Inactive N1 · delayed shot pressure

A claimed N1 Load fits your lack of ordinary opening movement, then later inability to produce a justified shot can create suspicion.

- Open with N1: Load; do not claim an opening shot, because ordinary Vigilante must load first.
- A later held shot should have a plausible evidence reason rather than being an automatic refusal every night.
- Do not treat someone else’s kill as your own demonstrated shot. Death attribution and independent visits can expose the gap.
- Let justified demonstration pressure make you unresolved, while avoiding a demand that a real Town killer solve you by shooting instead of trial.

**Best when:** You prefer a slower TK pressure route and can keep the load/shot sequence mechanically legal.

**Exposure/limits:** You have no gun. Impossible N1 shots, unlimited ammo or a fabricated public kill can make the Jester plan too conspicuous.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jester_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vigilante_(ToS2)).

#### 6. Veteran (TK) — Home-history fit · easily overplayed

Jester’s nonvisiting opening can fit a home-based Veteran history, with suspicion emerging from implausible Alert decisions or missing proof.

- Write only home-based held/Alert entries; Veteran never Alerts at another player’s numbered house.
- Keep the three-charge count legal and avoid using Alert as an explanation for every unrelated death.
- Do not invite a mass visit merely to look suspicious. It may identify the act as Jester or cause a nonqualifying night death.
- Use ordinary claim questions and a weak defense to create trial pressure, keeping Chatterbox complete before the verdict phase.

**Best when:** You understand the three-Alert limit and can keep the account understated rather than loudly advertising a Jester bait.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot shoot visitors, and obvious fake bait can warn Town not to hang you. Real hostile visitors may kill you instead.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Jester_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Veteran_(ToS2)).

## Pirate

NE — Neutral Evil.

### Route 1: Verify targets with Scour (recommended)

**Best when:** Use the private Scour-first approach until you know which specific players, rather than merely which original role labels, are your Landlubbers.

**Action:** Scour an eligible unknown. You cannot Plunder on N1, so your opening value is locating one of the three assigned players.

**Target rule:** Prefer a player whose claimed original role matches one of your target-role hints. With no matching claim, Scour an unknown you have not checked; duplicate roles mean the hint does not prove which player is yours.

**Why:** Killing a non-Landlubber with Plunder removes all remaining Plunders. An early verification is more useful than gambling your limited attacks on a role claim alone.

**Change plan when:** Once a living Landlubber is confirmed, consider Plunder from N2 if Basic Attack can reasonably kill them. If defense or protection makes that unlikely, keep Scouring or pursue their daytime execution.

**Tradeoff:** Scouring quietly takes time, and Basic Plunder may fail against defense. A role hint does not identify the correct holder when duplicates exist.

**Checkpoints:**

- Get a real Scour result
- Identify objective targets before spending Plunder
- Track charges and whether Plunder remains enabled

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Identify actual target players through Scour instead of equating role hints with confirmation.
2. Spend Plunder on a confirmed, plausibly killable Landlubber.
3. Track remaining target players through role changes and survive until the last one dies.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pirate_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Seek daytime cooperation after checking your targets

**Best when:** Your Town Landlubber is already dead or the remaining verified target is a shared threat, and disclosure is more useful than preserving a weak Town cover.

**Action:** N1 still begins with Scour, not Plunder. After verified information supports cooperation, consider disclosing the Pirate objective and seeking a qualifying target’s ordinary daytime death.

**Target rule:** Name only a target you actually verified or can support publicly. Explain the difference between an original-role hint and a confirmed Landlubber player.

**Why:** Any death of the actual Landlubber can advance your objective. A vote can be more effective than spending Basic Plunder into known defense.

**Change plan when:** If cooperation will expose a Town Landlubber or identify you to a dangerous remaining target, retain the Town cover and continue private verification instead.

**Tradeoff:** Claiming Pirate does not oblige Town to spare you, and a remaining Landlubber may attack you. This is an optional disclosure route, not a suggested Coven or Neutral public-will template.

**Checkpoints:**

- Verify the actual player marker
- Assess who benefits from disclosure
- Survive until the final Landlubber dies

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Track the same target players through role changes.
2. Spend Plunder only when its limited charges have real expected value.
3. Reassess safety when your early Basic Defense ends at D3.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pirate_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is the low-upkeep Scour cover; Psychic gives more influence but requires invented group reports. Sheriff, Tracker and Bodyguard are lower alternatives with different information or protection promises. Scour only identifies your assigned Landlubbers, and a later Plunder changes the consequences of the same visit.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Simple Scour account · no shield

A real Scour visit can support the destination of a Barrier claim while you privately verify assigned target players.

- Use the actual Scour destination as the alleged Barrier destination; do not choose the public target after seeing who survived.
- Write only the claimed action on quiet nights. Learning Landlubber is not a saved or attacked message.
- When switching to Plunder, check the new death evidence against the protective story; the same destination does not mean the same effect.
- Keep original-role hints separate from player confirmation. A role claimed by two people does not tell you which one is your assigned target.

**Best when:** You want a short one-target history while verifying targets rather than inventing daily investigations.

**Exposure/limits:** Scour is not protection. Plunder can kill at that same house, and a false save or exposed Landlubber objective can unravel the account.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pirate_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Psychic (TI) — Discussion influence · no real vision

The Scour destination can be the selected member of a claimed vision, combining a real visit with an active daytime story.

- Include the actual Scour target as the selected person in the cover vision, not an unrelated player selected at dawn.
- Maintain N1 Good/two and N2 Evil/three, each with at least one apparent category match rather than exactly one.
- Do not present your original-role hint as proof of a name in the group; the hint does not identify the correct holder by itself.
- Keep Plunder nights, published visions and observed travel consistent, recognizing that none of your Pirate feedback generates a genuine vision.

**Best when:** You can maintain early daily posts and group logic while keeping the Landlubber search private.

**Exposure/limits:** Scour provides no Good/Evil group. Role hints, duplicate role holders and later Plunder deaths can contradict a fabricated vision history.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pirate_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Sheriff (TI) — Simple TI line · wrong information type

A Scour target can be your alleged Search target, giving a compact public check account attached to a real visit.

- Use the actual Scour route in the claimed check history so movement evidence does not immediately contradict it.
- Keep only Innocent/Suspicious wording; neither Landlubber found nor not Landlubber maps directly to either result.
- Post the cover check promptly and preserve old entries instead of rewriting them when target-role hints become clearer.
- A suspicious report can encourage a daytime death that helps your objective, but if based only on Landlubber status it is invented evidence, not a real Sheriff finding.

**Best when:** You prefer one-person TI reports over vision groups and understand that Scour cannot supply those reports.

**Exposure/limits:** Landlubber status is not Innocent or Suspicious. An assigned target can belong to either Town or an opposing faction.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pirate_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Tracker (TI) — Destination story · no tracking feedback

A real Scour visit can fit the person you say you followed, and public claim comparisons may give a reason to question their movement.

- Follow the actual Scour target in the public account, distinguishing their outgoing movement from people visiting their house.
- Do not invent a destination merely because their hinted role usually visits; a role can hold, fail, redirect or act differently.
- No visible visit is a limited movement statement, not proof of Town, evil or Landlubber status.
- Before claiming a report about a Plunder target, consider that killing them can remove the person who would explain the action but leaves graveyard and observer evidence behind.

**Best when:** A concrete public destination conflict already exists and you are comfortable with the higher report burden.

**Exposure/limits:** You learn no destinations from Scour. Original-role hints are not proof of what that player did tonight.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pirate_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tracker_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Bodyguard (TP) — Quiet guard history · missing interception

Scour can use the same one-person route as Guard, requiring no routine investigative result on a quiet night.

- Claim Guard on your real Scour destination, not on a convenient surviving public role chosen after resolution.
- Do not label Scour success as an interception or a save; it identifies an assigned target only.
- Check the actual cause if your alleged guarded player dies. Bodyguard is not an all-purpose defense shield.
- Switching to Plunder changes the action even if the route stays the same; never imply a Pirate kill demonstrated Bodyguard.

**Best when:** You want another simple visiting TP cover and no real interception event must be explained.

**Exposure/limits:** You intercept no harmful visitor, and Plunder is itself an attack rather than a guard. Resident deaths or counterattack evidence can expose the difference.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pirate_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

## Arsonist

NK — Neutral Killing.

### Route 1: Build living Douse coverage (recommended)

**Best when:** Use active Douse while safe living opponents remain undoused and no current ignition would produce a decisive swing.

**Action:** Douse one eligible opponent likely to remain alive. Unless already Doused or facing an exceptional immediate finish, use the first night to build coverage.

**Target rule:** Prefer a durable, useful opponent who is not already Doused. With no claims, pick an eligible unknown outside obvious visiting danger. Defense alone does not prevent Douse.

**Why:** Your payoff depends on living Doused opponents when you Ignite. A player likely to die elsewhere tonight is usually a lower-value investment than an unpressured survivor.

**Change plan when:** If you are Doused, consider taking no action to Clean before an ignition can kill you. Ignite when existing Douses can remove decisive threats or votes; do not delay forever chasing perfect coverage.

**Tradeoff:** Each direct Douse creates movement evidence, and delaying Ignite too long lets already Doused opponents die elsewhere or execute you.

**Checkpoints:**

- Build a real Douse set
- Track your own Doused risk
- Ignite for value with timing understood

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Add living non-Doused opponents while tracking passive Douses from visitors.
2. Clean yourself when actually Doused and survival outweighs another setup action.
3. Ignite when the living Doused set creates a decisive threat removal or winning endgame.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Arsonist_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Clean when you are actually Doused

**Best when:** The client says another Arsonist or a redirection Doused you, and an ignition could end your game.

**Action:** Choose neither Douse nor Ignite to Clean yourself tonight; keep the missing direct visit consistent with your public account.

**Target rule:** This is a self-survival choice, not a selected-player target. Do not idle for cleaning value when you have no gasoline to remove.

**Why:** Your own Unstoppable ignition can kill you if you are Doused. Removing that immediate danger can outweigh one more setup target.

**Change plan when:** Once clean, return to Douse or a justified Ignite. Cleaning does not prevent you from being Doused again later.

**Tradeoff:** Cleaning costs a setup or kill night and does not make you safe from votes or all attacks.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm an actual self-Douse
- Leave both active modes unselected
- Update the movement account

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Check your own gasoline state before every Ignite.
2. Track living Doused opponents rather than historical selections.
3. Choose ignition timing from the current vote and threat map.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Arsonist_(ToS2)).

### Route 3: Ignite early for a decisive removal

**Best when:** Your existing Doused set already contains an immediate execution organizer, dangerous killer, or enough opposing votes to change the endgame.

**Action:** Ignite the existing Doused opponents instead of adding another direct Douse. This is exceptional on the opening night and requires actual existing coverage.

**Target rule:** Count living Doused players you actually know about and check that you are clean. New visitors passively Doused tonight do not burn in the same ignition.

**Why:** A smaller well-timed ignition can save your position; perfect coverage is useless if you die before using it.

**Change plan when:** If the visible Doused set does not justify the action, keep building rather than treating an early ignition as inherently stronger.

**Tradeoff:** You may burn a small set and leave yourself without leverage. Passive Douses are not a complete visitor list, and unseen Douses belonging to another Arsonist complicate certainty.

**Checkpoints:**

- Count real living coverage
- Check that you are not Doused
- Identify the vote or threat swing

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Recheck which threats survived the resolution.
2. Rebuild coverage only where it changes the next decision.
3. Keep direct Douse and astral Ignite nights distinct in your cover history.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Arsonist_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is the easiest active-Douse cover; Oracle is conditional on genuinely nonvisiting Clean or astral Ignite nights. Bodyguard, Psychic, Sheriff and Trickster are lower alternatives for direct Douse routes. One cover has to survive your changing movement modes, not merely fit N1.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Direct Douse fit · no real protection

An active Douse is a one-person visit, allowing a short Barrier account without inventing daily investigative results.

- Keep the claimed Barrier destination equal to your actual direct Douse destination when travel may be observed.
- Do not add passive Douse visitors as extra protection targets: those are contacts at your house, not visits you made.
- A Doused target surviving is not save feedback. If they die, compare the real cause instead of claiming any chosen target must survive.
- Before Clean or Ignite, check whether the claimed role would still visit. Repeating a fictional Barrier destination on a nonvisiting night creates a new weakness.

**Best when:** You are actively Dousing and want one manageable destination line per night.

**Exposure/limits:** Douse grants no protection. Cleaning or Igniting changes your movement, and actual attack or fire evidence may contradict the cover.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Arsonist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Oracle (TP) — Nonvisit mode fit · earlier Douse risk

Clean and astral Ignite do not require an ordinary numbered-player visit, matching an Oracle-style absence of travel better than an active-Douse cover does.

- Use role choices such as Aegis Sheriff rather than Aegis 4; Oracle targets Town role categories, not player numbers.
- Rotate consecutive claimed roles and limit the Oracle self-role option to its permitted use.
- Keep actual Clean and Ignite nights private and do not invent protection feedback from fire deaths or surviving Doused players.
- Audit earlier Douse travel before choosing this cover. A good fit tonight does not erase a visible visit from N1.

**Best when:** Your actual recent actions are nonvisiting, with no observed direct Douse history already contradicting the account.

**Exposure/limits:** Direct Douse nights do not match Oracle’s astral role targeting, and neither Clean nor Ignite grants an Aegis.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Arsonist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Bodyguard (TP) — Simple direct route · no interception

An active Douse can match the destination of a quiet Guard entry, avoiding the report volume of an information claim.

- Use the real direct Douse target as the claimed Guard target; preserve the selection before the morning outcome.
- Keep quiet nights quiet. Passive Dousing of a visitor to you is not Bodyguard killing an attacker at somebody else’s house.
- Do not invent a vest or sacrifice outcome to repair your survival; compare actual events with what a Bodyguard could do.
- Recheck the account when you Clean or Ignite, because the ordinary guard visit no longer matches those modes.

**Best when:** You want a second simple visiting TP story and understand interception is different from a general shield.

**Exposure/limits:** You never intercept the attacker. Clean/Ignite nights and a resident’s incompatible death can expose the missing guard action.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Arsonist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Psychic (TI) — More influence · fabricated group results

A direct Douse target can be the chosen person in a claimed vision, giving the cover one real movement anchor.

- Include the real Douse destination as the selected vision member and keep it distinct from passive visitors to your own house.
- Use N1 Good/two names and N2 Evil/three, with at least one apparent match rather than an exact identification.
- Do not use the Doused set as a fake evil list; gasoline says nothing about faction.
- Plan how the claimed visit history handles a later Clean or Ignite. A fabricated successful vision can add a movement contradiction on those nights.

**Best when:** You understand parity and can post early while your current plan still makes direct visits.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no group vision, and your nonvisiting Clean/Ignite nights cannot automatically reproduce Psychic’s selected visit.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Arsonist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Sheriff (TI) — Compact TI cover · no check supplied

Direct Douse can share an Search destination, giving a simpler report vocabulary than a Psychic group claim.

- Name the actual direct Douse target in each alleged Search entry where that movement overlap exists.
- Use Innocent/Suspicious only. Neither being Doused nor passively visiting you determines the correct result.
- Do not present Innocent as a Town clear or your private defense as investigative confirmation.
- When you Clean or Ignite, do not keep copying a successful visiting check as though nothing changed; the cover must explain the whole history.

**Best when:** You prefer simple early TI posts and are prepared to keep the movement and result timeline stable.

**Exposure/limits:** Dousing gives no suspicion check, and later no-visit modes conflict with an ordinary visiting Sheriff routine.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Arsonist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 6. Trickster (TK) — Mirror setup story · unsupported Unleash

A direct Douse route can resemble placing Magic Mirror on a player, creating a quiet setup account without routine TI reports.

- Use Magic Mirror on the actual Douse destination to preserve the observed route.
- Do not claim an Absorb because a person lived through the night; you know their gasoline status, not Trickster feedback.
- An Unleash requires an absorbed attack and replaces mirror placement. A mass Ignite is not that one borrowed attack.
- Expect Clean and Ignite to change the movement account; an initial mirror destination is only a partial fit.

**Best when:** Your visible activity is still direct setup visits and you understand the mirror/absorption sequence.

**Exposure/limits:** Douse absorbs no attack. A claimed Unleash needs a prior real absorption and cannot be manufactured merely from your Ignite deaths.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Arsonist_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trickster_(ToS2)).

## Serial Killer

NK — Neutral Killing.

### Route 1: Controlled attack with Cautious on (recommended)

**Best when:** Use Cautious on for a controlled opening attack when avoiding an exposed roleblocker counterkill is worth more than a possible bonus kill.

**Action:** If attacks are enabled, attack an apparently unprotected opponent. My beginner default is Cautious on: keep your intended attack while avoiding an automatic roleblocker counterkill that can expose your route.

**Target rule:** With no role evidence, pick an eligible unknown outside obvious Veteran bait. Do not interpret a previous Basic-attack survivor as an easy repeat target.

**Why:** Actual kills build Bloodlust; a night without a kill resets progress. A simple killable target is generally a better opener than a likely defended role or crowded protection call.

**Change plan when:** Turn Cautious off when the potential extra roleblocker kill is worth its exposure and counterattack risks. At Apex, reassess likely visitor traffic for a valuable rampage; obey Grace Period if attacks are disabled.

**Tradeoff:** You give up possible extra kills from non-Cautious retaliation. Failed kills still interrupt Bloodlust progress.

**Checkpoints:**

- Make a legal attack with an intentional toggle
- Track real kill progression
- Adapt to defense and retaliation risks

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Secure consecutive actual kills rather than repeatedly hitting known defense.
2. At Apex, select a useful opponent likely to draw additional visitors.
3. Recheck Cautious each night based on known blockers, exposure and surviving threats.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Serial_Killer_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Turn Cautious off for counterkill potential

**Best when:** A blocker is likely to target you and the extra kill is worth the public exposure and protective-counterattack risk.

**Action:** Disable Cautious, then choose your ordinary attack target if attacks are enabled. Turning Cautious off does not replace that selected attack.

**Target rule:** Keep the main attack aimed at a plausibly killable opponent. Evaluate the suspected blocker separately; protection on them may make retaliation dangerous.

**Why:** A successful counterkill can add Bloodlust progress and remove a disruptor. The distinctive death and destroyed will can also direct attention toward you.

**Change plan when:** Return to Cautious when preserving a plausible identity matters more than the expected extra kill, or when the blocker has credible protection.

**Tradeoff:** Retaliation is not a guaranteed safe kill. Source descriptions disagree about its exact power; do not base this route on defeating a particular shield or Invincible role.

**Checkpoints:**

- Check the actual Cautious setting
- Keep the main target deliberate
- Weigh exposure against a bonus kill

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Count actual kills and the client’s current Bloodlust state.
2. Use Apex traffic only when the current attack tier is active.
3. Reassess known blockers, protection and observers each night.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Serial_Killer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Keywords_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is the lower-upkeep direct-visit fallback. Vigilante has an attractive later kill story but conflicts with N1 attacks, loading, ammo and death attribution. Bodyguard, Psychic and Investigator are lower options with specific missing effects or information. Cautious changes retaliation, not your selected attack.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Simple visit story · no Barrier

A normal attack has one destination, which can match a claimed Barrier route without a daily invented information list.

- Keep the alleged Barrier destination compatible with the actual attack route rather than selecting a convenient survivor afterward.
- Do not label a failed attack or surviving defended target a successful save. The feedback describes your attack, not Cleric protection.
- Choose Cautious deliberately: sparing roleblockers reduces one distinctive exposure route but does not remove your ordinary attack visit.
- At Apex, multiple deaths or Rampage visitors can exceed what a single protection story explains. Reassess instead of copying another quiet Barrier line.

**Best when:** You need a compact direct-visit account and accept that the ability match is weak.

**Exposure/limits:** You supply no protection. The resident’s death, counterkill feedback and later Apex Rampage can expose the actual harmful effect.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Serial_Killer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Vigilante (TK) — Later shooting story · timing mismatch

A later direct attack can be described as a shot more naturally than a protection event, particularly when a target was already publicly questioned.

- Open the cover with Load, never a normal N1 shot; a real N1 attack is therefore an immediate chronological weakness.
- Track legal loading and shot opportunities rather than claiming unlimited consecutive fire.
- Do not describe SK-labelled deaths or counterkills as demonstrated Vigilante shots; the public evidence matters more than the wording.
- A Town-hit story needs the real gun-loss consequence, while an Apex Rampage cannot be explained as a routine one-target bullet.

**Best when:** You understand the full load/shot timeline and no established N1 movement or kill already defeats it.

**Exposure/limits:** Serial Killer can attack N1 while a real Vigilante must Load first. Death labels, ammo, Town-hit penalties and Rampage distinguish the roles.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Serial_Killer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vigilante_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Bodyguard (TP) — Short target log · wrong harmful effect

A normal direct attack can occupy the same route as an alleged Guard visit and avoids invented group information.

- Use the actual attack destination as the alleged guarded house if observers can trace where you went.
- Do not claim an interception merely because another visitor died; SK retaliation and Apex Rampage have different causes.
- A Bodyguard is not a general shield, so keep any resident-death explanation tied to the actual cause and direct harmful-visitor evidence.
- Your natural defense does not demonstrate a vest or a protected counterattack. Avoid inventing extra feedback to explain survival.

**Best when:** A straightforward visiting TP story fits your movement better than a no-visit cover.

**Exposure/limits:** You intercept nobody and are the source of harm. Resident deaths, protection evidence and retaliation can expose that difference.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Serial_Killer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Psychic (TI) — Public influence · invented visions

One direct attack destination can be the selected member of a vision, supplying a route anchor for an active daytime cover.

- Use the actual visited player as the selected member where the claimed vision is successful; do not rewrite the selection after the death reveal.
- Keep N1 Good/two and N2 Evil/three names with at least one apparent category match, not an exact culprit.
- Neither surviving your Basic attack nor dying to your attack tells you whether someone should fit a Good/Evil vision.
- Post old groups consistently and keep counterkill or Bloodlust knowledge private; none of it is Psychic feedback.

**Best when:** You can maintain legal early visions and prefer daytime influence to a quiet unverified TP list.

**Exposure/limits:** You get no real vision and may be including your own killed target. Later group logic, other investigators and kill evidence can expose the account.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Serial_Killer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Investigator (TI) — Crime-based discussion · no real investigation

The one-player direct route can fit an alleged Investigate visit, while public attack discussions create a natural subject for a crime report.

- Use the actual attack destination as the alleged investigated player when movement must align.
- Do not equate your kill succeeding with that target having Murder: Murder concerns an attack the checked player made, not merely a death at their house.
- Use current crime wording rather than an exact role reveal or outdated Blood claim; No Crime is not a Town clear.
- Compare your own action with independent Investigator or Coroner evidence before leaning on the cover. The target route alone does not hide your attack.

**Best when:** You know the current Murder/Trespassing mechanics and accept a more demanding active report history.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no target crime result. Your own harmful action may produce Murder evidence against you rather than validate the claimed investigation.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Serial_Killer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Investigator_(ToS2)).

## Shroud

NK — Neutral Killing.

### Route 1: Control a direct attack (recommended)

**Best when:** Use a direct Attack when you lack a reliable visiting read and want control over the intended victim.

**Action:** With no reliable visiting claim, use a direct attack on an apparently unprotected unknown if attacks are enabled. This gives you control over the intended victim.

**Target rule:** For a direct attack, avoid obvious Veteran bait and crowded protection calls. For Shroud, favor a credible multi-visitor or a visiting role whose likely destination threatens your survival.

**Why:** Shrouding an unknown visitor can send the attack somewhere unhelpful or revert to attacking that player. Its higher upside is easier to use once you know a target’s likely destinations.

**Change plan when:** Switch to Shroud when a known visitor offers stronger reach or avoids a dangerous direct route. Do not assume every astral or nonvisiting target will carry an attack elsewhere.

**Tradeoff:** Your visible direct visit is easier to trace. Basic Attack still fails against suitable defense or protection.

**Checkpoints:**

- Choose mode for a specific victim plan
- Distinguish carrier from victim
- Use actual feedback before repeating the plan

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Remove a plausibly killable threat with a controlled direct attack when information is sparse.
2. Exploit credible multi-visit roles for additional Shroud attack opportunities.
3. Reassess defense, protection and actual movement after each failed or redirected kill.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Shroud_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Shroud a credible useful visitor

**Best when:** A known visiting role has predictable useful destinations; a credible multi-visitor offers especially strong potential.

**Action:** Select Shroud on that visitor, aiming to send attacks through their actual direct visits rather than choosing the final victim yourself.

**Target rule:** Prefer a credible direct visitor, not Oracle or another astral-only action. A Seer can visit more than one house, but a role claim alone does not guarantee what they do tonight.

**Why:** One carrier can reach destinations you would rather avoid visiting directly and may produce more than one attack. That upside depends on the carrier actually making the expected visits.

**Change plan when:** If the carrier holds, is blocked, acts astrally, or changes destination, reassess from feedback; the ability can fall back to attacking the carrier.

**Tradeoff:** Shroud visibility and fallback behavior remain inconsistent in the source. A carrier may be protected, nonvisiting, redirected, or sent to an unhelpful target.

**Checkpoints:**

- Identify a real direct-visit route
- Keep carrier and final victims separate
- Avoid assuming guaranteed astral safety

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Compare actual deaths and movement reports.
2. Change carriers when their route is no longer useful.
3. Return to direct Attack when predictability matters more than speculative reach.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Shroud_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Seer_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is the straightforward direct-Attack starter; Oracle is conditional on genuinely astral Shroud behavior. Tracker can exploit a known carrier route but receives no complete report. Psychic, Vigilante and Bodyguard are lower choices. Shroud’s source-flagged visibility inconsistency prevents a blanket claim of safe invisible movement.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Direct-Attack fit · mode-dependent

A direct Attack makes the one-player visit a Barrier account would require, offering a simple target history.

- Use the direct Attack destination as the alleged Barrier target rather than naming a different survivor after resolution.
- Keep a quiet entry separate from save feedback; a failed Basic attack does not demonstrate a Barrier.
- When switching to Shroud, distinguish the carrier you select from the people the carrier visits. Your old direct-visit story may no longer fit.
- Treat a resident’s death as evidence to examine, not something a polished will can erase. You never supplied Cleric defense.

**Best when:** You are using direct Attack and want the lowest report upkeep among plausible visiting covers.

**Exposure/limits:** You give no shield. Shroud mode, fallback travel and actual victim deaths can conflict with the protective story.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Shroud_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Oracle (TP) — Astral-mode fit · fallback uncertainty

An actually astral Shroud action can fit a cover without an ordinary numbered-player visit, unlike your direct Attack mode.

- Claim role-based Aegis choices rather than a numbered carrier or victim as the protected target.
- Rotate consecutive role choices and obey the one-time Oracle self-role limit.
- Do not assert you are guaranteed invisible: a nonvisiting or astral carrier can cause fallback attack behavior, with source-flagged visibility uncertainty.
- Check the full prior history before adopting Oracle. A later astral night does not repair an earlier observed direct Attack.

**Best when:** You are using Shroud on a credible direct visitor and have no established movement contradiction.
