**Exposure/limits:** No Aegis exists, and the source warns some fallback Shroud actions register direct movement. Earlier direct attacks remain part of the history.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Shroud_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Tracker (TI) — Carrier clue · incomplete report

A carrier’s independently known destination can provide a grounded movement lead for a limited Tracker-style account.

- Keep three things separate: the player you Shrouded, where that player actually visited, and your own visible movement.
- A Shroud death at 7 is evidence of an attack there, not automatically proof that every claimed carrier visited 7.
- A real Tracker reports the followed player’s actual outgoing destinations; do not fill unknown extra visits with convenient names.
- If observers say you did not make the claimed follow visit, carrier information cannot solve that movement mismatch.

**Best when:** A particular carrier route is well supported publicly and you understand the limits of your information.

**Exposure/limits:** Shroud does not give a full Tracker report, and your own movement to a claimed follow target may be missing or inconsistent.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Shroud_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tracker_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Psychic (TI) — Direct-visit option · high upkeep

On direct Attack nights, the destination can be the selected member of a vision, giving an active cover one real movement anchor.

- Include the actual direct Attack destination as the selected member instead of repicking it after death information appears.
- Use N1 Good/two names and N2 Evil/three with at least one apparent category match, not a guaranteed culprit.
- Do not use the carrier’s status or a Shroud victim as automatic Good/Evil knowledge; those are different information types.
- When changing modes, recheck the whole visit history. A successful cover vision promises a visit that your actual mode may not make.

**Best when:** You can maintain early daily group reports and your current route is a straightforward direct visit.

**Exposure/limits:** You obtain no vision, while Shroud-mode and fallback nights can change whether the selected visible visit fits.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Shroud_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Vigilante (TK) — Later attack story · load mismatch

A later direct Attack can be described as a shot, offering a killing-role story rather than claiming protection you never provide.

- The ordinary opening is N1: Load, so a visible N1 attack already weakens this cover.
- Keep a legal load/shot sequence and the real Town-hit gun-loss consequence in mind; endless firing is not valid.
- An attack traveling through a carrier is not an ordinary Vigilante visit to the victim, even if both produce a death.
- Do not claim Shroud-attributed kills publicly confirm the role. This is a lower-ranked conditional account with substantial evidence pressure.

**Best when:** No established N1 action defeats a loading story, and you understand how much later death evidence can contradict it.

**Exposure/limits:** N1 attacks, Shroud-labelled deaths, carrier attacks and ammunition rules do not reproduce Vigilante mechanics.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Shroud_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vigilante_(ToS2)).

#### 6. Bodyguard (TP) — Direct route · missing interception

Your direct Attack route can share a quiet Guard destination without requiring a complex TI result.

- Use the direct Attack target as the alleged Guard destination while that route actually exists.
- Do not turn a carrier dying elsewhere into Bodyguard interception feedback; the causes and visited houses differ.
- A guarded resident’s death needs cause-specific analysis because Bodyguard is not a universal shield.
- Once you use Shroud mode, reassess your personal movement and the claimed effect rather than assuming any one-target selection fits Guard.

**Best when:** You prefer another simple visiting TP option and have no known interception event to explain.

**Exposure/limits:** You do not cancel a harmful visit or counterattack its source. Shroud-carrier harm and actual deaths can contradict the claimed Guard.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Shroud_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

## Werewolf

NK — Neutral Killing.

### Route 1: Scent off-moon; attack traffic on-moon (recommended)

**Best when:** Use persistent Scent off-moon and a chosen traffic house on-moon when your movement and attack route are reasonably safe.

**Action:** Check the actual moon indicator. On a non-full-moon opening, Track Scent an eligible likely visitor; on a full moon, choose an attack location with useful visitor traffic if enabled.

**Target rule:** Off-moon, prefer an active visiting claim likely to survive; without one, scent an eligible unknown outside obvious visiting danger. On-moon, prefer a valuable opponent expected to attract visitors, after weighing protection.

**Why:** Scent gives continuing movement information while that player lives. On attack nights your rampage can remove several opponents, so the house’s visitors matter as well as the resident.

**Change plan when:** Use persistent scent results to choose later attack houses. If deliberately staying home on a full moon, remember that this still rampages against visitors; it is not an ordinary harmless hold.

**Tradeoff:** An attractive traffic house may also be protected. Scent information does not make later attack-night movement fit a Tracker cover automatically.

**Checkpoints:**

- Follow the actual moon state
- Track persistent Scent separately
- Reassess movement compatibility on attack nights

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Build lasting scent coverage on credible visiting players during non-full-moon nights.
2. Use full-moon rampages on valuable houses with likely opposing visitors.
3. Keep your cover consistent with your actual movement after switching between scenting and attacks.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Werewolf_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Stay home on a full moon

**Best when:** You expect useful hostile visitors at your own house and an outward visit would face a more serious known danger.

**Action:** On a full moon, choose no outward attack target and Rampage at home. Off-moon this option is not an attack: continue an eligible Scent action if useful.

**Target rule:** Use an actual reason to expect visitors, such as an existing threat to inspect or attack you. Do not create a generic TP/LO request that mainly attracts friendly Town visitors into the attack.

**Why:** Home Rampage trades certainty about the resident you attack for the chance to remove visitors while keeping no outward visit.

**Change plan when:** If nobody has a reason to visit you, a useful external traffic house usually has more immediate value. Recheck the actual moon indicator.

**Tradeoff:** Nobody may visit, and an outward-target opportunity is lost. Staying home is not harmless and does not grant Invincible Defense.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm a full moon and enabled attack
- Recognize that holding still attacks visitors
- Keep the no-outward-visit history accurate

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use real surviving Scent information for later choices.
2. Reassess whether your house will still attract useful visitors.
3. Count votes as well as potential Rampage kills.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Werewolf_(ToS2)).

### Claim strategy

Tracker ranks first because Scent supplies genuine outgoing movement information. Cleric is lower upkeep but grants no protection; Psychic, Crusader and Veteran are more conditional. The strongest cover on an off-moon night may fail when a full-moon attack sends you to a different house.

#### 1. Tracker (TI) — Real movement clues · later visit conflict

Track Scent gives genuine Tracker-style destination information, letting you support a report with more than a guessed visit.

- For the initial scent night, report the real followed player and game-supplied destinations, distinguishing no visible visit from a failed action.
- Later scent feedback is information you still receive, not proof that you visited that same player again.
- Before a full-moon attack, compare your real attack house with the alleged follow destination. Correct result information does not repair a different observed visit.
- Do not add guessed destinations for unscented players, and post the claimed TI report promptly rather than waiting for every role claim.

**Best when:** You scent a useful living visitor off-moon and can manage the movement history as the moon changes.

**Exposure/limits:** Persistent scent reports continue without revisiting the scented person. Full-moon attack movement elsewhere can contradict the ordinary Tracker visit you claim.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Werewolf_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tracker_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Cleric (TP) — Simple visiting cover · no defense

A direct scent or chosen attack house can share a one-player Barrier destination, requiring fewer result details than Tracker.

- Use the actual chosen visited player for the claimed Barrier route, whether the real action was off-moon Scent or an outward full-moon attack.
- Do not use persistent scent reports as evidence that you protected the player. Movement knowledge and defense are different promises.
- A home Rampage night has no ordinary outward Barrier visit, so it cannot simply copy the previous target line.
- When Rampage creates several victims, the attack and death pattern may overwhelm a quiet protection explanation.

**Best when:** You want a compact destination history and accept losing the credibility benefit of your genuine scent information.

**Exposure/limits:** Neither Scent nor Rampage grants protection. Victim deaths, visitor kills and full-moon home nights can expose the mismatch.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Werewolf_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Psychic (TI) — Information cover · fabricated vision

A chosen Scent or outward attack destination can be the selected member of a vision, while your real movement clues may inform ordinary public reasoning.

- Include the actual selected visited player in the claimed vision; do not choose a passive previously scented person as tonight’s visit without checking movement.
- Keep N1 Good/two and N2 Evil/three names, meaning at least one apparent match rather than identifying an exact evil.
- Treat scent destinations as separate public deductions, not proof that someone belongs in a Good or Evil category.
- On a full moon at home, reassess the claimed successful visit before posting. A legal-looking group does not repair a missing outbound action.

**Best when:** You know Psychic group logic and prefer broad discussion influence over explaining exact scent information.

**Exposure/limits:** Scent never supplies a Good/Evil group. Home attacks and other movement changes can also contradict the selected vision visit.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Werewolf_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Crusader (TP) — Visitor danger overlap · protection missing

Both roles can create danger for visitors at a chosen house, making a visitor-death event somewhat easier to discuss than with a harmless protection cover.

- Use the actual outward attack house as the alleged Fortify location only when the visible route fits.
- A Crusader promises Powerful Defense for the resident. A Werewolf attack supplies no such defense and may kill that resident.
- Do not claim Crusader attacks every visitor or learns all struck identities; a large Rampage death pattern is a major contradiction.
- Off-moon Scent and home full-moon Rampage need their own timeline checks. A single visitor kill does not establish the entire cover.

**Best when:** A specific visitor-danger event needs explanation and you understand why the two effects are still substantially different.

**Exposure/limits:** Crusader protects the resident and attacks one random direct visitor; Werewolf Rampage attacks the resident and multiple visitors without granting that protection.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Werewolf_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Crusader_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Veteran (TK) — Home-full-moon fit · earlier scent conflict

A deliberate full-moon Rampage at home resembles a home-based dangerous visitor response more than an outward attack does.

- Keep the claimed effect at your own house; Veteran cannot Alert at a selected opponent’s house.
- Audit all previous off-moon Scent visits before choosing a stay-home role cover.
- Track a legal three-Alert history and do not claim every later home Rampage is another unlimited Alert.
- Do not invent visitor identities beyond supported evidence or equate the roles’ attack and defense details. This is a narrow situational overlap.

**Best when:** You are genuinely staying home on a full moon and no earlier observed scent or attack visit already contradicts Veteran.

**Exposure/limits:** Veteran can Alert on legal nights with a three-charge limit, while Werewolf is moon-bound and may already have visible Scent visits.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Werewolf_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Veteran_(ToS2)).

## Cursed Soul

Neutral Outlier — Neutral Outlier.

### Route 1: Acquire a useful eligible role (recommended)

**Best when:** Choose a useful eligible role to acquire when a reliable claim exists; otherwise choose an eligible unknown and read the actual swap result.

**Action:** Select an eligible player whose role would be useful to acquire. With no reliable role claims, select an eligible unknown rather than treating no selection as a safe hold.

**Target rule:** Prefer a credible useful-role claim where the swap is legal. Avoid assuming a Neutral Evil or transformed Horseman is an eligible guaranteed acquisition; check actual feedback.

**Why:** Soul Swap can activate with a random choice if you leave it blank. In the default Wandering Souls faction, your chosen target is not guaranteed, and changing roles does not change your faction objective.

**Change plan when:** At dawn, record the real resulting role and faction. Consult that role’s ability plan while continuing the Wandering Souls objective: eliminate every living Cursed Soul with a faction member surviving.

**Tradeoff:** In the normal Wandering Souls faction, selection does not guarantee that player’s role. No selection is an automatic random Swap, not a safe hold.

**Checkpoints:**

- Observe the actual swap result
- Keep faction separate from role
- Follow the acquired role with an honest timeline

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Resolve the actual swap outcome before making claims about your role or someone else’s.
2. Use acquired information or killing tools to locate and remove current Cursed Soul holders.
3. Keep at least one Wandering Souls member alive through the final Cursed Soul elimination.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cursed_Soul_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Seek an ability that can remove Cursed Souls

**Best when:** A credible eligible killing-role claim exists and acquiring its ability would help eliminate the remaining Cursed Soul role holders.

**Action:** Attempt Soul Swap with that eligible role holder. At dawn, check the actual new role and use its legal ability rules toward the unchanged Wandering Souls objective.

**Target rule:** Avoid known Swap-immune targets. Distinguish the player you selected, the actual swap outcome, and whoever currently holds the Cursed Soul role.

**Why:** A useful acquired ability can pursue your faction’s objective directly. Role acquisition does not make you join that role’s usual faction.

**Change plan when:** If randomness gives you a different role, follow that real card rather than the desired one. If another swap changes the Cursed Soul holder, update before attacking.

**Tradeoff:** Your selected acquisition may not occur, and immediately exposing your faction can invite a day shot. A former holder is not automatically the current Cursed Soul.

**Checkpoints:**

- Separate role from faction
- Read the resolved swap rather than the selection
- Find the current Cursed Soul holders

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Use acquired abilities only on their legal timings.
2. Recheck chained swaps before eliminating a stale target.
3. Ensure a Wandering Souls member survives when the final Cursed Soul dies.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cursed_Soul_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-balance-changes-and-bug-fixes-patch-notes-r1-5-05/).

### Claim strategy

Oracle fits the initial astral Swap. Amnesiac is a conditional transition story only with the correct eligible Town-death timing. Coroner, Veteran and setup-legal Pilgrim are weaker opening accounts. After a real swap, the acquired role and unchanged faction matter more than keeping a preselected generic cover. These ranks describe the initial Cursed Soul role. Once Soul Swap resolves, open the acquired role guide for your new ability; your faction stays Wandering Souls and its objective remains eliminating the remaining Cursed Soul role holders while a faction member survives.

#### 1. Oracle (TP) — Initial astral fit · acquired-role change

Soul Swap is astral, so the initial absence of an ordinary visible visit is compatible with Oracle’s role-targeted protection footprint.

- Use role-based entries such as Aegis Sheriff, not the numbered person you attempted to Swap with.
- Respect consecutive-role restrictions and the once-only Oracle self-role option in the account.
- At dawn read the role you actually acquired, not the role you wanted. Wandering Souls selection does not guarantee the intended player.
- If the acquired role is Town, its real ability may support a more specific account, but keep the unchanged faction objective separate from that role demonstration.

**Best when:** You need an initial nonvisiting cover while waiting to learn the actual swap result.

**Exposure/limits:** No Aegis is supplied, and your acquired role changes future movement and abilities. A role swap does not make you Town faction.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cursed_Soul_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Amnesiac (TS) — Transition explanation · strict chronology

A genuine change of role can motivate a transition account, avoiding the need to pretend you always held the acquired role.

- Check a real eligible Town death and the Remember timing before naming Amnesiac as the previous role.
- A live-player Swap is not a corpse Remember. Do not claim the transition merely because any role change occurred.
- After the alleged transition, follow the acquired role’s actual targets, charges and result obligations.
- Never describe role acquisition as joining Town. Soul Swap leaves the Wandering Souls faction objective in place even when the ability is Town-like.

**Best when:** The actual acquired Town role and public eligible-death chronology happen to support an Amnesiac transition.

**Exposure/limits:** Amnesiac must gain an eligible dead Town role at the correct time; acquiring a living player’s role, an evil role or the wrong priority does not fit.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cursed_Soul_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Amnesiac_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Coroner (TI) — Quiet N1 fit · future ability mismatch

Ordinary Coroner has no useful opening autopsy, giving your initial astral Swap a plausible nonvisiting-night account.

- Use N1: No usable autopsy instead of claiming a killer comparison from the initial Swap.
- Do not convert knowledge of somebody’s former role into autopsy evidence; current role, former role and killer record are different facts.
- Read the acquired role before adding later Examine targets, because it may move or act in a way that immediately contradicts Coroner.
- A real eligible Autopsy must precede any claimed comparison. This opening match is weaker than simply following an acquired Town ability when available.

**Best when:** You need a brief opening account and can maintain exact legal corpse timing if later circumstances allow it.

**Exposure/limits:** A later role acquisition may have no Coroner ability at all. Corpse records and normal Examine visits cannot be generated by the original Swap.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cursed_Soul_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Coroner_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Veteran (TK) — No outward visit initially · effect risk

Initial astral Swap can look like staying home, avoiding an invented numbered-player visit in the first account.

- Keep the attempted Swap target private; a Veteran cover has no outward night destination.
- Do not call a swap-related death an Alert kill or claim everyone who visited you must be dead.
- Maintain the legal three-Alert count if you use this account, and do not invent direct-visitor feedback.
- After acquisition, reassess the actual role immediately. A visible visiting ability cannot keep passing as a stay-home Veteran merely because N1 was astral.

**Best when:** No known initial direct visitor or later movement already contradicts a quiet home-based story.

**Exposure/limits:** Swap does not shoot visitors and the acquired role may require visible travel. You cannot produce Veteran’s Alert effect unless you actually acquire it.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cursed_Soul_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Veteran_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Pilgrim (Town Outlier) — Setup-restricted · quiet opening only

Pilgrim’s lack of an active ability can fit an initial invisible Swap without a fabricated result or protective effect.

- Check Pilgrim’s deck eligibility separately from the fact that Cursed Soul is present; one allowed Outlier does not prove every other Outlier is possible.
- Record public questions and vote reasons rather than exposing role-swap knowledge as though it came from Pilgrim.
- Add no fabricated protective target or night investigation: the claimed role has no active ability.
- At the actual acquisition, compare new actions against the no-ability story and your unchanged Wandering Souls objective before committing further entries.

**Best when:** Town Outliers are explicitly possible and public observation is a more sustainable initial account than inventing a power.

**Exposure/limits:** The role must be legal in the deck. An acquired role may begin visiting or demonstrating abilities that directly contradict a no-ability account.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cursed_Soul_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pilgrim_(ToS2)).

## Vampire

Neutral Outlier — Neutral Outlier.

### Route 1: Convert a useful Town ally (recommended)

**Best when:** Use Convert while charges remain and an apparently undefended Town player offers a useful ally and retained ability.

**Action:** Choose Convert on an eligible likely Town player without known defense. The opening aim is an ally, an extra vote and their retained role ability.

**Target rule:** Prefer a credible useful Town claim outside obvious Veteran danger and expected heavy protection. Without claims, choose an eligible unknown; no-information conversion is a calculated guess, not a Town read.

**Why:** Converting useful Town expands your faction; simply Draining them removes that potential ally. Convert instead becomes Drain against non-Town or defended targets, so it is not a guaranteed recruit.

**Change plan when:** After resolution, check whether a conversion actually occurred and coordinate with any new ally. Switch to Drain for known unconvertible opposition; a failed conversion attempt does not automatically identify the reason.

**Tradeoff:** A non-Town or defended target falls back to Drain. Gaining an ally also changes your solo defense and detection situation.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm an actual recruit
- Track role and faction separately
- Coordinate the next conversion with real charges

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Recruit a useful eligible Town role while Convert charges remain.
2. Coordinate retained ally abilities and votes; count the cost of losing solo defense.
3. Drain known unconvertible threats and preserve the faction through the endgame.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vampire_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Drain an immediate threat instead

**Best when:** A known opponent threatens your survival now, conversion is unavailable or unsuitable, and a Basic attack can plausibly kill them.

**Action:** Choose Drain on that opponent if attacks are enabled instead of spending a Convert charge on a low-confidence candidate.

**Target rule:** Prefer an apparently undefended exposed threat. Do not attack a known Basic-Defense opponent merely because conversion would fail on them too.

**Why:** Growing your faction is normally valuable, but removing an immediate danger can preserve your ability to convert later and protect existing members.

**Change plan when:** If the threat is protected or not immediately dangerous, seek a better Convert target. During Grace Period an attack may be unavailable even when Convert remains possible.

**Tradeoff:** A kill forfeits a potential ally and may fail against defense. Do not treat Drain as a universally better opening than Convert.

**Checkpoints:**

- Check remaining Convert charges
- Verify Drain is enabled
- Compare one kill with one useful ally

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Coordinate retained abilities and votes with real faction members.
2. Track shared conversion resources rather than assuming a replacement Vampire refreshes them.
3. Avoid exposing the whole faction through an unnecessary linked claim.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vampire_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://www.townofsalem.io/town-of-salem-2-season-5-patch-notes-r1-5-00/).

### Claim strategy

Cleric keeps a direct Convert account simple; Psychic adds invented daily vision groups and more discussion influence. Bodyguard, Sheriff and Trickster are lower visiting alternatives. Conversion changes allegiance while preserving the new member’s real role ability, which is stronger evidence than a cover label by itself.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Simple direct route · no Barrier

Convert or Drain visits one player, so the destination can match a concise Barrier account while you build the faction privately.

- Use the actual Convert or Drain target as the alleged Barrier destination rather than a convenient survivor chosen after dawn.
- Do not label a successful conversion a save. The new member retains their role but changes faction; that is not Cleric feedback.
- If Convert falls back to Drain, compare the death evidence with the protective account before making a stronger claim.
- Coordinate with real members without using identical fabricated target histories. A converted Town ability can demonstrate an ability without proving Town allegiance.

**Best when:** You want a low-upkeep target history and no current attack event requires real protection.

**Exposure/limits:** Neither action supplies defense. Conversion effects, fallback Drain deaths and newly linked faction members can expose the difference.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vampire_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Psychic (TI) — Discussion control · invented visions

A direct Convert destination can be the selected player in a cover vision, providing one real movement point and a broader daytime narrative.

- Include the actual visited player as the selected member of the claimed vision.
- Maintain N1 Good/two names and N2 Evil/three with at least one apparent match, not exactly one or an exact-role reveal.
- Do not publish a chain of invented Good results solely to clear every converted member; their real faction has changed.
- Keep successful Convert and fallback Drain consequences distinct. Neither gives permission to invent a genuine vision or change yesterday’s group.

**Best when:** You can post early and manage group logic without treating every new member as a confirmed Town result.

**Exposure/limits:** Convert supplies no vision. Counts, parity, independent reports and the behavior of new faction members can contradict the invented group.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vampire_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Bodyguard (TP) — Short guard account · no interception

A direct Convert route can be described as a one-person Guard route without routine TI result obligations.

- Use Guard on the actual Convert or Drain destination to preserve any observed movement.
- A converted player living is not interception evidence, and their retained ability is not a shield you supplied.
- Do not describe Bodyguard as blanket defense; analyze the specific harmful-visitor event if the resident dies.
- Keep the faction’s real conversion and charge history private rather than manufacturing a sacrifice story when a linked member dies.

**Best when:** You prefer another compact visiting TP account and no real guard event must be demonstrated.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot intercept a harmful visitor. A failed conversion becoming Drain or an attacked resident exposes a different effect.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vampire_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Sheriff (TI) — Compact TI story · allegiance changes matter

A direct Convert or Drain route can match an Search destination, allowing a familiar one-person result account.

- Use the actual visited player in the alleged check history, not another faction member selected after dawn.
- Use Innocent or Suspicious rather than an exact Town role. Learning that Convert succeeded is not a Sheriff result.
- Keep report dates fixed when a player’s allegiance changes; do not rewrite an old account to match today’s faction.
- Avoid clearing all your new members with invented results. Real role demonstrations can remain valid after conversion while Town allegiance does not.

**Best when:** You can maintain prompt TI posts and understand that a formerly Town role may now belong to another faction.

**Exposure/limits:** Vampire learns no suspicion check. A conversion changes apparent allegiance on later investigations, so old and new reports need their actual timing.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vampire_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Trickster (TK) — Mirror-placement story · missing absorption

A direct Convert target can match the destination of Magic Mirror placement, giving a quiet setup narrative instead of an invented TI check.

- Use Magic Mirror on the actual Convert destination as the claimed setup visit.
- Do not label a successful conversion or a surviving member an Absorb event; neither supplies that feedback.
- An Unleash needs an absorbed attack from an earlier night and replaces ordinary mirror placement. A Basic Drain is not proof of that sequence.
- Check retained member abilities and real attack outcomes before coordinating claims; mutually confirming a missing effect can expose multiple faction members.

**Best when:** A TK setup story fits the deck and you understand the difference between placing a mirror and receiving an attack.

**Exposure/limits:** Vampire creates no mirror or absorbed attack. Drain cannot be called Unleash without the required earlier absorption and compatible effect.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vampire_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trickster_(ToS2)).

## Famine

NA — Neutral Apocalypse.

### Route 1: Survive and Starve Bread-holders (recommended)

**Best when:** As an already transformed Famine, prioritize surviving the vote and accelerating starvation on a useful living Bread-holder.

**Action:** You are already transformed: use Starve on a living opposing player with Bread. Do not try to Feed three recipients again.

**Target rule:** Prefer the Bread-holder most able to organize your hanging or supply opposing votes. With equal threats, shorten a known remaining Bread countdown; avoid wasting the visit on a breadless player.

**Why:** Players without Bread already face the ongoing famine. Starve is most useful on a Bread-holder because it accelerates a survivor’s countdown instead of duplicating the automatic threat to a breadless opponent.

**Change plan when:** When a Bread-holder dies, update the remaining survivors before selecting again. If a different opponent becomes a decisive daytime threat, move your pressure there rather than following a rigid original recipient order.

**Tradeoff:** Invincible Defense does not prevent trial execution. An irrelevant target may waste the extra Bread reduction while a dangerous recipient organizes your hanging.

**Checkpoints:**

- Survive the transformation day and every following vote; Invincible Defense does not stop hanging.
- Starve surviving opposing Bread-holders to reduce their remaining food faster.
- Use the shrinking survivor and vote counts to eliminate the faction most able to hang you.

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Survive the transformation day and every following vote; Invincible Defense does not stop hanging.
2. Starve surviving opposing Bread-holders to reduce their remaining food faster.
3. Use the shrinking survivor and vote counts to eliminate the faction most able to hang you.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Finish a Bread countdown for a vote swing

**Best when:** A lower-influence Bread-holder is close enough to starvation that accelerating them can remove a needed opposing vote sooner than targeting the main organizer.

**Action:** Use Starve on that near-expiry Bread-holder while keeping the main organizer under daytime scrutiny.

**Target rule:** Choose an actual living opposing Bread-holder with supported remaining-food information. Compare the immediate vote effect with pressuring the loudest opponent.

**Why:** The default prioritizes the player organizing your execution. An earlier certain vote reduction can matter more when one fewer opponent changes who can be nominated tomorrow.

**Change plan when:** If no immediate starvation or vote swing is supported, return to the dangerous Bread-holder default instead of chasing an imagined countdown advantage.

**Tradeoff:** A guessed countdown can be wrong. Removing a weaker voter may leave the strongest organizer alive for another day.

**Checkpoints:**

- Use current Bread information
- Count the vote change from an earlier death
- Keep the transformed Starve rules

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Recount surviving opposing votes after starvation resolves.
2. Return pressure to the remaining execution organizer.
3. Survive each day so passive Famine continues.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)#Famine).

### Claim strategy

Cleric and Psychic remain the starter choices: Cleric requires less invented information; Psychic creates more discussion influence. The lower options reuse a direct Feed destination but promise effects or results Feed never supplies. These are conditional cover choices, not equivalent ability matches. After becoming Famine, use the transformed action guide. Starve accelerates Bread loss; it does not keep supplying any claimed Town protection or vision. You are Famine now: a pre-transformation cover does not explain new attacks, altered movement, or the public transformation.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Low upkeep · no real protection

Feed is a real one-person visit, so its destination can support the travel part of a Barrier account while you quietly build living Bread recipients.

- Use the actual Feed destination as the claimed Barrier destination so a movement report does not force you to rewrite the night.
- Write N1: Barrier 4, for example; do not add saved 4 unless genuine public evidence supports that separate claim.
- Choose likely surviving recipients for your Baker objective, then explain why each could reasonably have been a protection choice.
- A Bread-holder dying is a warning for the cover and your progress; inspect the cause instead of claiming Barrier makes every death impossible.
- After becoming Famine, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You want one stable nightly target line and can tolerate weak independent proof.

**Exposure/limits:** Bread never grants a Barrier. An incompatible attack, a failed-save story, or the Famine transition can expose the missing protection.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Psychic (TI) — More influence · invented visions

A Feed recipient can be the selected player in a Psychic vision, giving you a real destination while the group report creates daytime leads.

- Include the real Feed recipient as the selected target; you cannot claim a different selected visit merely because another player looks useful at dawn.
- N1 Good uses two names with at least one appearing Good; N2 Evil uses three with at least one appearing Evil. Neither identifies exactly which name fits.
- Publish the whole cover vision near day start and keep previous lists unchanged; assembling it after every claim is easier to challenge.
- Keep Bread recipients private: several living recipients do not become confirmed Town, and Famine does not validate any prior vision.
- After becoming Famine, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You can post promptly every morning and understand Good/Evil group logic.

**Exposure/limits:** Feed supplies no vision. Extra names, parity, group size and later public information can contradict the invented report.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Bodyguard (TP) — Simple target log · interception risk

Feed can follow the same one-player route as Guard without requiring a daily investigation report; quiet guarding nights are easy to describe.

- Claim Guard on the actual Feed destination rather than a post-dawn replacement house.
- Keep a quiet entry quiet: Guard 4 does not mean you blocked an attack or killed its source.
- Remember that Bodyguard is an interception role, not Cleric’s general shield; a resident surviving says little by itself.
- Before defending a target death, compare the real cause and any known harmful visitor. Do not invent a sacrifice or a self-vest to repair it.
- After becoming Famine, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** Your visits fit a plausible guard route and nobody has supplied an event that demands a real interception.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot intercept an attacker. A visible harmful visitor, the resident dying, or an impossible interception-survival story can distinguish you.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Trickster (TK) — Quiet setup · later proof burden

The Feed visit can resemble placing Magic Mirror on one person; a mirror that never absorbs anything can produce a quiet history.

- Use Magic Mirror on the actual Feed target as the setup account, preserving the observed route.
- Do not write Absorb just because the protected-looking player lived: the role needs actual absorption feedback.
- Never add an Unleash before an alleged absorption, and remember that an Unleash night replaces ordinary mirror placement.
- Your private aim is keeping Bread recipients alive; if they are attacked, the missing mirror effect may make this cover much weaker than the destination match suggests.
- After becoming Famine, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** A TK cover fits the deck and you can sustain a setup account without claiming a demonstration you never performed.

**Exposure/limits:** Bread absorbs no attack. An invented Absorb or Unleash introduces an effect and attack history Baker cannot create.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trickster_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Sheriff (TI) — Active pressure · no real check

A Feed destination can be the person you claim to Search, allowing a simple result vocabulary and a more active daytime account.

- Name the actual Feed recipient as the checked player; keep that choice fixed before reading the morning claims.
- Use only Innocent or Suspicious, not an exact Town role. An Innocent line is not a clear on a Bread-holder.
- Post the claimed check promptly and explain any missing check rather than using Baker feedback as an investigative result.
- Avoid repeatedly defending every recipient as Town: Bread membership supports your transformation, not their allegiance.
- After becoming Famine, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You prefer a simple TI format over protection claims and accept the daily posting burden.

**Exposure/limits:** You learn no Innocent or Suspicious result. Independent reports can expose the invented information even when the visit is genuine.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Baker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

## War

NA — Neutral Apocalypse.

### Route 1: Attack two distinct opposing houses (recommended)

**Best when:** As War, use two different meaningful opposing houses when both add value. The transformation has already happened.

**Action:** You are already transformed: confirm two distinct useful opponents for your Unstoppable rampages. Do not revert to the Berserker one-target buildup plan.

**Target rule:** Prioritize opponents who can organize your execution or provide decisive votes. If their threat is similar, favor houses likely to attract other opponents. Exclude actual allies if your setup contains them.

**Why:** Two selected houses can remove two residents plus visitors. Omitting the second target gives up a major part of your current power, while Invincible Defense makes daytime identification and hanging the main ordinary danger.

**Change plan when:** If one intended target dies or is no longer eligible before selection, replace them. Against another Invincible opponent, do not assume Unstoppable damage guarantees the kill; recalculate the endgame.

**Tradeoff:** Huge night power does not protect against a daytime execution. Reusing an unhelpful target or ignoring visitor traffic can waste the second selection.

**Checkpoints:**

- Survive daytime voting while keeping your cover consistent with known movement.
- Confirm both separate targets every night to remove the most dangerous opposing voters.
- Use visitor traffic to increase rampage value after selecting the essential threats.

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Survive daytime voting while keeping your cover consistent with known movement.
2. Confirm both separate targets every night to remove the most dangerous opposing voters.
3. Use visitor traffic to increase rampage value after selecting the essential threats.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Split one threat and one visitor hub

**Best when:** A direct execution organizer and a separate busy house offer more combined value than simply taking the two loudest players.

**Action:** Confirm Rampage at the known immediate threat and a distinct useful house expected to receive opposing visitors.

**Target rule:** Count unique opponents you may remove; visitors shared by both houses do not become two different players. Do not choose an ally in a setup that actually provides allies.

**Why:** One selection removes a known danger while the other uses the Rampage attribute for broader reach. Unstoppable attacks still do not overcome every form of Invincible Defense.

**Change plan when:** If the visitor prediction is weak, prefer a second concrete opposing vote. Confirm both selections rather than assuming the second is automatic.

**Tradeoff:** Traffic is a prediction, and an attractive house may be empty of visitors. A complicated night plan cannot replace surviving today.

**Checkpoints:**

- Confirm two distinct useful houses
- Separate guaranteed residents from speculative visitors
- Preserve daytime survival

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Recount opposing voters after resolution.
2. Reassess known Invincible opponents and special win threats.
3. Use the next two targets to close the actual endgame.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)#War).

### Claim strategy

Vigilante preserves the quiet-N1 timing best among the starter options; Cleric is a simple destination story only on later direct-attack nights. Coroner and Trapper offer alternative inactive openings, while Deputy is short but easily tested. No Town cover reproduces growing attacks and Rampage indefinitely. After becoming War, a two-house Unstoppable Rampage no longer resembles a normal Vigilante shot or single-person protection visit. You are War now: a pre-transformation cover does not explain new attacks, altered movement, or the public transformation.

#### 1. Vigilante (TK) — Quiet N1 fit · later kill mismatch

Your normal N1 inactivity resembles Vigilante loading before a later shot, so you can avoid inventing an opening visit.

- Write N1: Load as the cover, while privately recording that Berserker had no legal N1 attack.
- From N2, a claimed shot must fit an actual prior Load; you cannot report a shot every night as though Vigilante has endless ready ammunition.
- Keep claimed shot targets aligned with any observed direct attacks, but do not claim Berserker-labelled deaths prove you are Vigilante.
- Track the cover’s Town-hit gun penalty separately from your Berserker upgrades; a Rampage is not an ordinary single-player shot.
- After becoming War, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You want a TK cover with a mechanically plausible inactive N1 and can maintain the later load/shot history.

**Exposure/limits:** Berserker kills are not Vigilante shots. Attack upgrades, Rampage, death attribution and the load/ammunition sequence can expose the difference.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Vigilante_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Cleric (TP) — Later visit fit · weak N1 fit

A later direct attack has a single-player destination that can be described as a Barrier visit, with little writing on quiet claimed nights.

- Do not claim an ordinary N1 Barrier visit if a movement report already establishes that you stayed home.
- For later nights, preserve the actual selected attack destination instead of naming a convenient survivor after dawn.
- Write only the alleged Barrier choice unless you can support additional feedback; your kill confirmation is not a saved-target message.
- As the attack upgrades, expect multi-victim or distinctive deaths to defeat the simple one-house protection account.
- After becoming War, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You are evaluating a later attack-night account and understand that the cover is weaker across the whole timeline.

**Exposure/limits:** The normal Berserker N1 is nonvisiting and no attack grants protection. Deaths, observed N1 inactivity and Rampage undermine the story.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Coroner (TI) — Inactive opener · crime-report upkeep

Coroner normally has no usable N1 autopsy record, matching your inactive opening without an invented early visit.

- Use N1: No usable autopsy rather than claiming to identify a killer before a legal body exists.
- Record a real eligible daytime Autopsy before the cover’s first related night Examine; a role name alone cannot supply a killer record.
- From N2, compare the claimed Examine destination with your real attack movement before committing the entry.
- Do not turn your knowledge of a Berserker victim into a Coroner match: you know your own attack, not the borrowed investigation’s result.
- After becoming War, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You can track genuine corpse timing and want a quieter N1 before taking on TI obligations.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive neither autopsy evidence nor killer comparisons, and your later direct attack need not match the claimed Examine destination.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Coroner_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Trapper (TP) — Build-night fit · persistent trap burden

A claimed Build on N1 fits your lack of a direct visit. The later placement sequence supplies a TP story with fewer routine result lines.

- Open with N1: Build, not a placed trap; that preserves the one clear timing overlap.
- A later Place Trap target should be compatible with any observed attack visit, but your Berserker attack creates no actual trap.
- Keep only one active trap in the story and do not invent visitor-role feedback from a quiet death report.
- Once later attack nights or Rampage conflict with rebuilding or leaving a trap active, the initial N1 match no longer carries the cover.
- After becoming War, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** A TP setup cover fits your initial inactivity and you understand the build-before-placement sequence.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot place or trigger a real trap. Later victims, visiting roles and an impossible rebuild schedule can expose the invented device.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Trapper_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Deputy (TK) — Minimal writing · immediate test risk

Deputy needs no night visit or investigation, so your N1 can be described without inventing a nightly target.

- Write No night ability for N1 rather than manufacturing a check or protection target.
- Explain a held shot as a decision based on insufficient evidence; do not claim a miss is public proof of Deputy.
- A real shot is a visible daytime event. Berserker’s N2 kill cannot substitute for it.
- Reassess immediately if someone observes you visiting or requests a justified demonstration; minimal typing does not make this a durable cover.
- After becoming War, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You need a short early account and accept that a reasonable shot request can make it collapse.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot produce the daytime shot. Later observed attack visits also conflict with a role that has no night action.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Berserker_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Deputy_(ToS2)).

## Pestilence

NA — Neutral Apocalypse.

### Route 1: Remove the execution organizer (recommended)

**Best when:** As Pestilence, directly remove the opponent most likely to coordinate your execution while passive stack pressure develops.

**Action:** You are already transformed: use Spread Pestilence on the opposing player most likely to organize your hanging. Do not keep selecting targets as though you still need new Plague infections.

**Target rule:** Prioritize an active execution organizer or decisive opposing voter who is not already expected to die from accrued stacks. With equal threats, choose the one whose removal most weakens an opposing voting bloc.

**Why:** Spread Pestilence applies maximum stacks to its target. Other opponents can die as stack transmission reaches the threshold, so your chosen action should accelerate a meaningful removal beyond passive progress.

**Change plan when:** Recheck living players and displayed stack information after every resolution. If the intended target has died or is already certain to expire, move to a different threat rather than duplicating that outcome.

**Tradeoff:** You still lose to a qualifying daytime execution. Do not confuse current Pestilence stacks with the pretransformation infection-coverage goal.

**Checkpoints:**

- Survive daytime voting; Invincible Defense does not protect against hanging.
- Apply maximum stacks to the most important opponent who would otherwise survive.
- Use actual stack progress and survivor counts to plan the next removal.

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Survive daytime voting; Invincible Defense does not protect against hanging.
2. Apply maximum stacks to the most important opponent who would otherwise survive.
3. Use actual stack progress and survivor counts to plan the next removal.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Break a known opposing vote bloc

**Best when:** An organized group has the decisive voting numbers, while the loudest individual is not the only reason you are threatened.

**Action:** Use Spread Pestilence on a key opposing bloc member whose removal changes the next voting threshold, rather than automatically choosing the loudest speaker.

**Target rule:** Count actual surviving coordinated votes and select a meaningful member not already expected to die from passive stacks. Do not treat every quiet player as part of the same team.

**Why:** A coalition can continue organizing your execution after one speaker dies. This route chooses by the bloc’s remaining numbers rather than individual prominence.

**Change plan when:** If the bloc association is only a hunch or one active organizer is clearly the immediate danger, use the default direct threat removal instead.

**Tradeoff:** Team or coalition membership is often uncertain. A mathematically attractive night removal does not prevent your execution earlier in the same day.

**Checkpoints:**

- Use Spread Pestilence rather than Infect
- Count real opposing votes
- Avoid duplicating a likely passive death

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Recount blocs after each death resolution.
2. Recheck which opponent can still assemble an execution majority.
3. Stay alive through the day so ongoing Pestilence remains lethal.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)#Pestilence).

### Claim strategy

Cleric is the simplest direct-Infect cover. Lookout stays the second starter because actual spread can supply limited contact clues, but it is substantially harder and not a complete report. Psychic, Tracker, Bodyguard and Sheriff are lower conditional options. Seeded neighbors never count as observed visits. As Pestilence, Spread Pestilence has a different lethal effect. Do not continue describing the actual objective as finding the last uninfected player. You are Pestilence now: a pre-transformation cover does not explain new attacks, altered movement, or the public transformation.

#### 1. Cleric (TP) — Low upkeep · no real Barrier

Direct Infect gives a real one-player visit that can support the route of a claimed Barrier without requiring invented daily information.

- Use the actual direct Infect target as the claimed Barrier destination; passive spread is not another visit you personally made.
- Keep any no-attack entry modest. A target surviving is not a real saved message and infection does not protect them.
- Choose productive uninfected targets for your objective, then keep a plausible protection reason for that same route.
- Separate every infection marker from your public notes; neither infected neighbors nor new carriers are confirmed Town.
- After becoming Pestilence, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You want a stable one-target cover while focusing privately on the infection map.

**Exposure/limits:** Infection supplies no defense. A real attack, observed spread or Pestilence transformation can expose the missing protection.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Cleric_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Lookout (TI) — Partial contact clues · high upkeep

Source-checked infection spread can reveal some contact relationships, which may help you reason about visitors rather than inventing every name blindly.

- Choose the watched house consistently with your actual direct Infect destination if observers may track your own visit.
- For each name, ask whether your evidence is truly an incoming visit to that house; a carrier visiting another house is a different direction.
- Never label your initial adjacent infections as N1 visitors. They began infected without needing a visit.
- A real Lookout owes the game-supplied visible visitor list, not whichever infection contacts are convenient. Disclose the limitation to yourself before selecting this difficult cover.
- After becoming Pestilence, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You have concrete contact evidence for a particular house and understand the difference between incoming visitors and outgoing movement.

**Exposure/limits:** Spread feedback is not a complete Lookout result. Seeded infections, outgoing spread, invisible visits and unrelated contacts can make the claimed house list wrong.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Lookout_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Psychic (TI) — Broad leads · invented group reports

The direct Infect target can be the selected member of a vision, keeping one genuine movement point while a group account drives discussion.

- Include your direct Infect target as the chosen member of the claimed vision, not a passive spread recipient selected after dawn.
- Maintain N1 Good/two names and N2 Evil/three names, with at least one matching the category rather than exactly one.
- Post the cover list near day start and keep previous groups unchanged as roles are revealed.
- Do not use spread to call someone confirmed evil or Town. Infection records contact and progress, not faction.
- After becoming Pestilence, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You can manage early daily posts and strict group counts without treating infection as faction knowledge.

**Exposure/limits:** Plague gives no Good/Evil vision and no guaranteed allegiance information about any group member.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Psychic_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Tracker (TI) — Partial outgoing clues · movement risk

Some transmission feedback can support a narrow inference about where an infected player traveled, giving more grounding than a completely arbitrary destination guess.

- Claim to follow the same person you directly Infect if your own visit is visible; following an unrelated spread contact creates another mismatch.
- Distinguish X infected Y through an outgoing visit from Y visiting X; the claimed Tracker result needs the outgoing direction.
- A seeded infection or lack of a new infection does not establish no visit. The target could visit an already infected person or act astrally.
- Write only a legally structured destination history and recognize that a genuine Tracker would receive fuller information than your partial map.
- After becoming Pestilence, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** One particular outgoing contact is well supported and you can resist filling missing movement with invented certainty.

**Exposure/limits:** It does not provide the full Tracker result for every chosen player. Your direct Infect route must also fit the person you claim to follow.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Tracker_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Bodyguard (TP) — Simple guard line · no interception

A direct Infect can share Guard’s ordinary visit route and avoids the daily fabricated names required by an information cover.

- Use Guard on the actual Infect destination and keep passive spread contacts out of the target list.
- Do not claim an interception because a player stayed alive; you received infection progress, not Bodyguard feedback.
- If the resident dies, inspect the cause and known visits rather than treating all deaths as either automatic proof or automatically harmless.
- After Pestilence appears, a new lethal direct effect is not retroactive evidence of guarding anyone.
- After becoming Pestilence, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** A simple visiting TP story is preferable to managing TI reports, with no known interception event to explain.

**Exposure/limits:** You never intercept a harmful visitor. Real attack and survival evidence can disprove the claimed Guard effect.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Bodyguard_(ToS2)).

#### 6. Sheriff (TI) — Simple vocabulary · no real suspicion check

The direct Infect visit can double as the alleged Search route, producing a compact TI account rather than a protection story.

- Check the same person you directly Infect in the public account; passive carriers are not additional Search targets.
- Use Innocent or Suspicious only, and remember Innocent is not a Town clear.
- Do not derive Suspicious from a new infection, a busy spread branch or an infected neighbor. Those are not suspicion checks.
- Keep a consistent early-posted history as other investigators and the eventual transformation put the account under pressure.
- After becoming Pestilence, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You want a familiar one-person TI format and accept prompt posting and independent contradictions.

**Exposure/limits:** Infections reveal no Innocent/Suspicious result. Confusing contact with allegiance creates unsupported accusations.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Plaguebearer_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

## Death

NA — Neutral Apocalypse.

### Route 1: Survive the transformation day (recommended)

**Best when:** As Death, the transformation day is the decisive phase: survive it with a stable account and checkable answers.

**Action:** Your immediate decision is daytime survival. Keep a consistent claim, answer accusations with checkable facts and avoid supplying a fresh contradiction that makes you the easy hanging.

**Target rule:** Focus your attention on the players organizing votes against you. With no accusation yet, participate plausibly and preserve your cover instead of volunteering the transformed identity.

**Why:** The Apocalypse resolves at sundown if you survive the day. Another Reap target or extra Soul no longer improves that win condition; staying off the final execution is the entire urgent objective.

**Change plan when:** When nominated, address the strongest specific evidence against you before arguing broad possibilities. If actual allies exist in the setup, coordinate legal voting support; normal Apocalypse usually has no teammates.

**Tradeoff:** Invincible Defense does not prevent being executed. There is no additional Reap target or ordinary night setup that fixes a losing vote today.

**Checkpoints:**

- Prevent your own execution during the transformation day.
- Answer the strongest accusation without contradicting your recorded claim or movement.
- Remain in the match and reach sundown alive so the automatic Apocalypse can resolve.

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Prevent your own execution during the transformation day.
2. Answer the strongest accusation without contradicting your recorded claim or movement.
3. Remain in the match and reach sundown alive so the automatic Apocalypse can resolve.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)).

### Route 2: Answer the strongest case directly

**Best when:** One concrete contradiction or competing account is concentrating votes on you.

**Action:** Identify the exact accusation, give the shortest consistent timeline that addresses it, and point to facts the lobby can check now. Preserve your previous claim rather than inventing a new night ability.

**Target rule:** Your target is the decisive public objection, not a player to Reap. Compare your existing movement and claim history with the accusation.

**Why:** Fixing one checkable misunderstanding can cost the opposing majority its strongest reason to execute you. Adding several unsupported stories increases the chance of a new contradiction.

**Change plan when:** If the evidence truly exposes you, assess the actual vote coalition and any other supported urgent case; do not promise another night result that Death cannot produce.

**Tradeoff:** A sound explanation may still fail against a coordinated majority. The transformation announcement makes Town especially likely to compare every claim.

**Checkpoints:**

- Recognize the current-day deadline
- Address one concrete claim conflict
- Keep the public timeline unchanged

**Follow-up priorities:**

1. Track nominations and remaining opposing votes during this day.
2. Use discussion to preserve survival rather than to plan Reap.
3. Remain in the game through the end-of-day resolution.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)#Death).

### Claim strategy

Oracle matches astral Reap’s lack of an ordinary visit most cleanly; Sheriff can pressure a genuinely framed appearance but invents a check and a visible visit. Veteran, Coroner and setup-legal Pilgrim are progressively weaker alternatives for the nonvisiting history. Reap never supplies Town investigation or protection. After becoming Death, the only immediate route is surviving the current day. There is no next Reap or ordinary nighttime ability to promise. You are Death now: a pre-transformation cover does not explain new attacks, altered movement, or the public transformation.

#### 1. Oracle (TP) — Astral footprint · no real protection

Reap is astral, so the lack of an ordinary numbered-player visit is compatible with Oracle’s role-targeted Aegis history.

- Write Aegis Sheriff or another legal Town role, not Aegis 4. The private Reap target and public role choice are different kinds of selection.
- Rotate the claimed role on consecutive nights and keep the Oracle self-role choice to its one permitted use.
- Do not call a marked player saved or identified. Reap adds a Soul opportunity and changes apparent investigation identity; it is not a Barrier.
- At the Death announcement, answer scrutiny using the existing role-choice timeline instead of inventing a final protective effect.
- After becoming Death, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You want a quieter cover that does not promise a visible visit every night.

**Exposure/limits:** You grant no Aegis. Reap targets are player numbers, while Oracle chooses Town roles; confusing those methods exposes the claim.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Oracle_(ToS2)).

#### 2. Sheriff (TI) — Framing interaction · visit mismatch

Reap can make a marked player appear Suspicious, so some other Sheriff evidence may align with a pressure story rather than contradicting it immediately.

- Use only legal Innocent/Suspicious vocabulary and post a stable cover check promptly; a Reap selection is not itself a result.
- Expect a Tracker or Lookout to challenge the missing normal visit to your alleged Search target.
- A marked player can genuinely appear Suspicious without being evil. Do not describe the framing effect as an exact discovery.
- Avoid treating every mark as a mandatory accusation: your actual gain comes from later deaths, while overfocusing exposes the connection.
- After becoming Death, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** Public investigative evidence already supports questioning a marked player, and you understand that the apparent result is not their true faction.

**Exposure/limits:** You receive no Sheriff check and do not make its normal visible visit. Repeated Suspicious reports on your marked players can reveal the framing plan.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Sheriff_(ToS2)).

#### 3. Veteran (TK) — Stays home · no real Alert

Astral Reap leaves no ordinary visit, which fits a stay-at-home Veteran account better than claiming to walk to a house every night.

- Separate the private Reap target from the public home-based ability: Veteran does not Alert at another player’s house.
- Keep an accurate claimed three-charge Alert count and do not write Alert automatically on every night.
- Do not invent kills or visitor identities from Souls gained; a marked player can die elsewhere with no connection to your house.
- A quiet stay-home account is possible but weak. If real direct visitors report surviving an Alert, the movement fit cannot repair the missing attack.
- After becoming Death, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You want a nonvisiting TK cover and have no public event requiring a real Alert demonstration.

**Exposure/limits:** You cannot produce Alert attacks or a valid visitor-kill event. An unharmed confirmed direct visitor during a claimed Alert can expose the missing effect.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Veteran_(ToS2)).

#### 4. Coroner (TI) — Quiet N1 · later visit problem

The ordinary Coroner has no usable N1 autopsy, giving a plausible inactive opening without pretending Reap is a visible visit.

- Start with no usable N1 Autopsy; never name a killer comparison before an eligible body exists.
- Record a real eligible daytime corpse before alleging the corresponding night Examine.
- Do not turn the death of a marked player into a killer match. Soul gain tells you about your objective, not Coroner evidence.
- Expect later movement checks to expose the missing Examine visit; the inactive first night is a limited overlap, not a full-role imitation.
- After becoming Death, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** You need a plausible N1 and are willing to manage a much weaker later TI history.

**Exposure/limits:** Later Coroner Examine normally requires movement and actual corpse records, neither of which Reap supplies.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Coroner_(ToS2)).

#### 5. Pilgrim (Town Outlier) — Setup-restricted · no ability proof

A setup-legal Pilgrim expects no active night visit or result, fitting astral Reap’s observed inactivity without promising an effect you cannot demonstrate.

- Check roledeck eligibility before selecting this claim; common Town buckets do not automatically permit every Outlier.
- Use dated public observations and vote reasons rather than pretending Soul counts are Town information.
- Do not add a night target or special result to make the will look busier; Pilgrim has no such ability.
- When Death appears, your public reasoning needs to stand on its own because the cover cannot independently confirm you.
- After becoming Death, reassess this earlier cover against your new ability and any public transformation; do not claim the old movement pattern automatically continues.

**Best when:** The roledeck explicitly allows Town Outliers and you prefer an observation-based account to fabricated powers.

**Exposure/limits:** Pilgrim must be possible in the deck. A bare no-ability claim supplies little trust and can become a convenient execution target.

Mechanics: [source 1](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Soul_Collector_(ToS2)), [source 2](https://townofsalem.wiki.gg/wiki/Pilgrim_(ToS2)).
